**The codes do not agree across the four members, and the split is not where the names put it.** `errorCondition` draws a pair, `commonPrecondition` and an alternative's `precondition` draw `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, and `operation` draws `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT` — so what separates them is the member's grammar ROLE, condition against expression, and not its carrier. Two of the three answers were already in the repository, each measured for a different question and never compared; the `commonPrecondition` row that completes the set had never been measured and was predicted wrong.

**`errorCondition` is excluded from the new rule, and that is a report-shape decision stated as one.** Its owning family already reproduces A12's pair, so the blank rule would add a third finding duplicating the second code. The defect is surfaced either way, which is what makes this a choice about noise rather than about coverage — and it is locked, so a later reading of "be faithful to A12" cannot quietly make the report louder.

### The illegal-CHARACTER class — where a recorded negative was a positive control (2026-09-13)

**The gap said the class was unmeasured, and cited the very probe that supports it.** Its note read that A12's message names an illegal character but "which characters those are is unmeasured — `@@@` lexes cleanly and fails a phase later". That reads as evidence the class is elusive. It is the opposite: `@` is the `AT` terminal, so `@@@` lexing is exactly what the grammar predicts, and the probe had agreed with a derivation nobody had performed. A recorded negative result kept a measurable class closed for as long as nobody asked what it was a negative result *about*.

**Deriving the set is what makes reporting it safe.** `Base_ConditionLexer.g4` declares no catch-all rule, so every character outside its terminals is a token-recognition failure and the admitted set falls out of the terminal list rather than out of a survey of characters someone tried. That matters more here than in most places: this check REFUSES text, so a set assembled by eye and short by one member turns a legal condition into a false rejection ([KF309](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf309)).

**The control is what separates the two failure phases, and A12 supplies it.** A character the lexer cannot take answers `MVK_LEXER_STANDARD_ERROR`; one it can take, standing where the grammar cannot use it, answers `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`. So `$`, `@`, `,` and `ß` in the same slot as `%` and `&` are not decoration — without them the grid would be measuring the slot, and every row would agree for the wrong reason.

**Two characters defeat the intuition the code would otherwise encode.** `!` exists only inside `'!='` and `#` only inside `'#Zeile'`, so a lone one of either is illegal though both are punctuation the lexer plainly knows. `!` is reported, because the two-character terminal is tried first and anything reaching the fallback is genuinely alone. `#` is NOT, and that asymmetry is the slice's one deliberate under-report: `#Zeile`'s other language spellings are unknown here, so refusing an unpaired `#` could reject text A12 accepts.

**The literal rows are the over-rejection guard, not extra coverage.** `StringLiteral` admits every character but a quote, so all of these are ordinary text inside one. A checker scanning raw text rather than tokens would refuse conditions the kernel takes — the direction nothing downstream can catch, and the reason those rows sit beside the grid rather than after it.

### Field variant filters — the carrier whose law is the OPPOSITE of its siblings' (2026-09-13)

**`variantFilter` has three carriers and the checker had measured two, so the third was left silent — correctly, as it turned out, but for the wrong reason.** The gap note said what A12 does with the member on a field "is unmeasured, so a rule measured on the rules-and-computations carrier is not transferred to it". That caution was right and the measurement reverses the sign of the law: on a rule or computation, `ONLY` SUPPRESSES — A12 says nothing about that declaration, so silence is faithful. On a FIELD, `ONLY` DELETES: `ValidationModelConverter` keeps a field only while `VariantFiltering.fieldIsAcceptedByFilter` accepts it, so the field is gone from the validation model and everything naming it is naming nothing. A12 therefore says MORE about such a model, not less, and the checker was under-reporting ([KF305](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf305)).

**The first discriminator was confounded and the confound is worth naming.** The probe rule both TARGETED the filtered field and mentioned it, which answers with the error-target pair rather than the entity code — two roles moving together. Separating them (target `AutoRenew`, mention `Tier`) gives `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` against an `ALL_EXCEPT` control on the identical model. Both rows are kept, because together they show the code follows the naming ROLE rather than the filter.

**The fix is one derived list, not a family.** `PreparedModel.resolvableElements` is `elements` minus the fields A12 deletes, and the four resolution sites read it; the existing role→code mapping then produces A12's own codes on both paths with nothing new to state. A family reporting this itself would have had to re-derive that mapping, which is the second live statement the estate keeps refusing to mint.

**What it deliberately still missed, CLOSED the same day.** Keying strictly on the spelling `ONLY` meant an unknown or unbindable `filterType` on a field left that field visible, so a model A12 cannot build at all drew no finding — under-reporting, safe, and recorded as a gap rather than as a claim the member was unmeasured. That gap named shapes the field grid had already measured, which is the second time in this session a gap's own text turned out to be a to-do; it is now closed and deleted.

**The fix is a SCOPE split, and the split is the finding.** The family asks two questions with different denominators. *Can A12 bind this filter?* is asked of every carrier that declares one — rule, computation and field, enumerated from the three serializers that write the member — and every shape answers alike on the field carrier and the rule carrier. *Does an `ONLY` filter suppress this declaration?* is asked of rules and computations alone, because on a field that same spelling deletes the field, which resolution already handles; including fields there would silence findings A12 does make. One scope function each, named for its question.

**The binding rows were measured on the field carrier rather than transferred, and the reason is this member specifically.** Its semantic law is already known to differ by carrier, so "the reader binds it the same way everywhere" is exactly the kind of reuse that needs no new branch and therefore prompts no question. Five rows, and they agree with the rule carrier — which is the answer the code wanted, arrived at by measuring rather than by assuming ([KF305](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf305)).

**The real-model corpus is silent on this, and that is worth recording rather than reading as support.** No `variantFilter` appears in any of the 59 `a12-kernel-mm` models — `grep -rho '"filterType"…'` returns nothing — so the corpus cannot regress on this change and equally offers it no evidence. There is no checker-versus-Kernel full-report differential over real models yet; that is order 7's F2 lane, and until it exists a change to resolution rests on its own grid and the checker's suite.

### C2 record — the corrective delivery: a projection whose DENOMINATOR came from the consumer (2026-09-13)

**The open question was the applicable subset, and the consumer answered it.** A2's record showed that an `RkCode` filter over `Severity` + `DiagnosticSource` over- and under-matches in both directions, and concluded a per-constant disposition was needed. It is not: the projection carries exactly the codes the CHECKER's own sources spell, read out of them, so the applicable subset is derived from the thing that consumes it rather than judged. Seven entries today — six curated structural correctives and one `RK_*` — and a code the checker gains whose owner has guidance fails the guard until the projection is regenerated.

**Amended 2026-09-13 — "spelled" is a superset of "emitted", and the generated header claimed otherwise.** A textual scan cannot tell a code the checker REPORTS from one it holds to compare an authored value against, and no rule separates them that is not also false of `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, a module constant the checker does report. So the Java guard now claims only the bound, and reachability is measured where models exist: `consistency.CorrectiveTest` runs one model that draws every reachable entry and asserts the residue is exactly the comparison-only code. Two smaller defects rode along — the scan walked the whole of `commonMain` including the file it generates, so the denominator contained its own keys and could never shrink, and the Kotlin test named for this asserted only that each key starts with `MVK_`/`RK_`, which passes for every possible over-match.

**A projection rather than direct reuse, for the reason A2 named**: a Node consumer cannot read Java source. The mechanism is the estate's existing `dump…`/guard pair (`./build.sh :dumpCheckerCorrectives`), not a fourth one, and the generated Kotlin SOURCE avoids the per-target resource read a multiplatform module would otherwise pay.

**The one thing no JVM-side guard could see is whether the guidance SURVIVES packaging**, so the assertion that settles it lives in the packed Node consumer rather than beside the generator. Reading the generator's output proves nothing about what ships — the same reachability rule that governs any capability claim about a running surface.

**`StructuralKernelFix` gained `codes()`, which is the gap this exposed in the owner.** It served a lookup and nothing else, and a lookup-only surface cannot be projected: a generator needs the denominator, not a way to ask about one code at a time. Fixed in the owner rather than worked around by scraping its source.

**The catalog half is deliberately not delivered, with its re-open trigger.** A2 ruled that `operators.json` ships as-is because it is already data; that remains right, and it is not yet USEFUL — no finding this checker produces names an operator id, so 293 KB would be packaged with nothing to join it against. It lands when a finding carries an operator.

### C record — the unprefixed estate DISPOSITIONED to the last code (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: no code drained; the remaining 22 are classified, each with a cited reason.** Derived, not remembered: every constant of `FehlertextErrorType` matched against the checker's reported literals, and every unreported one's THROW SITE read in the 30.8.1 checkout. 28 of 50 reported.

| why it is not reported | codes | the citation |
|---|---|---|
| behind `isHModel()` — no A12 document model reaches it | `INVALID_PATH_SPEC`, `INVALID_PATH_SPEC_IN_PARAMETER` | the guard is the first statement of its site |
| refused by an EARLIER gate on the A12 route | `NO_Z_ITERATION`, `NO_U_ITERATION` | `checkZeile`/`checkUSB` answer `PARAM_INVALID_IN_NEW_WORLD` first, and the checker reports that |
| composed as a BARE message — no `[CLASS]` key for a consumer to read | `ERROR_TEXT_PARAMETER`, `RULE_ERRORTEXT_WRONG`, `FIELD_INVALID_PREFIX` | `getBareErrorMessage` at each site |
| needs a list of ≥2 same-short-named index fields, the shape the terminal family declines | `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_REP`, `_ENUM_XML_VALUES`, `_ENUM_XML_TO_CAT`, `_ENUM_XML_TO_DISPLAY`, `_INCONSISTENT_TYPES` | each compares list members against the first |
| structurally unreachable from any A12 model | `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_COMPOSED_INDEX` | [KF280](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf280) attacked every link |
| needs PARALLEL-ITERATION detection or the kernel's own condition visitor | `INVALID_USE_OF_ITERATION_PARAM`, `INVALID_USE_OF_SEMANTIC_INDEX`, `INVALID_HASH_LFDNR_FOR_PARALLEL_ITERATION` | `checkSemanticIndexAndParallelIteration`, `checkFehlerEnum` |
| reads the H-model's form/row dimensions (`getMaxValues()[0]`/`[1]`) | `INVALID_FIELD_L`, `INVALID_FIELD_Z` | `checkLfdNrSpecifier`/`checkZeileSpecifier` |
| **measured absent** on six authored shapes | `INVALID_CONDITION` | `MessageOnAnUnparsableConditionLawsTest`, [KF343](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf343) |
| adjacent shape measured and draws another class; no shape known to reach it | `WRONG_ENCODING` | [KF279](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf279)'s supplementary-plane row carries NO class |
| the form-line suffixes' H-model half | `NO_VORDRUCKZEILE`, `NO_VORDRUCKNAME_IN_H` | no throw site in `kernel-core-service`; the name states the model kind |

**The last candidate was measured to be ABSENT, and that absence is worth more than the code would have been.** `INVALID_CONDITION` looked reachable from a source read — `RuleParser` re-parses the condition whenever a message text contains a `$` and composes the class when that parse yields nothing. Six unparsable conditions later, the verdict is the condition's parse class and nothing about the message. That is the fact every message family in this checker depends on without having had it: they all skip a declaration whose DSL they could not read, and now something says that skip loses nothing.

**Reading the throw site is what made a 22-code remainder cheap to classify.** Nine of the 22 are excluded by a guard on the FIRST line of their site — an `isHModel()` test, an earlier `PARAM_INVALID_IN_NEW_WORLD`, a `getBareErrorMessage` with no class key. None of those is visible from the code's name, its message text, or the ledger; all are visible in one grep per code. A remainder nobody has read reads as work, and most of this one was never work at all.

### C record — a whole message POSITION the checker had never read (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the FORM-LINE suffixes' one gate.** `INVALID_FIELD_IN_PARAMETER` newly reported, extending `MESSAGE_PARAMETER_REFERENCE` ([KF279](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf279) extended in place). No new family.

**A measured EXEMPTION had left the position unread entirely.** [KF279](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf279) established that `.Vordruckzeile` and `.Vordruckname` skip the membership gate, and the checker's reader duly yields nothing for them — correct, and it stopped there, so no family looked at the position at all. Skipping one gate is not skipping every gate: `checkValidFieldName` still refuses a GROUP, and that row had never been put to the kernel because the finding that opened the exemption had no reason to.

**The throw-site read is what sorted the remaining codes, and most of them are out of reach for a stated reason.** `INVALID_PATH_SPEC` and `INVALID_PATH_SPEC_IN_PARAMETER` both sit behind `isHModel()`, so no A12 document model reaches them. `ERROR_TEXT_PARAMETER`, `RULE_ERRORTEXT_WRONG` and `FIELD_INVALID_PREFIX` are composed as BARE messages — no `[CLASS]` key — so they are not classes a consumer can key on at all. Reading where the kernel throws each code is what separates those from the reachable ones, and it costs one grep per code.

### C record — the gate behind a reproduced one, found by reading the kernel's throw sites (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the `$#…$` group position's WILDCARD gate.** One class newly reported — `ITER_PARAM_WITH_STARREFERENCE`, extending `MESSAGE_GROUP_PARAMETER` ([KF214](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf214) amended in place). No new family.

**The worklist ran out of measured codes, so the next derivation was over the kernel's THROW SITES.** Every remaining unprefixed code had no laws expectation anywhere, which means each needs a measurement before any checker work — and the cheap way to choose among them is to read where the kernel throws each one and ask which sites sit behind a gate this checker already reproduces. Three of the remaining codes turned out to live in `checkLevelParam`, immediately behind the `INVALID_GROUP` gate `MESSAGE_GROUP_PARAMETER` owns.

**Two of the three are behind MACHINERY and were dispositioned instead.** `checkSemanticIndexAndParallelIteration` returns true for every shape except a parallel iteration or a keyed parameter, and then hands the decision to a visitor over the parsed condition. That is not another reading of the model, so the two classes are recorded in the family's KDoc with their precondition rather than left as worklist entries.

**Two wrong predictions before the right shape, and the kernel's own comment held the answer.** The first grid aimed the gate at a group named while a DESCENDANT's field was read starred, and the second at an ancestor — both admitted. The kernel's German comment beside the throw says *"bei #Kontext: wird der Kontext in der Bedingung ohne Stern referenziert?"*, which names the shorthand and the exact question; the shape it describes refused first time. Reading the comment in the file I had already opened would have skipped two rounds.

**Then the gate turned out to be wider than the comment, which is why the absolute spelling was measured too.** The same group spelled out draws the same class, so the law is the position's rather than the shorthand's — and a checker keyed on the word `RuleGroup` would have under-reported every other spelling while looking complete.

**The reducer's one dangerous branch got the only mirror row that could catch it.** A star BELOW the named group leaves that group ridden per row, so a reducer testing "does any reference carry a star" would over-report on a legal model. The star's own level is found by resolving the reference's prefix up to the starred segment — the resolver handles relative spellings and `..` steps that arithmetic over segment counts would get wrong — and an unresolvable prefix counts as a ride, which under-reports.

### F2 record — the class comparison could not see the error MESSAGE (2026-09-15) <a id="f2-message-position"></a>

**Frozen change: three message-aimed mutation kinds in `CheckerClassAgreementTest`.** The population grows from 32 compared mutants to 98, and none is refused without a keyed class. No family changed.

**The gap was a POSITION, not a gate.** Every existing kind perturbs a declaration or a condition, so the message text was untouched in all 43 committed models — which is exactly where the wrong class this estate held longest lived (`$RuleGroup$` named as a reference failure until this same day). An instrument aimed at the gates a defect was once found in cannot find the next one in a position it never writes to.

**Appending is what keeps a message mutation one-variable.** The condition kinds rewrite a member; these three append to an existing text, so every declared element stays exactly as authored and a class A12 draws on the mutant is the parameter's. That also made them free of `masked` rows: the sweep went green first time across all 43 models.

**The population argument matters more than the three kinds.** Four of 43 committed models write a message PARAMETER at all, so a kind that perturbs an existing parameter reaches four models; a kind that appends one reaches every model with a rule. The estate's own corpus decides what an instrument can see, and picking mutations by what the corpus already contains is how a sweep stays small without anyone choosing to keep it small.

### F2 record — the class comparison could not say WHERE, and the assumption about how A12 says it was wrong (2026-09-15) <a id="class-agreement-by-declaration"></a>

**Frozen change: `CheckerClassAgreementTest` keys both sides by DECLARATION.** No family changed and no code drained. The comparison was `ours - kernelClasses == {}` over the whole mutant; it is now that plus three positional assertions, each with a killed control planted in `MessageParameterSyntax.kt` — a finding moved to its parent group (`element.path.substringBeforeLast("/")`), a finding emitted twice for one message, and a finding with no `at` at all.

**The measurement refuted the plan for it, which is the part worth recording.** The slice was derived from A12's notification prose as `<Kind> with id '<id>': … [KEY]`, so it was scoped to build an id→path map out of `prepare()`'s `DeclaredElement.id` — required rather than optional, because the reference corpora carry opaque ids. A12 keys by **PATH** (`Rule with path '/Cart/BeyondFirst': … [MVK_NO_WILDCARD]`, all 98 of the population, measured before a line was written), so the map is not needed at all: A12's spelling and the checker's own `at` compare directly. The id-map half of the slice was deleted rather than built. This is the cheap direction of the [claim-oracle rule](../CLAUDE.md#kernel-claim-oracles) — a format claim read off prose, settled by printing the string.

**What the positional key buys, stated against what the class key already had.** A12 answers each of these mutants with exactly ONE notification (98 of 98), so `ours ⊆ kernel` was already forcing a one-element set — the count invariant the [first F2 record](#f2-message-position) asked for could not be expressed at all while the key was the model. Keyed by declaration it falls out: two classes at one declaration, or one class twice, is a disagreement. And the defect the class key structurally cannot see becomes visible — A12's OWN class named about a declaration A12 named it about nowhere, which is right in class, wrong in the element it hands the author, and was the exact shape of the defect this test was built from.

**Only declarations A12 keyed are compared, and the restriction is the honest half.** A12 stops at its first answering gate, so its silence about the rest of the mutant is not evidence; comparing there would refuse honest content the moment that stop point moved. The cost is a finding whose class A12 named about a declaration A12 never reached, which is why the classes A12 named are compared as (declaration, class) PAIRS — a class A12 named is wrong wherever A12 did not name it, and a genuine second instance is a row in `positionsA12Masks` carrying its measurement, empty today because all 82 positioned findings landed exactly where A12 keyed.

**The parser is built to fail LOUD, because the alternative is invisible.** A keyed notification whose declaration prefix cannot be read is collected and asserted empty, so a changed notification shape fails with the row that could not be parsed instead of silently emptying the keying and leaving a green class-only comparison behind. The same asymmetry drives the new floor: 82 of the 98 pairs have both sides speaking, and the checker is SILENT on the other 16 — under-reporting, the safe direction, disclosed rather than asserted, but not unguarded, since a compared pair needs both sides and the floor is what fails when the checker goes quiet.

### C record — the measure-first guard could not see half the estate (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: `CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest` now covers the UNPREFIXED error-text vocabulary.** No code drained and no family changed. The guard's claim — every A12 code the checker reports was measured against the kernel — held for the `MVK_` half and was silent about 26 unprefixed codes, because a prefix is recognisable from shape and `INVALID_FIELD` is an ordinary word. The vocabulary is now read off the kernel's own enums (`KernelErrorVocabulary`, the four `MvkLedgerTest` already reaches), so the denominator stays derived.

**The gap opened the way a guard's blind spot usually does: the surface it watched moved.** The guard was written when the checker reported `MVK_*` and little else; three weeks of slices later the unprefixed estate is where the work is, and nothing about the guard looked wrong — it passed, its floor passed, its javadoc described exactly what it did. A guard that names its recognition RULE rather than its subject cannot report that the subject grew past it.

**The floor went on the REPORTED side, which is the one that can go quiet.** The existing floor asserts the laws scan reaches its estate; the new half's likelier failure is an empty vocabulary or a membership test that matches nothing, and both pass silently against an empty reported set. Asserting the reported count is what makes the green mean something.

### C record — a WRONG-CLASS defect the language axis had been hiding, and two codes with it (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the message name position's own TERMINALS, keyed by the model's condition language.** Two classes newly reported — `INVALID_PARAMETER` for the rule-group shorthand (extending `MESSAGE_PARAMETER_REFERENCE`) and `NO_BASE_YEAR` for the base-year terminal (extending `BASE_YEAR_REQUIREMENT`, [KF403](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf403) amended in place). No new family; the count stays where it was.

**The slice found a live WRONG CLASS, which is the one direction this estate forbids.** `$RuleGroup$` in a rule-owned parameter is recognised by A12 and refused with `INVALID_PARAMETER`; the checker resolved it as an ordinary name, found nothing, and said `INVALID_ENTITY`. The fact had been measured and tabled in [KF198](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf198) since before the family existed. What hid it is that the reader's exclusion list held only the BASE-YEAR spellings, so nothing in the family's own source mentioned the other terminal at all — a silence with no marker, which no audit of disclosed under-reports would have surfaced.

**A deliberate, documented under-report turned out to cost a finding in the opposite direction.** The terminal set excluded BOTH language spellings on the stated ground that an exclusion can only under-report, and only the English one was measured. It is true of the terminal's own class and false of the other language's spelling, which A12 looks up as an entity and refuses — so excluding it dropped an `INVALID_ENTITY` on every model that writes it. The lesson is narrower than "don't exclude": an exclusion is safe for the code it excludes and says nothing about what the excluded text would otherwise have drawn.

**The language axis is a CARRIER, so both arms are locked on both sides.** The German base-year class was measured rather than transferred — a `de_DE`-condition model declaring no base year, one row — and the mirror grew a German fixture so the checker's two arms are both exercised. The fixture's first draft was green-looking nonsense: `FeldGefuellt` is not A12's German spelling of `FieldFilled`, so every row including the control returned `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`. The control is what caught it, which is the whole reason a language grid carries one.

**And the index terminal's four CROSS-FIELD classes were dispositioned rather than left open.** A source read settles them: each compares the members of A12's same-short-name list against the first, so all four need a list of at least two — the shape the previous slice's guard declines. Recorded in the family's KDoc where the next session will meet it, not in a ledger row, because the reason is a property of the code it sits beside.

### C record — three more codes on a form already owned, and the gate order that made them safe (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the `$index(…)$` terminal's last three gates.** Three classes newly reported — `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_REP_ITER`, `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_NONREP_ENUM` and `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_PARAMETER` ([KF280](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf280), amended in place). **No new family**: `ERROR_TEXT_INDEX_TERMINAL` already owned the form and now walks all five of its gates, which is why the count does not move.

**The biggest remaining yield was behind a family already written, not in an unread form.** The derived worklist lists codes; grouping them by FORM put seven `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_*` constants on one terminal whose first two gates were already reproduced. Extending a family is cheaper than adding one — no enum constant, no call site, no `checksCompleted` entry, no wiring declaration — and it is where the estate's under-reports concentrate, because a family stops at the first gate whose ORDER nobody measured.

**The family's own KDoc had recorded exactly why it stopped, and that record was the worklist entry.** It said the three later classes were a disclosed under-report because reporting them would mean reproducing the repeatability machinery. That is a standing invitation to measure, and honouring it took one laws grid. A disclosed under-report that names its own precondition is worth more than a TODO, and this estate now has several.

**A source read predicted two rows and the measurement refuted both.** `checkIndexFieldRefRepeatability` iterates the CONDITION's index-field references filtered by short name, so a declaration the condition never names looked admitted and a mixed shape looked like `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_REP`. Measured: the non-repeatable arm refuses with no condition reference at all, and the mixed shape answers with the error entity's OWN class when that one also offends. The rows were written as predictions and run before anything was implemented, which is the only reason the checker was never built on the wrong law.

**The under-report is now a NAMED shape rather than a named class.** `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_REP` stays unreported, and the guard is the question that makes it safe: a short name that more than one group declares as its index field is left alone entirely, because A12 then walks a list this family does not reproduce. Guarding on the SHAPE rather than on the class is what keeps both reproduced arms from naming a wrong class there — the failure mode that is worse than silence.

**A mirror that filters by code hid the class it was supposed to watch.** `ErrorTextIndexTerminalTest` filtered its rows to `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD`, so the row for the suffix-omitted parameter read `emptyList()` and its comment claimed deliberate silence — and it would have kept passing, unchanged, after the class started being reported. Filtering by the declaration's PATH instead is the fix, and it is the general one: a mirror that filters by code cannot see the family grow.

### C record — two codes on one FORM, and the order between them nobody had measured (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the error message's `.value` SUFFIX.** Two classes newly reported — `PARAM_VALUE_WITH_STARREFERENCE` and `INVALID_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION` ([KF279](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf279), amended in place). New family `MESSAGE_VALUE_SUFFIX`; re-derive the count rather than increment it — it reads 96 today.

**The slice was chosen because one FORM carried two of the remaining codes.** The derived worklist's middle column — measured-but-unreported unprefixed `FehlertextErrorType` constants — lists codes, and codes are not the unit of work: these two sit behind the same three characters of message grammar and share a membership pre-emption, so reading the form once bought both. The previous slice made the same move on a parameter POSITION. A form or a position is the unit; a code is the accounting.

**Two gates on one form need their ORDER measured, and the finding that owned them said they were independent.** [KF279](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf279) had each gate measured against its own within-model twin and closed with "(Independent of the suffix's known non-starred-reference gate.)" — true of the two-cell diagonal it had, and not a claim about the cell where both hold. A checker must answer that cell, so the reducer would have had to pick, and picking the unmeasured one names the wrong class on a model A12 does refuse. One row settled it: the reference gate wins. The correction went into the existing paragraph, not a new entry.

**The subtraction the star gate rests on is not exhibitable, and the mirror row says so.** The gate asks whether every read of the field carries a star, so a condition reading it both ways must clear it — and A12 refuses that condition first, with `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, because an unstarred read of a repeatable level at the root is unbound. The row is kept with that class as its expectation: it still kills the mutant that drops the subtraction (this family falls silent on it either way only if the subtraction is there), while the comment states plainly that the suffix verdict on that shape is unmeasured and the silence is the under-report direction.

**Two shared readers already existed and one was nearly restated.** `waivesValueValidation()` in `Reference.kt` answers the declaration gate for two other families at different points of their own orders; the draft had a private copy reading `stringTypeBody()` directly. The other, `plainParameterName`, was private in `MessageParameters.kt` and already strips `.value`, so exposing it as internal was the whole reader — a suffixed parameter resolves through exactly the path the plain form does, which is also why membership pre-empts.

### C record — a whole unread parameter POSITION, four codes at once (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the error message's `$#…$` GROUP position.** Four classes newly reported — `INVALID_GROUP`, `INVALID_ROOT_GROUP` on this carrier, `PARAM_INVALID_IN_NEW_WORLD` and `RULEGROUP_IMMEDIATELY_BELOW_ROOT` ([KF-§13](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md), amended in place). New family `MESSAGE_GROUP_PARAMETER`; the count goes 94 → 95.

**The derived worklist's middle column paid again, and bigger.** The previous slice took one code from it; this one took four, because they all belong to a single POSITION nobody had read — the tier was split by parameter FORM and this form was simply absent from the split. A position is a better unit than a code here: its gates are ordered against each other and against nothing else, so reading it once settles all four.

**A source read produced a confident wrong mechanism and one row cost 27 seconds to refute it.** `CheckIdNameImpl.checkAndCreateFullName`'s absolute arm answers `INVALID_ENTITY` for a candidate that does not resolve, so an address under a declared root naming nothing looked like a third class sitting between the root gate and containment. Measured, it is `INVALID_GROUP` — `resolveAndStoreEntity`'s group branch answers in this position. The law is SIMPLER than its source chain, and the practical consequence is the whole reduction: containment alone decides, so the family never asks whether the named group exists.

**The near-miss was a claim the estate had already measured and I restated from the GRAMMAR.** The draft admitted both language spellings of each shorthand, reasoning that a wider exclusion can only under-report — safe, and the wrong law. `RuleMessageParameterLanguageLawsTest` had measured both directions on one model pair: each language admits ONLY its own spelling, and the German far shorthand is `LfdNr`, not the word the draft guessed. The lexer file spells the German terminal while a running `en_US` model takes the English one, so the grammar read gets the pair backwards — the same one-layer trap [KF412](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf412) records, hit again nine slices later. **Before writing a language-keyed set, grep the laws estate for the code: the measurement was already there.**

### C record — the worklist derived instead of guessed (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the dollar count an error message has to balance.** `WRONG_NUMBER_OF_DOLLARS` ([KF343](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf343), amended in place). New family `MESSAGE_PARAMETER_SYNTAX`; the count goes 93 → 94.

**The slice matters less than how it was CHOSEN.** Picking families by reading around had started returning items already dispositioned — two in a row, both correctly closed with their reasons, and reading each cost more than the slice would have. So the worklist was derived twice instead: every `MVK_*` ledger row whose disposition column lacks `checker-reported` (113 of 306, of which 77 are `unreachable` or `erased` and nearly all the rest carry a recorded decision), and then every constant of A12's UNPREFIXED `FehlertextErrorType` diffed against both the checker's main source and the laws estate. The second derivation is the one that paid: **49 constants, 14 reported, 11 MEASURED IN LAWS AND NOT REPORTED, 24 neither.** That middle column is the cheapest class of work in the estate and nothing named it before.

**The prefixed estate is nearly drained and the unprefixed one is not — which the family COUNT hides.** Both surfaces are counted by the same `CheckFamily` constants, so progress on one reads like progress on the other. Derive the two denominators separately, and re-derive rather than increment: the first attempt at this diff scanned only the checker's source and put the middle column at 35, because it counted "not reported" without asking which of those had already been measured.

**The gate below this one had to be measured on its OTHER arm.** `INVALID_CHARACTERS` was known to pre-empt the dollar count; `INVALID_LINEBREAK` is a separate class from the same gate and had not been. One row settled it, and it matters because the checker's shared predicate answers for both arms — relying on the tab measurement alone would have been a claim about a gate half nobody had run.

### C record — a law the backlog had mis-sized, read off a source page opened for something else (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: where the `$` outer marker may stand.** `MVK_INVALID_OUTER_ITERATION` ([KF-§9](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md), amended in place). New family `OUTER_MARKER_POSITION`; the count goes 92 → 93. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 128 reported** — this code and `MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION` from the record below.

**The backlog had this one down as "the `$` correlation, large", and it is one branch.** `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkOuter` is three lines and the law names no carrier, so one family reads whole rule conditions instead of every operator's slots. The size estimate came from the MVK ledger row, which records the ADAPTER's `$` correlation threading — genuinely large, and a different piece of work that happens to share the code. **The transferable rule: a ledger row states a DISPOSITION, and where the disposition is about another component the row says nothing about the cost of reporting the class.** Nothing prompts that re-read, which is why it sat on the backlog through several slices that could have taken it.

**It was found by reading a kernel page opened for a different question.** `checkOuter` sits directly above `checkFilterCondition`, which the record below was about, and its three lines answered a backlog item on sight. Worth doing deliberately: when a source page is open for one gate, read its neighbours — they are the gates most likely to be mis-sized in a ledger, because nobody has had reason to open them.

**Two measured rows carry the family and neither is the law.** The law itself was already recorded and already measured on three carriers. What was missing is what a checker needs before reporting it: `checkOuter` runs inside `checkCommon`, AFTER the reference is built, so a marked path naming nothing draws `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` and never reaches the gate — report on text position alone and the class lands on every mistyped marked path. And the marker INSIDE a filter, the one spelling the existing table explicitly did not cover, is legal but not sufficient: a marked reference is skipped by the filter's index walk, so a body whose only reference is marked still draws `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD`. That second row was predicted ADMITTED and measured otherwise — by a finding this session had extended an hour earlier and did not apply.

### C record — the free gate at the end of a chain already reproduced (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the `Having` filter's LAST gate.** `MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION` for a `RepetitionNotUnique` or a `CustomCondition` in a filter body ([KF374](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf374)), plus the shape-parser fix that made the second of those reachable. No new family — it joins `FILTER_BODY_GATES`. No new family — it joins `FILTER_BODY_GATES`, renamed from `FILTER_BODY_ITERATION` because a family reporting a non-iteration class under that name misleads the next reader, and the estate already names a multi-gate chain `…Gates` in seven places. The count stays at 92.

**The gate is LAST and every gate before it is already reported**, which is the inverse of the case that usually licenses a slice and just as good: the argument gate of a chain is safe because nothing pre-empts it, and the final gate of a FULLY reproduced chain is safe because this family already decides whether everything before it passed. Putting it in the same reducer is what makes that true — a separate family would have had to reproduce four gates to know the fifth was reached. **The generalisation worth carrying: a chain is worth re-reading whenever its LAST unreported gate sits behind gates the estate has since closed** — the position that was expensive when the family was written may be free now, and nothing re-opens the question on its own.

**The denominator was wrong in the ledger and the source settled it in one read.** The row described the kit's three pre-checked constructs as "the representable forbidden constructs; rest kernel-delegated", implying A12 forbids more. Its listener carries exactly three overrides. A checker built on the ledger's wording would have gone looking for constructs that do not exist.

**The new gate exposed a parser defect one layer down, and the first attempt at this record had shipped it as a boundary.** The construct gate reported nothing for `CustomCondition`, and the reason looked like scope: the syntax family answered `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` first. It was a defect. `ConditionShape` spelled the keyword-plus-bare-name predicate in `conditionPart`, where a condition is expected POSITIONALLY, but a clause operand reaches the parser through `expressionOrCondition`, which fell through to the expression reader — so `Having (… And CustomCondition Foo)` was a token error on a text A12 parses. The fix threads the permission explicitly and **stops at the clause**: in a plain aggregate argument the same text IS an error, and widening it there would have traded a wrong class for silence. One helper now carries the form for both readers.

**The tell was that the silence had the wrong SHAPE for a boundary.** A family's own boundary is silence; this was a different family reporting a class of its own. Both estates refuse every model concerned, so no guard could have caught it and nothing was red — it surfaced only because the three rows were written out and compared against A12's three, rather than one representative row being taken as the family's answer. **With the parser fixed the three rows land on A12's own three classes** — the construct class, `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` — the last of which was predicted as silence and measured as agreement.

**The conjunct was measured rather than transferred.** Both existing kernel rows carried the construct with no separate in-scope conjunct, and the rule the family needs is about a body that has one. Since the conjunct changes what the gate BEFORE this one sees, the transfer was not free; one row in `FilteredOperandGateOrderLawsTest` settled it for 29 s.

### C record — two gate-order measurements cashed in the same lane (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: one more gate in each of two families already landed.** `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX` for a message parameter whose semantic-index key is itself keyed ([KF412](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf412)), and `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_AMBIGUOUS` for an `$index(…)$` argument matching the index field of more than one group on the error entity's path ([KF414](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf414)). No new family, so the count stays at 92; two more classes of A12's unprefixed error-text vocabulary are now reported.

**Both were licensed by an ordering measured for a DIFFERENT purpose, which is what made them cheap.** The two slices before this one each measured a gate order to justify what they would NOT report; the same two orderings then said exactly which further class each family could report for free. `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_AMBIGUOUS` is the second gate and the argument gate is the only one before it — already reproduced — so it needs no machinery the family did not already have. `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX` is thrown by `checkSemanticIndex` as the KEY sub-rule completes, before `checkFieldRef` consults the condition at all, so it needs *less*: no membership work whatsoever. **The transferable move is to ask, of every ordering measured to bound a family, which gate it also unlocks** — a measurement taken to justify silence is the same measurement that licenses the next class, and reading it only in the direction it was taken leaves that free.

**The membership row is the discriminator, and this is the one place a nested key could have been reported wrongly.** A chained key on a group the condition never reads draws `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX`, not `MVK_INDEX_FOR_ERROR_TEXT_INVALID` — a membership-first reading predicts the wrong class there, and the checker would have named it on every unread field. Predicting both rows and measuring them together is what settled it.

**The condition-side reduction deliberately kept refusing a nested use.** Only the MESSAGE side was measured, and the same helper reduces both surfaces — so widening it for the message would have silently changed what counts as a pairing the condition wrote, on a carrier with no measurement behind it. The nested class is answered before that helper is reached instead, which is the scope-transfer rule applied to a shared reader rather than to a fixture.

### C record — a family built from a grid that already existed (2026-09-15)

*(Superseded in part 2026-09-15 by the record above: this family now reproduces the first TWO gates, not one. Its argument for the three it still leaves alone is unchanged.)*

**Frozen change: the `$index(…)$` terminal's ARGUMENT gate.** [KF414](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf414); one class of A12's unprefixed error-text vocabulary newly reported — `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD`. New family `ERROR_TEXT_INDEX_TERMINAL`; the count goes 91 → 92.

**No new kernel measurement was needed for the family, only ONE row for its soundness.** `RuleMessageIndexTerminalLawsTest` had measured this form richly and nothing reported it, which is the cheapest class of slice in the estate: measured-but-unreported. The one row that had to be added is the one that makes reporting a single gate defensible — an unknown argument with NO suffix, which draws the argument class rather than the missing-suffix class and so proves the ordering the family relies on.

**Five gates, one reproduced, and the ordering is the whole argument.** Being FIRST is what makes a gate safe to report alone: nothing pre-empts it, so no reproduction of the other four is needed to know it was reached. The four after it each need the repeatability machinery computed from the condition's references, or a shape stored DM-JSON cannot spell — so their silence is a disclosed under-report with a stated reason rather than a gap. Read the other way, this is the rule the slice before it had to learn the hard way: a class drawn after an unreproduced gate cannot be claimed to have been reached.

**A row that PASSES the gate and fails a later one is the mirror's risk, not its noise.** `$index(ClaimNo)$` — the right argument, no suffix — is A12's `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_PARAMETER` and this family's silence, and the mirror asserts that silence explicitly with the reason. Left unasserted it would read as an untested shape rather than a decided one.

### C record — the same tier, batched by FORM rather than by code (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the error-text parameter's category suffix.** [KF413](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf413); three classes of A12's unprefixed error-text vocabulary newly reported — `NO_CATEGORY`, `FIELD_NO_ENUM`, `INVALID_CATEGORY`. New family `ERROR_TEXT_CATEGORY_SUFFIX`; the count goes 90 → 91.

**Eight of eight predicted on the first run**, against four runs for the slice before it. The difference is not skill but the depth of the read: these three classes are thrown by two short methods with no delegation, so the source says what runs. The previous slice's law lived partly in `refEntities`, whose matching rule no reading settles — which is the standing signal for when to predict and when to dump an empty map.

**Batching the TIER by parameter FORM is what made both slices cheap.** A12's message grammar is its third DSL and each form — plain, semantic index, category suffix, row specifier, base-year terminal — carries its own gate and its own classes. Taking a form at a time gives each family a boundary it can state, and the membership family's own KDoc had already enumerated the remaining forms, so the backlog was written down before the work started.

**Both families AGREED WITH A12 on a carrier neither had measured, and that is why both were narrowed.** The category family answered `FIELD_NO_ENUM` for `$index(Note)->Band$` — which is what A12 draws there, so nothing was red and nothing would ever have been. But `checkIndexFieldRef` owns the `index(…)` terminal and has eleven classes for it, several about an ENUM index field, so the agreement held on the one carrier tried and the families disagree exactly where this one is confident. Found by reading the NEXT slice's existing grid and noticing a row whose shape the just-committed family would also match. Each family now claims only its own parameter form, and the row is locked as deliberate silence rather than left to luck. The transferable rule: when a tier is split by FORM, every family needs an explicit refusal of its neighbours' forms — the shapes overlap textually, so silence has to be written rather than assumed.

**The membership ordering is the row both new families leave alone, and they say so.** `checkFieldRef` resolves against the condition first, so an unread field draws `INVALID_FIELD` whatever its suffix or key says. Neither family names a class there; the plain-form family that owns `INVALID_FIELD` reads only the plain form, so that row is an under-report disclosed in both docs rather than a class anyone reports.

### C record — the first family reading A12's THIRD DSL against its first (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the error-text parameter's semantic index.** [KF412](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf412); one code drained — `MVK_INDEX_FOR_ERROR_TEXT_INVALID`. New family `ERROR_TEXT_SEMANTIC_INDEX`; the count goes 89 → 90. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 126 reported**.

**A12 resolves a message parameter against the rule's CONDITION, not against the model**, which is what makes this family a comparison of two DSLs rather than a check of one. The message grammar is A12's third, and its indexed form reduces to the same `(field, key)` pair the condition writes — so the checker reuses `semanticIndexUsesIn` on both texts and compares. Reusing one reader for both sides is also what made the first draft wrong in a way worth recording: the value half was resolved with a bare-name reader, which silently judged nothing for every multi-segment path and left four of nine rows unreported.

**The discriminating pair is the finding.** A bound key does NOT make its repetition readable: `$Shipments/TrackingNo For "T-1"$` is admitted on a condition that never names `TrackingNo`, while `$Shipments/Carrier For "T-1"$` — a plain sibling at the same key — is refused. Either row alone yields a wrong law in a different direction, and only the pair gives the real one: the condition's own pairings, plus the field that names the key. The rival mechanism was named and its discriminating case run before the clause was written, rather than after a reviewer asked.

**A grammar file is one layer, and this one says the opposite of what runs.** `BaseErrorTextParameterLexer.g4` carries `FOR: 'Fuer'`, so the grid was first written with every row spelled `Fuer` and measured nine parse errors; an `en_US` model takes `For`. One run corrected it, which is the argument for measuring the spelling in the same grid rather than trusting the read — the cost was one invocation and the alternative was a family that reported nothing on every English model.

### C record — one question, three scans, wrong in both directions at once (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: what "the checker reports this code" MEANS, extracted to one reader.** No A12 code drained and no family changed — the count stays 89 at **125 of 142 reported** — but **17 ledger rows** were disclosed and the guard that should have demanded them was fixed at its root. New support class `CheckerReportedCodes` with its own controls; `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest` and `CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest` now read it, and `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` keeps its deliberately coarse token scan with the pattern corrected.

**The root cause is a character class, and all three guards had REASONED about it.** Each spelled a code as `MVK_[A-Z_]+[0-9]*`, and two carried a KDoc explaining that the trailing digits belong to the code because `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1` and `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` are two different laws — correct, cited, and beside the point. Digits also appear in the MIDDLE, and twelve ledger codes spell them there: the whole `_A12_DV` family, `MVK_ERROR_FIELDGROUP_ITERATION4_INVALID`, `MVK_POINT_IN_A12_MODEL`. A documented rationale for the case you thought of reads, to the next session, exactly like a rationale for the case you did not.

**The two scans failed in OPPOSITE directions and both stayed green.** The narrow one followed a helper literally named `finding(…)`, so it saw 99 of the 193 codes this checker states — 27 of the 34 `Finding`-returning helpers carry other names, and `nameFinding(` has no word boundary before its `F`. The coarse one took every string literal, so the four codes held only as members of `Check.kt`'s phase and suppression lists read as reported. Neither failed: the narrow one by never looking, the coarse one by luck, because every set member but one happened to be named in some laws test. Correcting the pattern alone would have turned the coarse guard RED on honest content — `MVK_ITERATION_RESTRICTION_IN_A12_MODEL` is `erased` for a shape rulekit cannot render and could not be given a measurement.

**The right boundary is not "reaches a `Finding`" — it is "stated, versus compared against".** Narrowing to a `Finding` construction was the first fix attempted and it misses 86 genuine reports, because codes travel through intermediate defect records (`ListDefect("MVK_…", …)`, a `String?` returned two frames up) that no amount of following helper NAMES reaches. The one shape that is not a report is a code parked in a declared `setOf`. That rule was measured both ways: it drops all four false positives and keeps every genuine report.

**The fix carried the defect it was fixing, twice, and the same question caught both.** Written first as `setOf`/`listOf`/`mapOf` — "a declared collection is membership" — it hid four codes, because three families key their explain fragments by code (`SemanticIndexStructure.ADVICE` and two siblings) and look the key up while building the finding, which makes every key a report. And its type annotation was `[^=]*`, which crosses newlines: `enum class ConstantArm(val code: String, val admitted: Set<String>)` scanned on to the next file-level `= setOf(` and cut out the enum between them, taking three more reported codes. Both are unanchored spans, the defect class of the day; neither would have failed a guard, since all seven rows already said `checker-reported`. What found them was asking the reader the question it exists to answer — *which codes are stated only inside a collection?* — rather than trusting it once it went green.

**The tell was a disagreement inside the ledger, found while ranking the remaining work.** Five rows said "Reported by the portable checker (`CheckFamily.ELEMENT_NAME_ALPHABET`)" in their prose while the cell beside them did not, which is the exact drift the disposition guard exists to prevent — so the guard's silence was evidence about the guard. Two of the 17 were worse than cosmetic: `MVK_ERROR_FIELDGROUP_ITERATION4_INVALID` and `ITERATION5` still read `kernel-delegated` for classes `ErrorTargetIteration.kt` had been reporting for two days, and this session had edited one of those rows without the cell going right, because nothing could see it.

**Which direction of error to leave in place, stated rather than assumed.** The reader can still over-count: a literal stated outside a set but never emitted reads as reported. That was chosen — over-counting only ever demands a truer ledger row or an earlier measurement, while the under-count it replaces hid nine stale rows and a guard's whole purpose.

### C record — the first family reproducing a gate from A12's CALCULATION module (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `NormalisierteBerechnungsFormHelper.checkBerechnungsFunktion`'s enum arm.** [KF411](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf411); one code drained — `MVK_ERROR_INVALID_ENUM_CALCULATION`. New family `ENUM_COMPUTATION_SOURCE`; the count goes 88 → 89. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 125 reported**.

**Every family so far reproduced the PARSER; this one reproduces the module that runs after it, and that is what shapes its scope.** A computation is lowered to a comparison against its target, so the comparison walk answers first wherever it has a class — three of the ten rows are pre-empted, and a reader building the gate from its kernel source alone would report all three. The TEXT source is what keeps the gate from being redundant: a plain text against a TEXTLESS enumeration is ADMITTED by that walk, so only the calculation gate catches it.

**Two rows had to be a PAIR or the law is unmeasured.** Containment runs one way, and a mirrored source passes both an equality test and a subset test — so "declaring FEWER" and "declaring MORE" together are the measurement, and either alone reads as a clean row that proves nothing. The category rows are the same shape: one covered, one not.

**Three mispredictions, all of them earlier gates rather than wrong laws.** The pre-emptions were invisible from the calc module's source, which is the standing lesson about reading one layer: a locally correct read of `isEnumValueCompatible` gives exactly the wrong answer for three of its own inputs. The fourth miss was a spelling — `Concat(...)` is not the operator, `+` is — and it hid a row that turned out to confirm the allow-list arm.

### C record — the slice that went looking for a code and found an over-report (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `IterationChecker`'s phase, not its classes.** [KF410](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf410); no code drained and one OVER-REPORT removed — the checker named `MVK_ERROR_FIELDGROUP_ITERATION5_INVALID` beside the parse-time `MVK_MULTIPLE_INDEX_FIELDS` on any model whose repeatable ROOT carries an index field. No family changed; the three `IterationChecker` classes joined `POST_PARSE_CONDITION_CODES`. The count stays 88 at **124 of 142 reported**.

**The phase list had been read off ONE syntactic form, and that is the transferable defect.** Its own comment says the members are "the `throwError` calls in `ParserService.checkNormalRule`'s own body, read off kernel `30.8.1` in source order" — a careful, citable enumeration that misses everything the method DELEGATES. That body makes two such calls and they carry five A12-model classes between them; only the one the checker already reported made the list, so nothing went red. A denominator assembled by grepping a keyword is bounded by the keyword, not by the question.

**The new-code pre-flight was too narrow, and two runs paid for it.** It read "a NEW code the checker REPORTS costs up to three guard rows"; the trigger is actually a code the checker's main source SPELLS, because the corrective projection scans text. Adding an inert member to a phase list duly projected an entry no report can deliver, and `CorrectiveTest` went red twice. The residue is now a named, asserted set rather than an empty one — a code with real owner guidance and no emitting family is a legitimate state, and naming it keeps the guard catching the dead entry it exists for.

**The sweep is what made it a class fix rather than a patch**, and it found two more members after the defect was already green: `MVK_ITERATION_RESTRICTION_IN_A12_MODEL` from the same delegate, and `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` from the second call. Both are inert today, which is precisely why they belong in the list — the next family to report one would repeat this defect with nothing going red, and the list exists for that and not for the members already live.

**The target code was not drained, and saying so is the result.** `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_AND_ITERATION_INVALID` guards level 0 — the level its two siblings' loop explicitly skips — and seven spellings failed to reach it, each for a different reason. That is an enumeration of attempts, not a proof of unreachability, so the ledger row records the attempts and stays delegated. Claiming the class unauthorable from seven failures is exactly the reachability claim the standing rule forbids.

**An exploratory grid asserting an EMPTY map is the cheapest instrument for an unpredictable chain.** The level model here could not be predicted from source — `getMax`, the index sizing, and the wildcard pre-emptions all interact — so the accelerator's "predict the whole grid" does not apply. Asserting `Map.of()` turns one run into a complete measurement of fourteen cells, and the round-two probes cost one more. Two runs bought a finding, a fix and a bounded negative result; predicting would have bought a wrong prediction.

### C record — the slice written whole before it was measured (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckAnzahlWertOpImpl`'s string arm past its type walk.** [KF409](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf409); one code drained — `MVK_NO_DATE_CONSTANT`, whose single caller in the estate this is — plus a further carrier for `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. An EXTENSION of `VALUE_IN_FIELDS_CONSTANT_KIND` rather than a family: same kernel method, same switch, two more statements of the same arm. The count stays 88; the measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 124 reported**.

**A ledger row named a construct the code does not have.** It read "date extraction takes fields/expressions, not string literals" — plausible, wrong, and it would have sent a reader to the extractors. One grep for the callers settled it. The general form is worth carrying: a `kernel-delegated` row's PROSE is an unmeasured claim, and the cheapest moment to check it is when the code is drained.

**The accelerator went one step further and the step paid for itself.** The last four slices measured the grid first and then wrote the checker. Here the laws grid, the family change, the reader promotion and the mirror were all written from one source read, and ONE invocation measured all four — twenty-six kernel cells and twenty-six mirror cells, every one right. A wrong prediction would have cost two text edits and no extra build, which is strictly cheaper than the two builds the split order spends every time.

**The near-misses are the measurement; the positives are almost free.** Seven spellings that look like dates are counted as text — wrong length, wrong shape, or a real pattern behind a non-lenient parse. A reader matching the shape alone would refuse all seven, and no positive row would have caught it.

**Separating "no offender" from "cannot judge" was the whole implementation risk.** The type walk returned one `null` for both while the type class was all the family reported; adding two gates behind it makes the conflation a live defect, because a list A12 has already refused would draw a later gate's class. The walk now answers with three cases and the tail gates run only on the third.

### C record — the slice whose kind gate answers for eleven declarations (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckFeldOpMitDatumSpecialImpl.check`, `ValueAsDate`'s four-gate operand chain.** [KF408](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf408); one code drained — `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE_FOR_OP` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED`. New family `VALUE_AS_DATE_OPERAND`; the count goes 87 → 88. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 123 reported**.

**One class for eleven declarations is why the whole type domain had to be measured rather than sampled.** The gate is `isDatePartialKnown`, two questions behind one code, and the second question is the one the operator's name hides: A12's ADO layer gives `TimeType`, `DateTimeType` and `DateFragmentType` the same `IDateType` interface a plain date carries. A checker keyed on the interface would have admitted three kinds A12 refuses, and no row short of the full domain would have caught it — every date row it looked at would have agreed.

**A whole-grid prediction cost one run and was wrong in exactly one cell.** Twenty-seven cells from one source read; the miss was the ROOT group, predicted `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED` and measured the plain group class. The lesson generalises past this operator: the root class belongs to the methods that ACCEPT a group and exclude the root one, so a method with no group arm never reaches it — a class is not available to a chain merely because the chain's operand could draw it elsewhere.

**The pre-emption row is what licenses the family's silence, and it needed the anchored twin to say anything.** An unstarred read into a repetition draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from the reference reader and hides all four classes — but the SAME read from the row's own rule reaches them, so the silence is keyed on the declaring group rather than on the operand's spelling. A grid carrying only the outer row would have justified a blanket silence that drops two real findings.

### C record — the slice that had to fix the authoring kit before it could measure (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`'s DISPLAY-value half.** [KF407](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf407); two codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_STRING` and `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_ENUM`. New family `ENUM_DISPLAY_COMPARABILITY`; the count goes 86 → 87. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 122 reported**.

**A fixture the typed builder refused to author was the finding, not an obstacle.** The grid needed an enumeration labelled only for an undeclared locale; `DocumentModelDraft` threw. The refusal turned out to be over-strict in two ways and was fixed first, as its own commit ([AF149](API-FINDINGS.md#af149), [KF406](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf406)) — which is the dogfood rule behaving exactly as written: the friction was the signal, and working around it with hand-written JSON would have hidden a live defect in a shipped kit.

The display question is about the PAIR — the same enumeration is fine opposite one partner and refused opposite another — and that family's reader is a per-SIDE walk whose type cannot carry a pair. Carrier-disjointness held: the halves partition the comparison, measured, so the two never contend.

**Two rows that look like implementation detail are the ones that bound the whole family.** A label for an undeclared locale leaves an enumeration TEXTLESS, and the value-label law forces the declared locales to be answered together — so A12's "shared display locales" intersection can never be narrower than the declared set, and a reader walking the declared locales is exact rather than approximate. Without those two measurements the family would have carried an intersection it could not justify.

### C record — the slice measured in one grid rather than five (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckFeldOpMitParamsImpl.check`, the two substring operators' one checker.** [KF405](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf405); two codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_TYPE` and `MVK_INVALID_INTERVAL` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED` and `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. New family `RANGE_OPERAND_GATES`; the count goes 85 → 86. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 120 reported**.

**The whole grid was written from one source read and measured once, and every cell was right.** The slice before it grew its grid across five runs because each measurement suggested the next question — which is the accelerator half-applied. The method here has six gates, so the questions are enumerable before any of them is asked: every gate against every other, and every declared type against the kind gate. Writing them all costs one file and one run; discovering them costs a build each.

**A code the kits PRE-CHECK is still a code the checker must report.** `MVK_INVALID_INTERVAL` was dispositioned `pre-checked`, which is a claim about the typed authoring surface and says nothing about a stored model — and the checker reads stored DM-JSON, where no builder ran. The disposition is now `pre-checked / checker-reported`, and the general form is worth carrying: a `pre-checked` row is a CANDIDATE for this checker, not an exclusion from it.

**Two readers were promoted rather than copied.** The `isLikeString` complement and the operand-with-category reduction both already existed, private, in the family that needed them first; the second consumer is what makes them shared readers. The alternative — a second copy of the type allow-list — is the failure mode this plan has already paid for once, where a set spelled `CustomType` silently matched nothing.

### C record — the slice that corrected the chain it was extending (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckSemanticIndexImpl.checkIndexField` and its containment test.** [KF404](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf404); two codes drained — `MVK_INDEX_TYPE_INCONSISTENT` and `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_CONTAINED_IN_INDEX` — plus a fourth carrier for `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. No new family: the residue sits in the same kernel method on the same carrier, so `SEMANTIC_INDEX_STRUCTURE` was extended and the count stays at **85**. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 118 reported**.

**Measuring a residue re-opened the chain it was joining, and the wrong class was already shipped.** `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_BELOW_WILDCARD` had been placed second in the chain from a source reading — `getIndexGroup` runs before the key is examined — and it is in fact LAST, raised by a later pass over the finished index list. Nothing in the earlier grid could have caught it: every one of its rows paired that star with a class that does answer ahead of it. **A gate's position is only measured against the gates it was actually paired with**, so a chain locked by N rows is not a chain locked end to end, and the cheap check is to pair the newcomer with every class on both sides of it rather than only with its predicted neighbour.

**A per-family type comparison had to be written rather than reused.** The shared `BaseTypeClass` reader splits the temporal declarations, and A12's model layer has ONE date interface — so reusing it here would have refused a time key against a date index field that A12 admits. The family carries its own table with the temporal declarations merged, which under-reports inside that class and cannot over-reject; the shared reader stays as it is, because its own three consumers measured their carriers separately.

**Reading the rule's ANCHOR is what made the second code reachable at all.** The family had treated every key inside a repetition as opaque, which is right for an unanchored rule and wrong for a rule declared in that repetition — and a key inside the indexed group is anchored by definition, so the blanket reading made the class unreportable rather than merely deferred. The helper that decides it already existed in another family, private; it is now a shared reader, and the two rows that license the widening are the anchored key indexing a THIRD group, measured admitted and refused.

### C record — the slice whose subject was a MODEL declaration rather than a construct (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckKonstanteReferenzjahrImpl` and `CheckDatumKonstruktImpl.checkJahresParam`.** [KF403](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf403); two codes drained — `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` and `MVK_MISSING_YEAR`. **A family keyed on a MODEL-LEVEL declaration is a shape the plan had not produced before, and it earns its own constant for the reason the others do not.** Nothing about the `BaseYear` constant and the year-less `Date` construction is shared at the construct level — different grammar rules, different checker classes, no common carrier. What they share is a property of the MODEL, so the law belongs to the declaration and the family's first line is its precondition: a model that declares one reports nothing at all.

**The declaration is not one switch in one direction.** It ENABLES these two shapes and DISABLES the year-agreement walk of the range-overlap operators, landed two slices earlier. A family assuming "a base year relaxes year questions" would have got half of that backwards, and only holding the three together makes the asymmetry visible — which is the argument for landing related slices close in time rather than by convenience.

**Widening one family's arity gate closed a silence in another, and the stale row was in a MIRROR that had never been measured.** `DATE_CONSTRUCTION_SLOT` declined every two-parameter `Date` as an unjudged arity, so its mirror asserted the checker says nothing there — and nothing had ever asked A12. This slice's grid did: the day and month gates run whatever the arity, so the written slots are judged and only the missing year is the new family's. **A mirror row expecting silence is a claim about A12 that no laws grid covers unless someone put that shape to the kernel** — the same class as the empty-cell finding one slice earlier, arriving from the opposite direction.

### C record — the slice where the empty MIRROR cell was the finding (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckPredefinedTypeValidityConditionImpl` and `CheckDateValidityConditionImpl`.** [KF402](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf402); three codes drained — `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM_OR_EXT_ENUM`, `MVK_NO_EMPTY` and `MVK_ONLY_CONSTANTS_USED` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. New family `VALIDITY_CONDITION_OPERANDS`; the count goes 83 → 84. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 114 reported**.

**The mirror is not a transcription step — it is the only instrument that finds a gate with NO owner.** Twice in two slices, a gate assumed to belong to some pre-gate family turned out to belong to nobody, and both times what surfaced it was an empty cell in the mirror opposite a kernel class. The laws grid cannot find it (A12 answers, as expected) and neither can a source read (the gate is plainly in another method). **So write the mirror's expectation from what the checker SHOULD say, never from what you assume some other family already says** — and when the cell comes back empty, the question is whether anything reads that carrier at all.

**One keyword, two grammar rules, two checker classes: the operand SHAPE is the discriminator.** `Valid(Field, "Type")` and `Valid(Date(…))` share a keyword and share nothing else, so the family branches on arity and on whether the sole operand is a construction. The two arms are carrier-disjoint by construction, which is what licenses one family for two kernel classes.

**A code's NAME listed a kind that is not one.** `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM_OR_EXT_ENUM` reads as three kinds and `isStringEnumOrExtensibleEnum`'s third disjunct is a flag on the first, so a `CustomFieldType` is refused. The ledger row had carried the name's reading since it was written and is corrected; the catalog's constraint prose said the same thing and is sharpened in the same change, which is where an operator-level fact belongs.

**A neighbouring family's walk was REUSED rather than restated.** The date arm's question is asked after the construction's own slot gates, so it needs both "does a slot already fail" and the slot walk's own reading of "this slot names an entity" — an EXTRACT operator does not, which is the arm's most surprising row. Exposing `readDateConstruction` from the slot family keeps one live statement of what a slot demands; restating either half would have been two chances to disagree about one law.

### C record — the slice where one gate's escape hatch was the whole finding (2026-09-14)

Of 64 kernel codes this checker does not report, roughly 25 are genuinely open; the rest are unreachable, pre-checked by a kit, message labels, or declined by decision.

Both were measured with and without one rather than inferred from the first.

Simplifying it to "does this reference carry a year" would have been cleaner code and would have admitted a model A12 rejects — the checker reproduces the step and says why in the one place a later reader would try to tidy it.

**One gate was closed rather than disclosed because the mirror measurement asked the question.** The plain list's group gate was predicted to be some other family's and is nobody's: the arity family declines a group operand on its own account, its law being about how many FIELDS a list names. The mirror's empty cell is what surfaced it, and reporting it cost four lines — cheaper than the disclosure paragraph it replaced.

### C record — the slice keyed on a SLOT rather than on an operator (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckDatumAngabeImpl`, the date-argument slot every date operator hands its operands to.** [KF400](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf400); three codes drained — `MVK_NO_DATE`, `MVK_INVALID_DATE_STRING` and `MVK_INVALID_DATE_FOR_OP` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`. New family `DATE_ARGUMENT_SLOT`; the count goes 81 → 82. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 108 reported**.

**A family keyed on the SLOT costs one map entry per operator; one keyed on the operator costs a table each.** Sixteen operators across four grammar families — difference, shift, numeric extraction, temporal extraction — share these gates byte for byte, because the kernel factored the slot out. `DATE_SLOTS_OF` therefore maps each operator to WHICH of its operand positions are date slots and nothing else. This is the same shape the previous slices reached by merging, arrived at from the source instead: **when the kernel has factored a check out, the checker's unit is that factor, not its callers.**

**The gate that is one layer up looks exactly like the gate that is here, and telling them apart is the whole slice.** `FieldUtils.isDate` tests `IDateType`, which covers time, stamp and fragment as well as date, so the slot ADMITS a `TimeType` and the carrier then refuses it as `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`. A reader that took "a date slot demands a date" from the class name would answer `MVK_NO_DATE` on a model A12 rejects with the other class — a correct rejection under the wrong name, which this project counts as a real defect. Four rows in the grid exist only to separate the two layers, and the same pair recurs on the constant form: the slot accepts `"01.02."` and the carrier refuses the MIXTURE as `MVK_DATE_WITH_AND_WITHOUT_YEAR`.

**Four of the grid's cells were predicted wrong and the measurement corrected all four**, three of them in that layering and one in the calendar: `"29.02."` is REFUSED, because the year-less pattern's missing year defaults to the epoch's 1970, which is not a leap year. The intuitive prediction — a year-less date being year-agnostic — went into a KDoc before it was measured and had to be corrected there too. **Predict the grid, but never write the prose before the run.**
