**A LENGTH switch is a denominator question, not a pattern question.** `DateUtils.isStringAValidDate` picks its one pattern by the string's length, so the accepted shapes are four rather than the two the kernel's own javadoc names, and two of the grid's refusals follow from the switch rather than from any pattern: a stamp spelled with a space is 19 characters and fails the date-time pattern, and the year-less shape stripped of its trailing dot is a length no case handles.

**The three guard rows a NEW code costs are now pre-flight rather than discovery.** Recorded in §2 after they cost three lane reruns on the previous slice; all three were satisfied before the first lane here.

### C record — the slice that merged across two kernel METHODS, and the rule that let it (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckFeldListenAnzahlOpImpl`'s distinct-count arm.** [KF399](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf399); three residue codes drained — `MVK_STRING_ENUM_AND_NON_STRING_ENUM`, `MVK_NUMBER_AND_NON_NUMBER` and `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_CMP_DATE_ALLOWED` — plus a third carrier for `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. Landed as a fifth carrier of `FIELD_LIST_OPERATION_OPERANDS`; the count stays at 81.

**The three previous slices merged within one kernel method; this one merged ACROSS two, which needs a different justification.** The pre-emption rule says two families reporting gates of ONE method over-report by construction — it says nothing about two methods. What licenses this merge is the weaker condition the rule is really about: the two methods never share a CARRIER, so neither family can name the other's class on any model. That plus an identical question (what kind must each expanded field be), identical pre-emptions (the shared pre-gates and the same `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN` arity gate) and an identical leader-decides-the-arm structure made a second family pure duplication. **The condition to check at a merge is therefore carrier-disjointness, not method identity.**

**The derivation that picked the slice overstated it, and the correction is worth keeping.** Subtracting reported codes from the codes each kernel file raises showed this file at 4 of 4 open — a whole unreported file. But the scan counts only literal `ParserErrorTypesEnum.X` references in a file's own text, and this file mostly DELEGATES: its arity, duplicate, root-group and temporal gates are attributed to `CheckEntityListenUtils`, where three families already own them, and one of its four codes is H-model-only. The real residue was three classes on one of three arms. **Per-file counts read high wherever the kernel delegates; re-derive with that in mind rather than citing the count.**

**A leader-decides-the-arm construct hides its hardest row in the branch that has no arm.** "Not string, not number, not date" reads like the numeric arm's territory and is a class of its own; three unclaimed leaders were measured separately rather than inferred, because the else branch is a complement and a complement is what a checker may not infer. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 105 reported**.

**One under-report has no owner and is disclosed rather than closed.** `checkNoCategories` is the first statement of this method and outruns even the duplicate walk — but the shared operand reducer declines a categorized operand, so no family sees such a call. Widening the reducer is a change to every family that reads an operand, which is its own slice; the silence is stated in the family, the finding, the ledger row and a locked mirror row so it cannot read as agreement.


### C record — the slice where the gate's own NAME was the trap, twice in one method (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the rest of `CheckFeldUndWerteListenBedingungImpl`.** [KF398](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf398); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE` and `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUMERATION_CATEGORY` — plus five further carriers (`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON`, `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`). `ENUM_CONSTANT_DOMAIN` is renamed `SCALAR_VALUE_LIST_GATES`; the count stays at 81.

**The pre-emption question was asked first and answered "merge", which is now the third time.** The incumbent family reported ONE gate of this method — the plain enum domain — and the six others were unreported by anyone. Two families reporting gates of one kernel method independently is an over-report by construction, so the slice extended the incumbent rather than adding a neighbour, and the rename followed from the previous slice's own rule: name the family after the METHOD's subject.

**A gate's NAME is evidence about nothing, and this method has two that say the wrong thing about the same declaration.** `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED` names three kinds and admits a fourth, because `isLikeString` takes `IPredefinedType` beside `IStringType`; and `matchBaseType` groups those same two, so a CUSTOM `[field]` entry matches a STRING subject where an ENUMERATION entry does not. The second cell is the one the grid got wrong on its first run — predicted `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`, measured admitted — and it is the only cell of thirty-one that missed.

**The measurement did NOT go into the shared base-type map, and that restraint is the point.** `baseTypeClass` has three other consumers, each asking the same question on a carrier where the custom grouping is unmeasured; adding the row would have changed all four families on one carrier's evidence. The grouping lives in this family with its own lock, and the shared map's KDoc keeps saying the grouping is unmeasured elsewhere — which is now true in a narrower way and stays honest.

**The refusal set was measured as seven rows rather than inferred as a complement.** A12 asks three POSITIVE predicates of the subject, so "what it refuses" is their complement over a thirteen-name vocabulary — and a complement is exactly the shape a checker may not infer, because an unmeasured member lands on the over-rejecting side. Three extra cells (Time, DateTime, DateFragment) cost one rerun and turned a transferred claim into a measured one.


### C record — the slice that found a WRONG CLASS in the family it was extending (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the rest of `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl`.** [KF397](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf397); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_DATE_RANGE_COMPARISON` and `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUMERATION_OR_CATEGORY` — plus a fourth carrier for `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED` and a second for `MVK_NO_CONST`. That kernel class is now fully reported. `VALUE_LIST_QUANTIFIER_OPERAND_KIND` is renamed `VALUE_LIST_CONDITION_GATES`; the count stays at 81.

**The defect was in the incumbent family and only a row it did not hold could see it.** It merged the field list and the value list into one same-type walk, which matches A12 wherever the field list is listable — every row its own grid held. Put a DATE-RANGE field list opposite a text one and A12 answers with the field list's own kind class, since the field gates run before the value list's type is looked at; the family would have answered with the disagreement class. **This is the constant-across-the-set confound in its plainest form: the set varied the disagreement and held "the field list is listable" fixed, so the account fit every row and was wrong.** The new slice exposed it by asking about the operator the old family had EXCLUDED, which is where the constant breaks.

**A silence that was really an exclusion turned into a locked handover.** The family had left `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` out entirely, justified by the classes its own arm owns — but the same-type gate runs ahead of the branch on the operator token and applies to all four. One measured row converted a mirror expectation of silence into an asserted code, and the operator is now a carrier.

**Two readings of "exactly one" separated by one row.** The date-range shape gate reads the REFERENCE list, so a group holding a single date-range field is refused even though every other operator in the same construct expands a group to its fields. A checker built on the expansion — which this family already had — would have admitted it.

**The rename is the second this session and the pattern is now clear enough to state.** A family named after one of its gates (`…_OPERAND_KIND`, `…_OPERATOR_ARITY`) stops describing itself the moment a neighbouring gate of the same kernel method lands, and the next slice of that method always arrives. Name the family after the METHOD's subject, not after the question the first slice happened to answer.

### C record — two codes that MERGED a family instead of adding one, and the measurement that forced it (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the type walk of `CheckOperationImpl`.** [KF396](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf396); two residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_NUMBER_FOR_CALC` and `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_NUMBER_FOR_ADD` — which completes that kernel class. `CALCULATION_OPERATOR_ARITY` is renamed `ARITHMETIC_OPERATION_GATES` and now carries all five; the count stays at 81 because nothing was added.

**The merge was not a tidiness call — a measured pre-emption made two families wrong.** `check` runs the type walk before both arity gates, so a string in a two-division chain is the kind class ALONE, with the all-numbers control drawing the division class. Two families reporting independently would have emitted a second class on a model A12 answers once, which is the over-report the plan's third open lane names and which no test in this estate can currently see. The slice found it by asking the precedence question before writing the family rather than after.

**The tell that a slice belongs INSIDE an existing family is a shared walk, not a shared code.** The two questions have disjoint code sets and disjoint kernel methods; what they share is one `OperationContext` and one order over it. A split by code — the shape every earlier slice took — would have cut that order in half.

**The renaming is the cost of having named a family after one of its gates.** `CALCULATION_OPERATOR_ARITY` was accurate when it held three codes and became a misdescription the moment the walk's first gate landed, and a constant that under-describes its family is read by the report's own consumers. Six sites plus three ledger rows; worth doing in the same commit rather than leaving a name that would have to be explained.

**One measured surprise, and it is the kind a source read produces the wrong shape of.** `+` refuses an enumeration whose values carry display LABELS, beside a plain text, under the KIND class rather than one of its own — two enumerations declaring the same tokens differ only in that property. The grid varies exactly it, with the labelless twin as the control.

### C record — the eighty-first family, and a construct where two operator NAMES point the wrong way (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what each of the four single-field value operators demands of the one field it reads.** [KF395](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf395); three residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_STRING`, `MVK_NO_CONVERT_IN_NUMBER` and the first REPORT of `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED`, on a construct that also widens `MVK_NO_NUMBER`, `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` to a further carrier.

**Slicing by kernel FILE landed the whole grid in one measurement again.** `CheckFeldOpImpl` is a straight-line head followed by a switch on the operator token, so every cell the source settles was predictable and only the cells A12 alone can settle needed a `?` — there were none, and roughly 45 cells went green on the first run. The three preceding slices behaved the same way; the pattern is now reliable enough to plan around.

**Two of the four operators are named for the opposite of what they demand, and only the grid says so.** `FieldValueAsString` refuses a string and takes a number; `FieldValueAsNumber` is the one arm that admits a CATEGORY, which its three siblings refuse. Neither is derivable from the names, from the sibling arms, or from the ledger rows — `MVK_NO_STRING`'s row had attributed the class to both `Length` and `FieldValueAsString`, and half of that was wrong.

**An unreachability claim cost two models rather than one, deliberately.** `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE` sits behind a gate whose demand excludes its own trigger, so the honest test attacks BOTH links: declare both and A12 answers the inconsistency class beside it, drop the pattern and the conversion class answers first. Measuring each link separately would have established neither, which is the reachability row's own rule applied rather than cited.

**The mirror's fixture trap recurred one layer out from where it was recorded.** A raw `"` inside the substituted condition breaks the embedding JSON and the model then carries no rule, which reads exactly like the family staying silent — recorded after the last slice for conditions written IN the raw string, and hit again for a condition assembled in Kotlin code, where `\"` is just a quote. Every `FieldValueAsString` row went silent at once, which is the tell: a whole operator failing while its siblings pass is a fixture fault, not a family fault.

### C record — four type sets matched a token A12 never writes, and one of them inverted into an over-rejection (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the custom field type's discriminator.** [KF394](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf394). A12 stores `CustomFieldType`; four of the checker's type sets matched `CustomType`. No new family, so the count stays at 80.

**A set that matches nothing is the quietest defect this estate can hold.** Three of the four sets were refusal lists, so the families reading them went silent on every custom declaration — an under-report, invisible from every direction at once: each family's mirror measured the checker against itself and agreed, no laws grid had put a custom field to the kernel, and the shared reader `baseTypeClass` returned `null` for a custom declaration exactly as three consumers' KDocs said it deliberately did. The accidental silence and the documented one were indistinguishable.

**The fourth was the forbidden direction, and it is the one that makes this a defect rather than a gap.** `ValueInFieldsConstantKind`'s text arm lists the declarations A12 ADMITS, so a token that matches nothing removes an admission: the family reported `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM` on a custom field under a text constant, which A12 takes. Its own KDoc table already said "String, Custom, Enumeration" — the intent was never in doubt, only the spelling — which is the tell worth carrying: **a set whose members are strings can disagree with the prose above it and nothing will say so.**

**The guard is `CheckerFieldTypeTokenGuardTest`, with both denominators derived.** The legal spellings come from `FieldTypeBinding.kt`'s own `DECLARED_FIELD_TYPES`, the table mirroring A12's deserializer switch; the scanned side is every checker source outside the `syntax` package, whose `ValidType`/`InvalidType` are operator ids — and the exclusion is itself asserted, so it cannot start hiding a real set. A shared constant would not have helped: the sets that bite are the literal ones a new family writes out, and there were seven when this landed.

**The measurement is five carriers wide because five methods ask the question separately.** Both rounding constructs, the summing and the ordering field-list arms, and a date shift's amount each refuse a custom declaration with their own class, and the value-list text arm admits it — with a number control beside it, since an ADMITTED cell means nothing without a row that fails. Every predicted cell landed on the first run.

### C record — a code the owning family had ruled out, re-opened by a helper that arrived later (2026-09-14)

**Frozen: an ITERATING semantic-index key, `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_INVALID_ITER`.** [KF351](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf351), amended with one new cell. It is not a new family — it is the first gate of `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY`, ahead of that family's own class, which is where A12 asks it.

**Its own KDoc had ruled it out, and the reason had expired.** The family said the gate "turns on the rule's anchoring, which this family cannot see" — true when it was written, and false once `OperandRepetitionScope` grew the anchoring lookup and the level helpers moved to a shared file. A recorded silence is a claim about the code as it stood; nothing re-reads those claims when the code beneath them changes, which is an argument for citing what a silence DEPENDS on rather than only stating it.

**The new cell is the one the reader could have got wrong in the safe-looking direction.** A NON-repeatable group inside a repetition, as the key: the key-kind class would have been a plausible verdict and the iteration gate takes it. Measured rather than predicted, because predicting it would have been a claim about which of two gates reads the key first.

**The reporting geometry is narrower than the gate**, stated in the family and in the row: only a repeatable level strictly below the error target's group, which is where both halves were measured. A PARALLEL level draws one of four index-keyed classes outside an index and nothing has measured which name it takes inside one, so that shape stays silent rather than guessing a rename.

### C record — the selection method's own input had drifted, and now a guard holds it (2026-09-14)

**33 MVK-LEDGER rows said the checker did not report a code it had been reporting for days or weeks.** Several of those rows' own prose already said the checker reported them, beside a disposition cell that did not — the two-copies-of-one-fact smell, in a registry whose column is read mechanically.

**This is not a tidiness finding; the column STEERS the work.** Family selection ranks `| kernel-delegated |` rows by how much measurement already sits against them, so a stale row puts a finished code at the top of the list. It happened twice in this session before the sweep: `MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE` was selected as delegated and was only half-true, and `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` sat at the top of the next ranking while the checker had been reporting it all along. The cost of a stale row is a session spent re-deriving finished work.

**The guard is `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest`, and its denominator is derived on both sides**: every A12 code appearing in a `Finding(...)` construction in the checker's own sources must have `checker-reported` in its ledger cell. The scan is deliberately NARROWER than the neighbouring `CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest`'s, which takes every string literal: over-counting is harmless when asking *was this measured* and is not harmless here, because the checker holds codes it never reports and demanding a row for those would fail honest content. Two exclusions have their reasons in the class — comments, where this estate's KDoc routinely names the neighbouring class a family stays silent on, and string interpolation, where a message tells an author which code the reported one is not.

**Only one direction is locked, and that is the honest limit.** A row claiming a report the checker no longer emits would need a scan that follows every helper, and the same over-counting returns. The drift that actually happens is one-way — the family lands and the row lags — so the guard covers the direction with the evidence.

### C record — the eightieth family, and the comparand confound on a second axis (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what the six rounding operators demand of the value they round and of the digit count.** [KF393](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf393); `MVK_INVALID_DIGIT_FOR_ROUNDING` and `MVK_NO_CONST` drained, and three more codes gained a carrier.

**One construct in the catalog turned out to be two in the kernel.** The `…Value` spelling and the bare one are different grammar rules with different operand gates, and nothing in the operator names says so — the `code-owner` file mode over `CheckOpUtils` returned four open codes spread across four unrelated callers, and reading those callers is what partitioned them into one coherent family and three other slices.

**The comparand confound cost the one round this slice spent, on an axis the previous note did not cover.** `ExtremumOperandKindGridLawsTest` records it for the operand KIND; here it is the SCALE — a rounded result's scale is the digits argument, so a field comparand fixes a scale of its own and `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` masked every admitted cell. The generalisation now in both classes' prose: **compare against a literal unless the row is about the comparand.**

**The remaining three `CheckOpUtils` codes are named and left.** `MVK_NO_NUMBER_FOR_CALC` types the arms of `^ * / + -`, which are operations this reader does not type; `MVK_NO_DATE_CONSTANT` extends the existing `VALUE_IN_FIELDS_CONSTANT_KIND` family rather than starting one; `MVK_NO_CONST`'s second carrier is `CheckFeldOperOpImpl`, a different construct. Each is a slice, not a widening of this one.

### C record — the seventy-ninth family, and a gate ORDER that inverts what the source seems to say (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a date-arithmetic operator demands of the date it shifts and of the amount it shifts by.** [KF392](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf392); `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`, `MVK_INVALID_TIME` and `MVK_NON_NUMERIC` drained, and `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` gained a second carrier.

**The finding is the ORDER, not any one gate.** Read on its own, "the format must carry a date component" says a day shift over a clock is `MVK_INVALID_TIME`; measured, it is `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`, because the component gate is asked first and a clock carries no day. That inverts which operators can reach the class at all — only the three clock ones — and it is the kind of claim a source read settles only if the read is of the CALLER rather than of the utility.

**Forty cells, one measurement, every prediction right.** Two slices running now have landed their grid first try, and the common factor is that both transcribed a caller method whose gates are literally sequential statements. When the source is a straight-line sequence there is nothing to leave as `?`; the `?` belongs to predicates whose admitted set the name misreports, which is where the previous two slices spent their rounds.

**Two shared readers were promoted rather than copied** — `declaresAPartialPrecision` into `TemporalFormat`, `PLAINLY_NON_NUMBER_TYPES` into `Reference` — and the amount slot reuses `bareReferenceField`, which already answers "one declared field, or nothing" for both the bracketed and the bare spelling. A third private copy of that reduction was written and deleted the same hour; the tell was that it handled one spelling and the measured fixture used the other.

### C record — the seventy-eighth family, and the slice whose grid was already on disk (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what kind of declaration each field-LIST operation admits in the entity list they share.** [KF391](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf391); `MVK_NO_BOOLY_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` and `Sum`'s carrier of `MVK_NO_NUMBER` drained.

**Slicing by kernel FILE rather than by code paid for itself twice over.** `CheckFeldListenOpImpl` is one switch over one list, so its three open classes are one family and one KDoc — and reading the caller instead of the utilities is what showed that the extrema's numeric arm is the complement of an arm this repo already owned, rather than a new law.

**A third of the measurement was already on disk, and the accelerator is to look before cutting a grid.** `ExtremumOperandKindGridLawsTest` had measured the whole `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` axis in September — leader set, follower set, typedef resolution, group-expansion order, and the comparand confound — so the new grid covers only the confirm and `Sum` arms. `code-owner`'s locks column is what surfaced it: the code reads `reported by: NOBODY` while naming three lock classes, which is exactly the shape of a measured law with no reporter.

**Every predicted cell landed first try, on a grid of about seventy.** The source settles this family completely — `isYes` is `IYesType`, `isNumber` is `INumberType`, and `getOpTypeForField` fixes the leader arm — so nothing was left as `?`. The one thing worth measuring anyway was the BOOLEAN row, because the code's name says the opposite of what the predicate does.

**The handover between two families is now locked from both sides.** `TemporalAggregateOperandsTest` carried a row asserting silence on a number-led extremum with the comment "A12 answers `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` there"; that row now asserts the code, so deleting either arm fails a test that names the other.

### C record — the seventy-seventh family, and the grid that stopped an over-rejection (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what kind of field `FieldValuesNotUnique` admits, and how its temporal ones must be spelled.** [KF390](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf390); `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED` drained.

**The class NAME is wrong about its own law, and one extra grid round is what caught it.** "Only string, enum, number or date allowed" reads as a closed list, and a family built from it would have refused a `TimeType`, a `DateTimeType`, a `DateFragmentType` and an `EnumerationType` — four shapes A12 refuses under the NEXT gate's class, which is a wrong class on a correct rejection. The rows cost one run; the defect would have been invisible to every guard, since the model is refused either way and only the code differs.

**The prediction was exact on the six rows that were predicted.** Two text fields, two numbers, two dates alike, a date range, the base-type neighbour and the raw-type neighbour all landed first try; the four kind rows were left as `?` precisely because the class name was not evidence about them. That is the split worth repeating — predict what the SOURCE settles, measure what only the kernel can.

**Slice selection cost two commands.** `code-owner` over `CheckEntityListenUtils` returned the open set, and the per-code mode then showed that the neighbouring `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED` was already reported while this one was not — a distinction one letter apart in the code name and invisible to a grep.

**One code of the pair is deferred, and the reason is a library gap rather than a measurement.** `MVK_NO_BOOLY_ALLOWED` sits on `FirstFilledValue` and needs a Confirm or Boolean operand, which `DocumentModelDraft` cannot author (DEV-API-GAPS DG55). Closing DG55 unblocks it, and that is its own slice rather than raw DM-JSON smuggled into a laws fixture.

### C record — an EXTENSION rather than a family, and the instrument that decided that (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the date-aggregate base-type gate.** [KF389](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf389); `MVK_DATE_AND_NONDATE` drained into the existing `TEMPORAL_AGGREGATE_OPERANDS`, whose KDoc had carried it as a deliberate silence — "which the builders pre-check". No new family, so the count stays at 76.

**The silence was justified by the wrong fact, and the ledger's own semantics say why.** `pre-checked` classifies the TYPED LIBRARY surface; whether the portable checker reports a code is a third, orthogonal fact. A stored DM-JSON model carries whatever it carries regardless of what a builder would have refused, so "the builders pre-check it" is never on its own a reason for the checker to stay quiet. Any other family's silence resting on that phrase is worth re-reading.

**The slice was chosen and sized by `scripts/typescript/code-owner.ts` rather than by grep**, which is the first slice to have been. Its file mode over `CheckEntityListenUtils` returned 15 open classes; three of them clustered on one method, and the per-code mode then showed that two of the three were already reported and only this one was not. That turned what looked like a three-code slice into a one-code extension of an existing family — the same judgement the previous slice got wrong by hand, reached in two commands.

**The instrument had to be corrected twice first, and both corrections are the same defect one layer down.** Its source scan could not see a code reached through a file-local `finding(…)` helper, nor one parked in an enum constructor and read as `law.code`. The first gap was real and shared with `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest`, which had been green while fourteen reported codes carried rows that did not say so; the second is unfixable by scanning, so file mode now reads coverage from the guard-locked ledger instead. **A tool that under-reports coverage is the expensive direction here** — it sends a session to redo finished work, which is exactly what the count in this plan's stage-5 row was doing.

**One over-rejection was avoided by a check worth repeating.** `declaredTemporalFormatOf` returns null both for a non-temporal field and for a temporal one declaring no format, and a `DateTimeType` may legitimately declare none. Refusing on that null would have rejected a model A12 admits, so the gate refuses only on an allow-list of types it is certain about — `CustomFieldType`, `TypeDefType`, `UnspecifiedType` and `DateRangeType` are all left alone.

### C record — the seventy-sixth family: the decider was the CALLER, and the prior art was one carrier wide (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the duplicate and root-group gates an entity list meets before its operator asks anything.** [KF388](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf388); `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`, `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` and `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED` now reported on the field and value lists, which is the carrier boundary the ledger had carried as unaudited since 2026-09-13 and the group-list families' own KDoc named as out of scope.

**The prior art answered a narrower question than the one asked, which is the failure mode that terminates an enquiry.** `GroupListOperands` and `GROUP_LIST_ROOT_OPERAND` already reported all four classes — on six GROUP-LIST carriers. A grep for the codes returns them confidently and on topic, and reads as covered. What made the difference was running the module lane against the new family before writing anything else: nine of its tests went red at once, which is a far cheaper discovery than a review finding a duplicated family. The partition that followed is the one the existing KDoc already prescribed, so the fix was to narrow the new table rather than to widen the old one.

**A gate whose class depends on the CALLER is measured by moving the operator and holding the list fixed.** That is the accelerator's caller-decides variant, and it paid: 22 operators × 9 list shapes settled the whole table in six runs, including three rows no source read would have produced — `GroupFilled` admitting a lone root group, `NumberOfFilledGroups` answering with its arity class instead, and `RepetitionNotUnique` asking the duplicate gates ahead of its own key-list gates.

**A class that looks dead from every one-root fixture was reachable, and only a second ROOT GROUP shows it.** Every entity of a model is under its root group, so a list naming the root beside anything else is the nested-duplicate class — which makes `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS` unreachable on any ordinary fixture and not unreachable at all. The cost of finding out was one added `rootGroup` call and one run; the cost of writing "unreachable" would have been a published claim that a later fixture falsifies.

**Three families now share one reader instead of restating it.** `bareReferenceField` split into `isBareEntitySpec` and `addressedTargetOf`, and `EntityListArity` and `ValueInFieldsConstantKind` both cite the pre-gate predicate rather than carrying their own copy. That found a real defect in passing: the arity family's copy did not exempt a STARRED duplicate, so it stayed silent on `(Lines*/Amount, Lines*/Amount)` where A12 answers with the arity class — the shared reader reports it, and the grid had already measured the row.

### C record — the seventy-fifth family, and `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` DRAINED (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a pattern comparison demands of its two sides.** [KF387](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf387); four residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`, `MVK_MISSING_CONST_IN_PATTERN_COMP`, `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN` on its condition locus. **All thirteen reachable codes of the estate's largest check method are now reported, across six families.**

**The construct took six slices, and the ORDER of those slices was the whole method.** The operator gates first, because they answer ahead of everything and every later family has to know where they stop; then the four type-agreement arms in A12's own sequence — boolean, text-and-enumeration, number, temporal — each measuring its predecessors as pre-emptions rather than assuming them; the pattern arm last, being the one that RETURNS and so contends with nothing. A slice ordered any other way would have had to re-measure the same precedence from the far side each time.

**The pattern gate came for free because a predicate already existed.** `kernelPatternBlacklist` in `:predicates` is the reader [declaredPatternFindings] uses for a DECLARED pattern, and the condition locus needs exactly it under a different code. One import, one call — which is what the extraction to `:predicates` was for, and the first time a second consumer has arrived without new work.

**A 15-row grid cost one wrong prediction: an EMPTY pattern is admitted.** It compiles and spells no blacklisted adjacency, so the last question takes it. Worth the row because an empty regex reads as the obvious refusal and a checker built from the source alone would have guessed.

**The corrective table's reachability fixture grew twice in two slices, and that is the shape to expect.** A family that reports a code A12's owner explains needs a declaration in `CorrectiveTest` that draws it, and the guard names the code the moment the projection is regenerated — so writing the fixture row in the same pass as the family costs nothing and discovering it costs a red gate run.

### C record — the seventy-fourth family: the module lane caught what the family's own grid could not (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a date or date-range side of a comparison demands of its partner.** [KF386](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf386); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`, `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE_RANGE`. **`CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` is now drained but for its pattern arm**: eleven of thirteen codes, across five families.

**The co-firing defect is the case for running the module lane before the mirror, and it is the second time it has paid.** The family judged every comparison operator, so a TOLERANCE comparison with a date operand drew the date class ALONGSIDE the tolerance class A12 actually answers with. Nothing in this family's own 20-row grid could see it — a tolerance row reads as another family's business — and `ComparisonOperatorOperandsTest`, written for a different slice on a fixture built for other reasons, went red on the pair. The fix widened the grid by three measured rows (a date ordered against a number is the date class, a date ordered against a text is the operator gate's, a date in a tolerance is the tolerance arm's) and narrowed the family to the six operators that reach the walk.

**A written-out value is left UNJUDGED, and that is not conservatism but the forbidden direction.** A12 types a date-shaped string CONSTANT as a date, so a reader treating `"01.01.2020"` as text would refuse `[Start] == "01.01.2020"`, which A12 admits. Requiring both sides to be declarations is what makes the arm safe rather than what makes it small.

**Two branches in these two methods are written and cannot fire**, recorded because a checker that reproduced them would name a class on a correct rejection: the range arm's ordering refusal (a range is not orderable, so the operator gate answers) and the base-year branches of the date arm (BASE_YEAR is not a declaration). The first was measured, not read.

**The corrective table's standing exemption is gone.** `CorrectiveTest` had carried `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` as "the code the checker only compares against"; the number-scale family now reports it, so the fixture draws it and the expectation is the empty set. An exemption that becomes stale is worse than none: it reads as a measured limit long after the limit has lifted.

### C record — the seventy-third family, and a disposition that had been wrong since it was written (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what equality demands of two numbers' declared scales, and what a number may stand against at all.** [KF385](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf385); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` and `MVK_INCONSISTENT_TYPES_COMPARED`. Four of `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`'s thirteen codes remain, all of them the date, date-range and pattern arms.

**Two of five predictions were wrong, and the grid is the only reason that is a footnote rather than a shipped family.** The source read said "equality of declared scale", so `[UnitPrice] == 1` was predicted refused; it is ADMITTED, because a written-out number carries the multiplicative-constant capability the gate waives on, and the pair passes exactly when the SMALLER-scale side is the capable one. The ledger row for the code had said so for months, in the kit's language rather than the kernel's, and reading it first would have cost nothing. **A slice's own ledger row is prior art about the law, not only about the disposition.**

**The second wrong prediction was a fixture, not a law**: the computation probe read the field it computed, so the suppression row measured `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` instead of the gate. Worth naming because a suppression row cannot be read from the verdict alone — an ADMITTED that comes from the wrong refusal being absent looks identical to the suppression working.

**A disposition row was wrong, and landing this family is what exposed it.** `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` sat in `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` under a comment saying rulekit "names these only to EXPLAIN a kernel verdict … never to refuse anything itself". `NumberExpr.requireCompatibleScale` refuses on it, naming the code in the message. The row is now `SAME_LAW_SEPARATE_CARRIERS`. The guard did its job — it recorded that a decision was made — and the decision was simply wrong; what caught it was needing the row to be right for a new reason.

**A shared reader was short in a way nothing had noticed.** `isNumberLiteral` capped at three tokens, so a NEGATIVE decimal (`-1.00`, four tokens after the tokenizer's digit-run terminal) read as no number at all in three families. It is now shape-based and returns the scale, which this family needs anyway — one function, one correct definition, three consumers.

### C record — the seventy-second family: two questions in one kernel method that never contend (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a text or enumeration side of a comparison needs of its partner, and which written-out values an enumeration admits.** [KF384](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf384); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_ENUM_OR_STRING` (also closing an entry on the ledger's *still unaudited* list) and `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON`. Six of `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`'s thirteen codes remain.

**Half a kernel method is a legitimate partition when the halves are DISJOINT, and that has to be shown rather than asserted.** `checkStringOrEnumType` carries four classes; this family reports two and leaves the display-value pair to A12. The justification is structural, not budgetary: the kind question fires only where the partner is a NUMBER, the display questions only where both sides are text-or-enum, and a written-out value opposite an enumeration takes the domain branch and returns before the display branch beside it. All three claims are measured on a display-bearing enumeration — the carrier that would have collapsed them had they contended.

**The `else if` was the whole argument, and reading it is what made the partition safe.** Had the display check run *after* the domain check rather than as its alternative, every constant row would have needed the display machinery to be right, and the slice would have been four codes or none.

**It also closed a documented under-report in a neighbouring family.** [scalarValueListGateFindings] (then named `enumConstantDomainFindings`) states that a COMPARISON answers with `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON` and that it does not report it, "because each needs an operand shape this reader does not reduce". The comparison reader built two slices ago is that shape; the note stays, now naming where the carrier went.

**A third reader collapsed into a shared home**, `declaredEnumValues` in `EnumCategories.kt` — three private copies in two shapes (ordered and set-valued), with the same null-on-unreadable contract stated three times. The ordered one won and the set consumers call `toSet()`.

**A test-side defect worth naming, and the guard that already owns it.** The first grid used the same row label twice in one `LinkedHashMap`, so the second measurement silently overwrote the first on BOTH sides and the assertion passed over a denominator short by one. It was caught by eye and fixed before any gate ran — which is why `GridRowKeyGuardTest`, which owns exactly this class in `leakGuardTest`, never saw it. Re-proved by mutation on this slice's own grid: it fires. The lesson is not a missing instrument but the order of the loop — the targeted `-Pfast` runs a slice is written against skip that lane, so a collapsed grid can steer a whole family's implementation before anything says so. Read the row count off the result XML when a grid is the thing being written.

### C record — the seventy-first family, and a published grid that varied one axis too few (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a confirm or boolean declaration may be compared to, and with which equality operator.** [KF383](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf383); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YES`, `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YESNO`. The first partition of `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` after the operator gates; eight of its thirteen codes remain.

**The grid found a defect in two entries this estate had already published.** [KF270](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf270) reported that a confirm "refuses every field on the right, including another CONFIRM", and [KF9](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf9) that it "compares only to `True` or another Confirm" — the first too strong, the second silent on the restriction. Both are right about `==` and wrong about `!=`, which A12 admits, and neither grid had varied the operator inside that arm. The kernel source carries the answer in plain sight: the two-confirm refusal is its own `throw` guarded on `eq.ne == null`, separate from the comparability test. **The confound is the one no control inside a set can catch** — every row of KF270's equality arm was individually clean and the whole arm held `==` fixed — and it was found only because a new family had to decide what to report for `!=`.

**A checker must not inherit an ERASURE, and this is the sharpest case yet.** The Java handles erase the whole confirm-versus-confirm comparison: `ConfirmRef` offers `confirmed`/`notConfirmed` and nothing else, so `[A] != [B]` between two confirms is unauthorable through the typed surface — and A12 accepts it. A checker that mirrored the handle's shape would refuse a legal model, which is the one direction this project forbids; the disposition row in `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` records that separation rather than the usual carrier split alone.

**The slice also widened the sixty-ninth family, on a measurement it had never taken.** `True` and `False` are the constants an author actually writes beside a boolean field, and A12 spells them as bare NAMES rather than as literals — so [comparisonOperatorOperandFindings]'s reader, which recognised only literals, left them outside all three operator gates. Four measured rows closed it ([KF382](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf382), extended). The general shape is worth naming: **a reader's constant vocabulary is a denominator, and a family that never wrote the missing member cannot see the hole.**

**Three helpers collapsed into shared homes in the same pass** — `bareReferenceField` (one entity spec, resolved, with the unaddressed-repetition pre-emption the corpus mutation found in the arity gates) and `isNumberLiteral`, both in `Reference.kt`, now serve three families that each carried their own copy. The pre-emption reaching the comparison family for free is the point: it was a defect there too, unmeasured, and sharing the reader closed it without a second corpus run.

### C record — the seventieth family, and the reader that unlocks the estate's largest construct (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what each comparison OPERATOR demands of the two sides it joins.** [KF382](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf382); three residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_CONST`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_TOLERANCE_OP`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`.

**The foundational half is `comparisonsIn`, and the gap it closed had been invisible because nothing failed.** This checker read CALLS, and a comparison is infix — A12's `vergleichsBedingung` — so the syntax module had no structure to hand a family, and `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` sat at the top of the ranking untouched with thirteen reachable codes, twice the next construct. The shape parser had been reading comparisons correctly all along in order to validate them; it simply never recorded one. Twenty lines in `ShapeParser.conditionPart` and a `ComparisonShape` beside `CallShape` is the whole reader.

**Both sides are recorded as TEXT, not reduced, and that is the load-bearing design decision.** A comparison law asks first what each side IS — a bare reference, a literal, arithmetic, a nested call — and a reduction that answered "a path" for the first and null for the rest would erase exactly the distinction those laws turn on. The first consumer proves it: the family types only a bare reference or a literal, and a side is a bare reference only when EVERY token belongs to one entity spec. An earlier draft reduced by "it names exactly one entity" and duly typed `{[Qty] + 1}` from the `Qty` inside it — a type claim about a result A12 computes and this reader does not.

**Three of the thirteen are drained; the other ten are their own partitions** (boolean agreement, string/enum agreement, the pattern arm, the decimal-places gate, the date and date-range arms). The slice stopped at three because the reader was the foundational part and a thirteen-code denominator is the shape the convergence gate exists to refuse.

**A NEW code costs three guard rows, and finding that out from the guard lane costs a rerun each time.** Measured over three slices: a code the checker has never reported before trips `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest` (its MVK-LEDGER disposition cell needs `checker-reported`), `CorrectiveTest`'s reachability assertion (the generated corrective table gains an entry, and the `reachability` fixture must carry a declaration that draws it), and — where the code also appears in Java main source — `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` (both estates state it, so the DISPOSITIONS map needs a classified row). None of the three depends on anything the lane computes, so do all three at family-writing time: list the codes the family newly emits, then grep the ledger cell, the corrective fixture and the Java main source for each. Three greps against three full lane runs.

**The verification order was also re-cut this session, and the reason is measured.** `:adapter:checkerSilenceGuardTest` — the corpus-mutation guard — now runs with the module lane straight after a family compiles, rather than last. It is the only instrument that generates shapes nobody thought to write, it caught a wrong-class defect in the sixty-ninth family, and it caught it AFTER all that family's documentation had been written. Cost of moving it: nothing; it was going to run anyway.

### C record — the sixty-ninth family: the accelerator pointed at a UTILITY instead of a construct (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: how many entities each entity-list operator's argument list needs.** [KF381](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf381); six residue codes drained — the whole `MVK_PARAMSIZE_*` set, across twenty-two operators and five construct classes.

**Every slice so far transcribed one CONSTRUCT's method. This one transcribed a UTILITY, and the enumeration is what made that safe.** Six arity gates live in `CheckEntityListenUtils`, and the variable is not the construct but the operator: thirteen call sites, `grep`-able in one command, each naming which gate one operator's switch reaches. The rule the previous slices established still holds — you transcribe the thing that decides the class — but the thing that decides it here is the CALLER, so the transcription is the caller table.

**Two predicates, and the difference is exactly where a rule of thumb would go wrong.** `checkIsMoreThanOneEntity` refuses only a lone FIELD, so `AllFieldsFilled(SomeGroup)` is admitted; `isMoreThanOneParameter` refuses a lone entity of any kind, so `AllGroupsFilled(SomeGroup)` is not. A family that had read all six as "needs at least two" would have refused a legal model on the first of those — the over-rejection this project forbids — and nothing in the six method names says so.

**Two classes differ only in an underscore.** `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID2` is the group conditions' "more than one parameter"; `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID_2` is `SumOfProducts`' "exactly two". Each is measured on its own carrier, never inferred from the other, because a transposition here is invisible to every guard.

**Two wrong-class defects were caught by the estate rather than by reading, and they were caught by DIFFERENT instruments.** The first implementation reported an arity class on lists A12 answers with `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`/`MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`; the existing `GroupListOperandsTest` caught it as a co-fire within a minute, which is the mirror suite working as a CROSS-family gate rather than a per-family one — worth remembering when deciding whether a row belonging to another family's carrier earns its place.

The second is the one no amount of source reading would have found, because the shape does not look like an arity question: an operand crossing an unstarred repetition (`Sum(Lines/Amount)`) is one entity, is a field, and carries no star — and A12 never asks its arity, the reference reader having refused it while the list was built. `:adapter:checkerSilenceGuardTest` found it by MUTATING five real corpus models (remove a star from a condition) and comparing class for class. **A synthetic grid cannot produce that row, because writing it requires already suspecting it.** The corpus-mutation guard is the estate's only instrument that generates the shapes nobody thought to write, and this is the second family it has corrected.

### C record — the sixty-eighth family, and the bucket that turned out to be the larger reservoir (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a value-list quantifier's field list may carry, and what may stand opposite it.** [KF380](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf380); three residue codes drained — `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`, `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON`, all of `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl.check`.

**The selection instrument was wrong about which pool to rank over, and correcting it changed the plan's remaining size.** Ranking had been over `kernel-delegated` codes alone. But the codes this family drains are `erased` — the ledger's word for "the typed builders cannot author it" — and an erased code is precisely the one a checker over STORED DM-JSON is for: the builders never emit the shape, so nothing but a checker ever meets it. Counted on 2026-09-14: **50 purely delegated codes remain unreported and 54 erased ones do**, with 8 erased codes already reported, so the route was live and simply unranked. The pool the instrument now ranks over is both buckets minus the rows marked unreachable from stored DM-JSON — 110 codes.

**Re-ranked that way, the estate's shape is different.** `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` — the comparison itself, the most common construct in any model — carries **13** reachable codes, twice the next; `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl` 10, `CheckFeldListenBedingungImpl` 9. The comparison is not yet takeable, and the reason is worth writing down as the next foundational investment rather than as a blocker: this checker reads CALLS, and a comparison is infix, so the syntax module has no structure to hand a family. One `comparisonsIn` reader unlocks a construct with thirteen codes and several partitions.

**One shared reader landed instead, and it is the smaller sibling of that idea.** Two operator families now put an infix `In` between two comma-separated lists, and the keyword sits INSIDE one operand because only a comma splits operands. `ConditionCall.splitAtIn()` is that scan, written once; the sixty-seventh family was refactored onto it in the same change rather than left holding a private copy.

### C record — the sixty-seventh family: one method, one switch, four classes (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: which field declarations each constant KIND of `NumberOfValueInFields` admits.** [KF379](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf379); four residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_TYPEYESNO`, `MVK_NO_TYPE_YES_OR_YESNO`, `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM`, `MVK_NO_NUMBER`, all of `CheckAnzahlWertOpImpl.check`.

**This is the accelerator at its cleanest, and the throw-site grouping is what found it.** The selection instrument introduced with the sixty-sixth family — group the remaining delegated codes by the kernel FILE that raises them — put `CheckEntityListenUtils` at the top with sixteen codes, and that file is a utility rather than a construct. Following its CALLERS instead named a 137-line construct class whose single method draws seven of those sixteen. One method, one grid, four classes: the largest slice of this plan so far, and it cost one measurement pass because every row of the grid is the same call with one variable moved.

**Every prediction in the grid was right on the first run, which is the accelerator's actual payoff.** Transcribing the method rather than the codes means the arms, their order, and the gates ahead of them all arrive together; nothing had to be discovered by a failed assertion. The work that remained was not finding the law but bounding it.

**Two codes of the construct were measured UNREACHABLE, and that is a result rather than a gap.** `MVK_DIFFERENT_YES_VALUE`/`MVK_DIFFERENT_NO_VALUE` compare a Boolean declaration's stored true/false representation, and the conversion feeding every consistency check replaces it with the literals — so a mismatch is admitted, and a family reporting the class would refuse models A12 accepts. The row that would normally settle it does not exist: the model reader ignores an unknown property, so a misspelled-key control is admitted exactly as the correct key is, and the mechanism had to be carried by a source chain instead. **Recording WHY no control is available is the transferable part** — a uniform column with no control behind it is indistinguishable from apparatus that never arrived.

**Four gates run ahead of the switch and each answers with its own class**, so the family refuses to judge a list that trips one. This is the same discipline as the previous three slices and the reason none of them over-reports: the risk in a construct-shaped family is never the class it names, it is the class it names on a model some earlier gate already refused.

### C record — the sixty-sixth family: the arithmetic the typed builders cannot author (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: how many divisions and powers one arithmetic expression may carry, and what an exponent has to be.** [KF378](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf378); three residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_DIV_FOR_CALC`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_POW_FOR_CALC`, all three of `CheckOperationImpl.check`.

**The selection instrument changed, and that is the transferable part.** The lock-count ranking that picked the last four families is now FLAT: every one of the 68 remaining purely-delegated codes cites exactly one lock class, so sorting by that count says nothing. What replaced it is a group-by over the kernel's own throw sites — for each remaining code, which file raises it — which immediately named `CheckEntityListenUtils` (15 codes), `CheckEntityUtils` (7) and then a tail of four-code CONSTRUCT files. That is the accelerator's own principle turned into a query: pick the method, not the code.

**These three are the clearest case yet for the checker existing at all.** `DslRenderer` braces a left power child and a left division under division, so the typed builders cannot emit any refused shape — the codes are reachable only from stored DM-JSON, which is exactly the input this checker reads and the builders never see.

**The measurement cost more than the implementation, and the reason is worth recording: the scale gate masks the whole construct.** A power or a division leaves an operation's scale undeterminable, so `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` answered every row until each was wrapped in `RoundDown(…, 2)` into a scale-2 target. Two false starts first — a hand-written model the kernel could not build at all, and a bare `BaseFee` where the grammar wants `[BaseFee]` — both of which read as a verdict about the construct.

**One row was dropped rather than asserted**, and the family is silent where it would have gone: an exponent field declaring no `maxFractionalDigits` at all. The kernel source refuses a null scale as plainly as a positive one, but the estate's fixtures cannot author such a field and a splice attempt did not resolve, so the claim stayed a source read and the checker stays silent on the shape.

### C record — a THIRD branch for an existing family, and the code that lives in two phases (2026-09-14)

**Frozen scope: the semantic-index branch of `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkIndicesInFilterCondition`, added to `FILTER_BODY_GATES`.** [KF377](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf377); one residue code drained — `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NOT_ALLOWED`. No new family: the class belongs to a method this estate had already transcribed, and the transcription had two of its three branches.

**The code had been filed under the wrong PHASE, and the ledger's own wording is what hid it.** It sits in `ParserService.checkNormalRule`, so the post-parse set took it on the strength of that one site. It has three, and the other two throw during the parse; every measured shape reaches the filter walk. The discriminating control is a rule violating the filtered-entity gate as well — a whole-rule gate checked two branches earlier, which loses anyway. **The general lesson for a set keyed by code: grep the class across the parser and read EVERY site, because a class is not owned by the phase that happens to name it.**

**Three under-reports taken deliberately, all in the same direction.** An entity-keyed index in a filter is left to the index's own spec gates; a filtered operand riding a parallel level is left alone by both families; and the filter walk's reader still refuses to judge an operand that crosses an unstarred repetition, which is where the second of those comes from. Each is a silence where A12 rejects, which the estate tolerates — what it will not tolerate is the class that was live instead.

**The slice also bounded a clause the estate had been applying too widely.** `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY`'s iteration gate is drawn against the RULE's error target; inside a filter the anchoring is the filtered entity, and two measured shapes there are answered by other gates entirely. It had been naming its class on both. That is the third time this month a gate measured on one carrier was applied to a second without measurement, and the tell was the same: no new branch was needed, so nothing prompted the question.

**Where the convergence gate bit, and what it bought.** The first implementation reported on every index in a filter; the mirror then showed two classes where A12 gives one, on two rows, and fixing that exposed the neighbouring family's over-wide gate. Partitioning at that point — report only the literal-keyed shape, leave the rest silent and say so — closed the slice with one measured claim per row instead of a third round.

### C record — the sixty-fifth family: a gate whose marked operands are not the ones it names (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: that no `Having` filter hangs off an operand A12 marked as parallel-iterated.** [KF376](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf376); one residue code drained — `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_FOR_FILTERED_ENTITY_NOT_ALLOWED`, the ranking's top row, and the second of `ParserService.checkNormalRule`'s seven `throwError` calls to get a reporting family.

**The gate's name is the wrong model of it, and one row is what said so.** `hasParallelIteratedFilterConditionId` reads as "the filtered operand reads a parallel-iterated group", and a family built on that reading passes every row but one: the marker is set by TWO passes, and the second marks the rule's OWN keyed group on every reference once any other operand parallel-iterates. The discriminator was written before the measurement precisely because the natural reading and the source reading differed — two conditions with byte-identical filtered operands, differing in a conjunct that names neither — and the kernel took the wider side.

**The family it neighbours had the same defect and was silent about it.** `PARALLEL_ITERATION_UNIQUENESS` excluded any reference carrying a star, where A12 marks per LEVEL: an aggregate over a keyed group with a star on a group beneath it still iterates jointly, and the rule is refused. One kernel row settled it and the fix is three lines. That under-report had been live since the family landed, invisible because a silence on a legal-looking condition is what a correct family also produces.

**One smell fixed on the way, and it is the reason it was fixable at all.** Promoting two helpers to `internal` collided with private copies in two other families — `OUTER_MARKER` and `indexFieldNameOf`, each stated three times byte-identically. They now live once, in `Reference.kt` beside the other cross-family readers. A duplicated helper is cheap until a third consumer needs it to change.

**A silence the report carries on BOTH sides of a boundary, now disclosed.** `OPERAND_REPETITION_SCOPE` does not judge a filter-carrying text at all — a filter body's references are bound to the filtered entity rather than the rule's target and the reader sees one flat list — so the operand's own levels go unjudged too. The measured case is an unkeyed parallel level under a filtered operand: A12 draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` during the parse and nothing in the report names it. The KDoc said nothing about the skip until this slice; an under-report nobody wrote down is indistinguishable from a partition.

### C record — the sixty-fourth family: the top of the ranking, and a gate that was pure transcription (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the two gates an entity-list aggregate asks of each temporal operand, its PRECISION and its COMPONENTS.** [KF193](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf193), amended with the gate's own mechanism, and [KF260](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf260). Two residue codes drained — `MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE`, which the ranking put at the top with three lock classes against it, more than any other delegated row, and `MVK_PARTIAL_DATE_NOT_ALLOWED`, which cost a second expectation in the same walk because A12 asks both of one operand before moving to the next.

**The second code is the argument for transcribing a whole METHOD rather than a code.** It was a separate delegated row with its own lock and would have been selected on its own merits eventually; landing it here cost one branch, because the loop that answers it is the loop already written. So when the accelerator says to transcribe the `Check…Impl` for a CONSTRUCT, the pay-off is not only that the chain's order comes out right — it is that every class the chain draws arrives in one slice.

**Nineteen mirror rows, green on the first run, and that is the ranking rule paying off rather than luck.** The kernel method is forty lines and every branch of it is decidable from stored DM-JSON: the component set is plain containment of six letters in the declared format string, the base year is a model-level presence, and the operand list is the expansion A12 already flattens. Nothing needed a grid, because three law classes had already measured the cells — including the two confounds this construct has cost the estate before, kind-versus-declaration and format-string-versus-component-set.

**The one cell that did not exist was the one the previous carrier-scope clause left open.** It named the admitted group operand and stopped, so "a group whose expansion DISAGREES" was a prediction the reader would have had to make. One kernel row settled it in twenty seconds, and the answer went into the finding rather than into the reproduction's comment.

**The row that was delegated says the opposite of what it meant, and that is worth naming.** It read "deliberately NOT pre-checked: this is exactly where a pre-check would over-reject" — true of the Java builders, which hold operand KINDS and provably cannot see a declared format, and false of a checker that reads the stored declaration. A disposition written for one estate had been sitting as if it governed both. The ranking surfaces rows like that on their evidence rather than on their prose, which is a second reason to run it rather than read the ledger front to back.

**Four new leak-guard dispositions, all PROSE_ONLY and three of them the MIRROR direction** — the kits enforce `MVK_DATE_AND_NONDATE`, `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` and `MVK_NO_NUMBER` while this family only names them, in the KDoc table saying which gate answers ahead of the one it reports. Naming the neighbouring gates is what makes a silence locatable, and it costs a disposition row each time.

### C record — the sixty-third family: three containment classes, and the shipped over-report they exposed (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: where a `RepetitionNotUnique` KEY may sit relative to the scope that bounds it.** [KF375](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf375) carries the phase law and [KF355](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf355) was amended with the chain mechanism; three residue codes drained — `MVK_NOT_IN_GROUP`, `MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH`, `MVK_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP`. The previous record predicted this family by name: the ranking pairs two codes citing the same locks, and this construct's pair was already sitting at the top of it.

**The find was not the family, it was a defect already shipped in a different one.** `PARALLEL_ITERATION_UNIQUENESS` co-fired with every new containment class, and the tempting fix — teach that family to stay silent here — would have been the first of a series. A12 checks a condition in two phases, and the second runs only on a parse that completed, so a parse-time class removes the whole-rule gate's input rather than outranking it. The fix is therefore one rule over the finished finding list, not a condition inside any family, and the set it applies to is derivable from kernel source: a second-phase gate takes a built `ICondition` rather than a parser context.

**Amended 2026-09-14: the fix was itself too narrow, and transcribing the METHOD is what showed it.** The post-parse set held one class while `ParserService.checkNormalRule` draws seven, three of which this checker already reports — so the same over-report was live on two more shapes and was measured on one of them. Worse, the estate held the identical law TWICE: a `withoutRelationsBehindAPositionedDefect` written months earlier, byte-identical but for its code set, because the two halves were discovered separately and neither session read the other's. One function and one derived set now, and the set's members are read off the kernel method rather than off the shapes that happened to expose them. **The lesson generalizes past this fix: a suppression list assembled from incidents is a list of incidents, and the way to size it is to go and read what the phase actually draws.**

**A suppression needs a positive control or it is indistinguishable from a family that stopped working.** The control is within-model: the same index-keyed pair with a CLEAN key list beside a parallel read reports `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_NOT_ALLOWED` on both estates. Without that row the new silence would have passed as correct on a checker that had simply lost the class.

**The hour this cost went to a wrong catalog id** — `"From"` for a clause whose id is `"FromScope"` — which made the whole family return an empty list at its first elvis. That failure mode has no signal at all: a family that reports nothing looks exactly like a family correctly silent on every row, and three of the mirror's rows legitimately expect silence. What found it was the positive control at the end of the table, which is the same instrument that caught the vacuous JSON one family earlier.

**One row was deleted rather than asserted.** A chain control — two keys whose repetitions nest — was written with a predicted expectation, and the kernel answered `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`. A prediction in a lock is a claim nobody measured, so the row went rather than being back-filled to whatever ran.

### C record — the sixty-second family: a construct already measured to exhaustion, and what reusing that grid costs (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: which repeatable levels a `Having` filter's body may name, and the one it must.** [KF374](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf374) carries it. Two residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` and `MVK_INVALID_ITERATION_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`, both of which had sat delegated while three separate laws tests measured them across some thirty cells.

**The cheapest family so far, and the reason is worth naming: the measurement already existed and nobody had noticed.** Three law classes had been built for peer exchanges about this construct and none of them had ever been asked whether a checker could report what they measured. The selection rule that found it is not "which code is next in the residue" but "which residue code has the most measurement already sitting against it" — a question the ledger answers directly, since a delegated row that cites three lock classes is a family waiting to be written.

**That ranking is mechanical, so run it rather than reading the ledger front to back.** Take every `| kernel-delegated |` row, extract the `…LawsTest` / `…DiffTest` / `…GuardTest` class names it cites, and sort by how many distinct ones each has. Measured 2026-09-14: 78 delegated rows, 38 of them citing at least one lock, and the top of that list is where a family costs a transcription and a mirror rather than a grid. It also names the pairs — two codes citing the same two classes are usually one construct, which is how `MVK_REPEATABLE_GROUP_MISSING` and `MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH` surface together.

**Reusing a grid needs its constants re-derived, and this time that was done first.** One new cell was measured before the family landed: every in-scope row in the borrowed grid was a CONJUNCT beside a failing reference, so "an in-scope reference clears the scope gate" and "an in-scope reference clears it when something else has already failed" were the same rule across all of it. The sole in-scope row is admitted, as predicted — but predicted is not measured, and the previous record's `positivesOnly` cell is what that sentence cost last time.

**Three silences, each locating another family's gate rather than declaring the shape safe.** A star in the body is `FILTER_WILDCARD`'s; an unaddressed repetition in the OPERAND is the reference reader's and answers first; and the `$` marker is invisible to this reader, because the shared reference scan normalizes it away — which is right for binding and wrong for a walk that must skip marked references exactly. The third is the one that would have produced a WRONG class rather than a missing one.

### C record — the sixty-first family: a construct whose SLOTS are the chain, and twenty predictions that all held (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what each slot of a `Date`/`Time` construction admits, on all three of its arms.** [KF373](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf373) carries it. Four residue codes drained. The ENTITY arm landed in the same slice once its measurement turned out to exist already — `TemporalComponentDeclarationLawsTest` pins the number widths, the flexible year maxima, the scale/sign/near-miss refusals, all six recognised pattern spellings and the exact-`yyyy` date exception — so the arm needed no new grid, only one new cell. A `TypeDefType` component stays delegated, its body not always visible to the reader.

**Reusing a measured grid is a claim, and the claim had a hole exactly where the grid had a constant.** Every row of that older grid declared a `minValue`, so "the sign gate is a declared non-negative minimum" and "the sign gate is that OR the `positivesOnly` flag" were the same rule across all of it — and the kernel's own comment beside the flag says the data modeller cannot set it, which reads as a reason not to look. The flag is declarable in DM-JSON, the source takes it as a second satisfier, and the reader built on the grid alone would have REFUSED a component A12 admits. Two rows and one 29-second run settled it before the family shipped; the general form is that a borrowed grid's constants are the borrower's to re-derive, never the lender's to have covered.

**The accelerator's cleanest run so far: twenty cells, twenty correct predictions, one build.** Two subclasses supply the per-slot bounds and the expected operator, and the base class supplies the gates — so the whole law is a table read straight out of two files. Where the previous family needed four new measurements to find one surprise, this one needed none, and the reason is worth keeping: the constant bounds are LITERALS in the kernel source, not behaviour.

**A third arm means a new way to be wrong: two families answering one declaration.** The constant arm reads no reference and can collide with nothing; the entity arm reads one, and A12's reference reader runs ahead of every operator gate, so a component that is also an unstarred cross-repetition read draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` and the slot gate never runs. Both families would have reported. Nothing in the suite would have gone red — the mirror's own fixture is flat, so the collision was unreachable from every row the family already had — and the differential lane compares classes A12 never draws rather than counting them. The lesson is the reach one: an arm that starts reading REFERENCES inherits every precedence the reference reader has, and the question to ask of a new arm is which other family now shares its input.

**The one cell that could still have gone wrong, and why it was put in anyway.** `QuarterFromDate` is a date extract operator that no date slot expects. The natural rule — "one's own family misplaced, the other family barred" — predicts the wrong-slot class for it; the source lists the three slot operators by name, which predicts the barred class. Reading the switch settles it, but a family built on the natural rule would have named the wrong class and no other row in the grid would have caught it.

**The vacuity trap from the fifty-ninth record recurred, in the same shape, and the positive control caught it in one run.** Every constant probe carries quotes, and an unescaped quote inside the JSON `errorCondition` ends the string — so the three constant tables measured nothing while the operator table, which has no quotes, passed. A silent-row table is where this hides, which is why the leave-alone table now ends with a row that MUST report. Escaping in the fixture helper rather than in each probe is what keeps the next row from reintroducing it.

### C record — the sixtieth family: five classes of one WALK, and the block chosen by construct rather than by code (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: which repetitions an operand may cross unstarred.** `CheckEntityUtils.checkIndicesForA12Model` is one method, and the five classes it can draw — `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, `MVK_MULTIPLE_INDEX_FIELDS`, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NO_INDEX`, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX`, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX_TYPE` — sat in the residue as five separate ledger rows, three of them annotated as needing "full iteration-model analysis". They needed one walk. [KF371](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf371) carries it.

**The selection rule keeps paying, and this is its largest single return.** Five residue codes drained in one family, and `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` is among the most common authoring mistakes in the corpus. Ranking by CONSTRUCT rather than by code is what found it: ranked by code, these five sit in three different ledger neighbourhoods with three different dispositions, and nothing pairs them.

**Most of the oracle was already on the shelf, which is the second half of the selection rule.** `RuleOperandScopeLawsTest` and `ComputationOperandScopeLawsTest` had pinned the sibling cells on both carriers; `ParallelIterationGateLawsTest` had pinned the type cell. Four new cells finished the chain — the target end of the index demand, the per-level walk, the multiple-index gate, and a star with an unstarred level beneath it — and the four old ones were re-measured on ONE fixture, because a checker reads one model and a table mixing three cannot say whether a verdict belongs to the chain or to a fixture.

**The prediction that failed is the one worth keeping: the WALK outranks the gates.** Transcribing `checkParallelIteration` gives a gate order in which the operand's own index chain resolves before the target's, so a doubly-keyed operand should draw the multiple-index class whatever the target looks like. Measured, it draws `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NO_INDEX` into an unkeyed target, because the OUTER level is refused first and the inner one is never asked. The accelerator reads a chain inside one level; it says nothing about the loop around it, and that is where the fifteenth prediction in this run went wrong.

**Two existing mirrors went red on landing, and both were the family doing its job.** `ErrorFieldReferenceTest` had two rows asserting SILENCE where A12 reports `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` — its own javadoc said so — and `ParallelIterationUniquenessTest` had two more, one of which had been reasoned rather than measured. All four now carry A12's class. A silence justified by "another family owns this" becomes a wrong answer the moment that family arrives, so the mirror tier is what converts the plan's under-reports into reports.

**A THIRD family was corrected on the way, and the lesson is the standing one about constants.** Writing this family's own "target outside the declaring group" silence exposed that `ERROR_TARGET_ITERATION` had no such silence and named the parallel class there. The grid that measured those two classes ([KF348](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf348)) had already had two constants pulled out of it; a fourth was still constant — every row declared the rule in the error field's own group — and moving the rule alone makes both classes vanish, the containment gate answering during the parse ([KF372](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf372)). The control for that measurement had to change fixture, because on a repeatable ROOT every reference iterates at level 0 and no non-iterating reference exists to control with.
