**Its own silences, each resting on a gate that answers first.** A target outside the declaring group is `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP`, drawn at the top of the same method — and our `Rule.build()` refuses that pair while A12 admits it ([KF212](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf212)), so the checker must not inherit the refusal; the divergence guard now carries it as `STRICTER_IN_JAVA`. A star on a non-repeatable level, a GROUP operand, a `Having` filter, a semantic index and an `$`-marked outer reference are each another family's or another target's.

### C record — the fifty-ninth family: six classes of one chain, and the JSON that made three rows vacuous (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the semantic index's STRUCTURE.** [KF351](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf351) carries the chain; `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY` already owned its first gate, and this family owns the six below it that need no comparison of declared types. *(Extended 2026-09-14: `MVK_INDEX_TYPE_INCONSISTENT` and `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_CONTAINED_IN_INDEX` were held out here for want of a measured domain; [KF404](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf404) measured both and they joined this family, which also corrected where `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_BELOW_WILDCARD` sits in the chain.)*

**The measurement this needed was the ORDER, and it was the cheapest part.** Every single-defect row already existed; six cells putting two live gates in one index settled the descent, and all six predictions from the kernel source held. The gate order is the whole reason to implement these six together — three of them are about a star and differ only in where it sits.

**One class could not be decided index by index, and an existing mirror caught it.** A12 reports the first defect in a condition and nothing after, so a text with two semantic indices is not two questions: a group key in the first withholds the class the second would draw. The family therefore walks the indices in order and stops at the first it cannot read through — including one another family owns. `SemanticIndexKeyTest` went red the moment the family landed, which is what the mirror tier is for.

**A reader capability landed with it.** `semanticIndexUsesIn` reads the index as the PAIR its laws are about — the indexed reference, the key, and what the key carries — where the existing scan answered only what stands in the key position. Its nesting flag encodes A12's left-recursive parse: in `[A For B For C]` the OUTER index is the last one in the text, which is also why the family answers a nested index before the walk rather than inside it.

**A test-authoring trap worth naming, because it makes a mirror VACUOUS rather than red.** Three rows silently measured nothing: their probe carried a `"` that was interpolated straight into the model JSON, so the source never parsed and the whole check returned no findings at all. Rows expecting a code fail loudly; rows expecting silence pass. The tell was that every failing row had a string literal in it, and the fix is to escape the quote in the probe — worth remembering because the same shape reads as a correct test.

### C record — the fifty-eighth family: a budget that is per CALL, and the reader capability it needed (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the numeric operand-list `Min`/`Max` constant budget.** The law was already measured (`OpListMathConstantLawsTest`, since 2026-07-23), so this slice is what the seam looks like when the oracle is already in hand: read the kernel's counting method, add the one reader capability it needs, wire, mirror.

**The capability is `CallOperand.constant`, and it belongs in the reader rather than in the family.** A12's `CheckOpUtils.isConstant` asks whether an operand is ONE literal with `{…}` unwrapped, and the shape parser could not answer it — `literal` has always meant "a string constant" and says nothing about a number. Adding it to the operand rather than re-tokenizing inside the family is what keeps the next constant law cheap, and its narrowness is deliberate: a temporal constant reads as false, so a counting law under-reports rather than refusing a call A12 admits.

**Two spellings had to be measured before the reader could match them.** A12's number terminal is a digit RUN, so a decimal arrives as three tokens and a signed literal as two; both count exactly like a bare digit run, measured rather than inferred from the grammar. Under-recognizing either would have been silent, and over-recognizing would have refused a call A12 admits.

**What makes this family worth having at all is the pair of admitted rows.** A literal inside arithmetic and a literal inside a NESTED call both leave the outer budget untouched, so the naive implementation — count the literals in the text — refuses two shapes A12 accepts. Counting each call's own operands is the whole family.

### C record — the fifty-seventh family: four codes from ONE operator, and the gate order that decides which one (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: `SumOfProducts`' operand PAIR.** [KF370](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf370) carries the chain. Four residue codes land together — `MVK_DIFFERENT_GROUPS`, `MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED`, `MVK_WILDCARD_ONLY_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_ALLOWED`, `MVK_REPEATABLE_LEVEL_REQUIRED` — because A12 decides all four in one operator's gate chain and a slice taking any one of them would have had to guess the order of the rest.

**The construct rule paid again, and faster: the matrix was already measured.** `SumOfProductsLawsTest` had eleven rows including a precedence grid; the work was reading `CheckFeldListenOpImpl` for the gates the grid did not cover, which took one grid of seven cells. Two of those seven were surprises, and both would have shipped a wrong class: the arity code is `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID_2` and not the sibling spelling, and a GROUP operand is taken by the DUPLICATE gate rather than by the kind gate, which makes the kind class unreachable on that pair.

**The class whose NAME is misleading is the one a reproduction most needs.** `MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED` sounds like "a star above the lowest level" and is never that — the reference reader answers that shape with `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` first. It is reachable only through a `Having` filter, because `isWildcardRestricted` reads a filter as restricting every level. A family written from the code name alone would have reported it on the wrong shape and stayed silent on the right one.

**The precondition that keeps three foreign classes out is one equality.** Each operand must resolve to a FIELD inside the declaring group and star exactly the repeatable levels between the two; unequal either way, A12's answer is the reference reader's and belongs to another family. Counting those levels from the DECLARING GROUP rather than from the model root is load-bearing — the kernel fixture that exercises two nested repeatables has a repeatable ROOT group, where root-relative counting silences a legal pair.

**Residue arithmetic.** Six `kernel-delegated` rows re-dispositioned across this slice and the one before it, four of them to `checker-reported`. What the two slices share is the method: one construct, one `Check…Impl`, the whole gate chain in source order, then a grid for the cells the chain does not settle by itself.

### C record — the fifty-sixth family: one `switch` in the kernel, three arms, and the two operators a name-driven family would have over-rejected (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the repetition wildcard's legality in a GROUP-CONDITION operand.** [KF369](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf369) carries the grid. Two codes land at once — `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` on `GroupFilled`/`GroupNotFilled` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` on `AllGroupsFilled`/`GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled`/`NotAllGroupsFilled` — because they are the two arms of one kernel `switch` and a slice that took only one of them would have had to guess the other.

**The accelerator generalized, and this is the record of it: read the CHECKER, not the rule.** The `n/a-structural` seam was drained by reading `CodeGenValidationDataCollector`; the same move on the parser side is to find the single `Check…Impl` that owns a grammar rule and transcribe its gate order. `CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl.check` is 40 lines and gives the whole seven-operator family: which arm each operator sits on, that three of them run a root gate first, and that two run no wildcard gate at all. The prediction accuracy that follows is the same as the collector's — 14 of 15 rows first time, the miss being a control rather than a law.

**The two ungated operators are why this needed measuring at all.** `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` and `NoGroupFilled` are spelled like the three that refuse and take the same operand list, so a family assembled from the operators' NAMES over-rejects two of seven. That is the forbidden direction, and nothing but the switch or the grid separates them.

**One prediction from the kernel's own predicate was wrong, and its row is the family's shape.** `EntityRef.hasWildCardsInGroup` exempts a FIELD operand's own repetition index, which reads as "a field one level down escapes"; measured, it does not. So the family tests the REFERENCE and never the operand's kind — a rule read straight from a predicate's body, refuted by the run.

**Three stale ledger rows fell out.** `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` was `kernel-delegated` on a row that said "in some positions" without naming them; `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` gained a second checker position; and `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` gained the strongest of its five triggers — the reference reader answers an unstarred repeatable operand ahead of every operator gate AND ahead of the root gate, so the three refusing operators admit no spelling of a repeatable operand at all.

**What this says about the remaining residue.** The `kernel-delegated` bucket is not one seam. Its per-operator operand-TYPING rows stay out of scope, but its per-operator PATH-SHAPE rows — wildcard placement, operand co-location, semantic-index admission — are ordinary declaration-reading work, and each one has a single `Check…Impl` that hands over the whole construct. That is the next block, ranked by construct as the queue record already directs.

### C record — second pass on the standing queue, and the unit that actually pays is the CONSTRUCT (2026-09-14)

**The queue's second item is `IterationLawsTest`, and greping its eight codes first — the method correction the queue's own record carries — said not to convert it.** Every one is already discussed elsewhere, several of them heavily; a grid over that file would mostly restate. What the grep also showed is why: the codes TRAVEL, so a file's assertions are a poor index of a law's coverage.

**Re-aiming the same pass at a CONSTRUCT instead of a file paid immediately.** `RepetitionNotUnique`'s eleven classes were measured one containment assertion at a time across four files, and reading A12's own `CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl` gives the whole chain in source order. Sixteen rows later: the complete key-list order, the complete scope order, that every key gate reports ahead of every scope gate, and that the `@From` and no-`@From` branches are EXCLUSIVE rather than ordered ([KF355](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf355)). Two of the eleven — `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED` and `MVK_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP` — had no measurement anywhere in this estate.

**One carrier fact cost a build and generalizes a narrower record.** No placement row was reachable at all until the two repeatable groups were INDEX-KEYED together: otherwise the rule reads a repetition its error field does not sit in, and [KF350](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf350)'s iteration gate answers on every row at once. [KF349](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf349) had recorded that requirement as a property of `MVK_NOT_IN_GROUP`; it belongs to the carrier, and three further classes need it too.

**It also settled a policy question that had been implicit across seven families.** A key-list defect pre-empts `REPETITION_SCOPE_GROUP`'s class in A12, so does the checker stay silent there? No — the scope's defect survives fixing the key, so it is independently real and reporting it is the accumulation this checker exists for. The line is the one the combination family already draws: stay silent only where the second defect exists ONLY because of the first. That distinction is now stated in both families' mirrors rather than inferred from their behaviour.

**Queue method, refined: rank by construct, not by file.** Take one construct, find the single kernel method that guards it, transcribe the chain, then grep the estate per code to drop rows already answered. The file ranking still says WHERE containment assertions cluster, which is a hint about which constructs are under-measured — not a work list.

### C record — the family residue, classified: what is left is the per-operator gap plus four dead codes (measured 2026-09-14)

**The residue was re-derived after the seventh family and then CLASSIFIED, which is the step the earlier passes skipped.** Every `MVK_*` the ledger calls `kernel-delegated` minus every code the checker reports leaves 99; scoring each by mentions in a laws test and in KERNEL-FINDINGS leaves 32 with at most one. Of those, **8 already carry a disposition in their own ledger row** — dormant, unreachable, or no firing site in 30.8.1 — which an evidence count cannot see, so the ranking sends a reader at them repeatedly. Two were chased this way before the rows were read.

**Of the 24 that remain, sixteen are per-operator operand TYPING** — `MVK_NON_NUMERIC`, `MVK_NO_DATE_CONSTANT`, `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM`, the `YES`/`NO` value pair, the tolerance and rounding kinds — which is the standing gap this plan names and does not reproduce. Three are enum/category typing, two are number-config, and the rest are one-offs already answered elsewhere. **So the relational and whole-rule seam that produced seven families is drained**, and the next family in this area needs either the per-operator catalog decision or a new seam, not another pass over this list.

**Four of the 24 could never have been families at all, and finding that out closed a class** ([KF354](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf354)): they are message LABELS a checker passes into a shared helper, so no model draws them. Two had been corrected individually, months apart, each as a surprise — the mechanism was described twice and named neither time.

**The method correction to carry forward: rank by evidence, then READ the ledger row before predicting anything.** The row carries a disposition the count cannot, exactly as the earlier record found that the laws-file ranking measures where containment assertions live rather than where knowledge is missing. Both are the same mistake — trusting a derived score over the prose that owns the fact.

### C record — the `n/a-structural` seam is DRAINED, and what it cost and paid (2026-09-14)

**Every one of the ledger's 96 `n/a-structural` rows now carries a disposition.** The last block — the enumeration's per-language labels — gave one more class, `MVK_DISPLAY_VALUES_DUPLICATE`, whose whole content is that the law holds within ONE language: the same text for one value in English and another in German is admitted ([KF368](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf368)). Everything else in that block is unreachable through one mechanism, the collector walking the declared locale SET, plus a dead extensible-enumeration arm.

**The seam's total, so a later reader can judge whether a similar one is worth opening.** Seven blocks measured over one stretch: **eight new classes** reported by the checker (five number-budget, the group repetition cap, the enum special value, the reserved root-group name, the duplicate label — across two new families and four existing ones), **~45 rows re-dispositioned as unreachable or unmeasured**, and **four ledger rows corrected** that had been under-stating work already done. No block cost more than about half an hour once the method settled.

**The method that made it cheap, in one line: read the two COLLECTORS first, and grep the checker before implementing.** The collectors decide reachability before a fixture exists; the checker grep is what stops a code that is already reported from being implemented twice, which happened once and was caught only by three existing tests going red.

**What is left in the residue is now genuinely different work** — the `pre-checked` bucket, whose codes are about a rule's own slots rather than a declaration's shape, and the standing per-operator typing gap this plan does not reproduce. Neither is another pass over this list.

### C record — the collector sweep run at scale: sixteen rows closed, one class gained, one published claim corrected (2026-09-14)

**The accelerator from the previous record was applied to the whole remaining residue in one pass, and it holds** ([KF367](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf367)). Sixteen `n/a-structural` rows are now re-dispositioned on a collector read — the four `*_H_DV` name laws and `MVK_ROW_TOO_BIG` all gate on an `oldWorld` flag only an H model sets; the two header laws are the collector's own constants; the date-format laws, `MVK_NAME_MISSING` and `MVK_TYPE_MISSING` all test members written unconditionally. **No fixture was needed for any of them**, which is the whole point: the reachability question is decided one layer above the grid.

**One class came out of it, and it is the estate's only whole-name reservation.** A ROOT group called `Id` in any case draws `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_NAME_INVALID`; `Identity`, `BinId`, a nested group and a field of that name are all admitted. It rides `ELEMENT_NAME_ALPHABET` as its first gate, since a reservation and a bad character want different fixes and the codes already differ.

**And the sweep corrected a claim this session had been acting on.** [KF362](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf362) recorded that a hand-written model draws `MVK_MAX_DIGITS_MISSING` and `MVK_PROGRAMMING_LANGUAGE_MISSING` — both unreachable, as the collector shows. `ModelHeaderSlotLawsTest` now measures what actually refuses one: a single absent `header.modelVersion`, with no class, while `id` and `modelType` may both be left out. The correction cost one small grid and removes a fixture rule that had been slowing every declaration block since.

**The method note to carry: the collector answers WHETHER, never WHICH TIER.** Three of this stretch's surprises were all of that shape — an empty enum value list, a duplicated header locale and a table member are refused at the build or reader tier rather than by the law that names them. So a collector read is enough to close a row as unreachable, and never enough to predict a verdict.

### C record — the enum-declaration block, and the accelerator that found it: read the COLLECTOR, not the rule (2026-09-14)

**Fifteen Enumeration shape laws yield one family, ten codes closed on a single member list, and two that stay honestly unmeasured** ([KF366](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf366)). `ENUM_S_VALUE_DOMAIN` reports `MVK_S_VALUE_INVALID` — the kernel basis modelkit's one documented over-strictness (API-FINDINGS 96) had only prose for, now measured on the stored carrier too, where another tool may have written the declaration.

**The accelerator is worth more than the block.** Every `n/a-structural` code comes from a rule in A12's metamodel document model, and whether a stored model can reach it is decided by ONE pair of files — `CodeGenValidationDataCollector` and `FieldDataCollector`, which build that document from a stored model. Reading the collector before writing a grid turned prediction from a coin flip into a formality: the number block ran 12-of-13 correct, the group block 12-of-12, and the enum block's surprises were all in the tier a refusal lands in rather than in whether it fires. The rule to carry: **find the collector line for the member the rule reads, and the reachability question is already answered** — a member written unconditionally kills every `FieldNotFilled` law over it, a member never written kills every `FieldFilled` gate.

**Two things the collector cannot tell you, and both bit here.** A shape may be refused ONE TIER EARLIER than the law — an empty value list is unbuildable, a `tableName` is UNREADABLE — and only a run says which tier. And a metamodel CUSTOM CONDITION is opaque: `ungueltigerXMLWert` and `ungueltigerKategorieWert` were not reached by markup or by a control character, so what they refuse is unknown and nothing reproduces them. Those two rows are recorded as *unmeasured predicate*, never as unreachable, because the difference is exactly what a later reader needs.

**Three ledger rows were STALE rather than open, and the triage that found the block found them too.** `MVK_CATEGORY_VALUE_DUPLICATE`, `MVK_PATTERN_INVALID` and `MVK_SORT_FIELD_DUPLICATE` are all reported by the checker and locked, with rows that still read as untouched — one of them cost a wrong implementation that three existing tests caught within a minute. A ledger row is the queue the next session works from, so a row that under-states its own state is not a cosmetic defect: **before implementing any code from the residue, grep the checker for it.**

### C record — the fifth declaration block: the fourteenth family, on a cap that is two caps (2026-09-14)

**`GROUP_REPETITION_COUNT` is one predicate and its whole content is that A12 states the law TWICE** — a root group is capped at 999 999 999 and a nested one at 1 999 999 999 ([KF365](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf365)). Measuring one carrier and transferring would have produced a family that refuses a legal nested group or misses an illegal root one, and nothing inside a single-carrier grid could have shown it. Its own constant because it is a claim about the declaration's SIZE rather than about a reference, which is what every other group family judges.

**Three of the block's four codes have no stored spelling, and the two index ones needed both spellings attacked.** `MVK_MAX_MISSING` cannot fire because A12's collector writes the count it derived rather than the member it read; `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_DUPLICATE` and `MVK_TOO_MANY_INDEX_FIELDS` both need a group to declare several index fields, and a stored model declares one — an array under the singular member is refused by the reader, a plural member is ignored. Fifteen ledger rows are now re-dispositioned across five blocks.

**The block also produced a third tier boundary worth encoding.** Past a signed 32-bit count A12 stops READING, so the family reports the unbuildable tier there rather than the cap's class: naming `MVK_MAX_TOO_BIG` for a model A12 never judged would be a verdict the checker invented. Both cells of that boundary are measured.

### C record — the fourth declaration block pays five codes into an existing family, and the seam's rate is now known (2026-09-14)

**The number-type block is the productive one, and the reason is that the checker already owned the construct.** `CheckFamily.NUMBER_TYPE_DIGITS` was reading the same `NumberType` body for two laws, so five more — `MVK_FRACT_DIGITS_MISSING`, `MVK_ARITY_TOO_BIG`, `MVK_MAX_LENGTH_TOO_BIG`, `MVK_MIN_LENGTH_TOO_BIG`, `MVK_MAX_LENGTH_TOO_SMALL` — landed as three predicates in that file with no new constant, no wiring, and no divergence dispositions (none of the five is named in a kit main source). A sixth, `MVK_MIN_LENGTH_BIGGER_MAX_LENGTH`, gained its THIRD carrier ([KF364](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf364)).

**So the selection rule the sort block suggested is now confirmed and can be stated as a rule: prefer a block whose construct a family already reads.** Three blocks measured against constructs the checker did not own yielded one family and eleven closed rows; this one yielded five codes in a single file. The `n/a-structural` queue should be worked construct-first rather than code-first.

**The budget had to be DERIVED, because it is a member of A12's metamodel document and not of a stored model.** `/MVK/ModelProperties/maxDigits` is 15, and the length threshold is `15 + a sign + a decimal point` — established by walking a `maxLength` of 16 across `positivesOnly` and across a declared scale rather than by reading the constant anywhere. A grid that had assumed 15 flat would have under-reported every signed number by one place.

**One code in the block is unreachable, and both links of that were attacked separately** — the arm IS reached (`extractFieldTypeName` names an amount `GeldBetrag*`), and on that arm the member `MVK_AMOUNT_INT_PART_MISSING` demands is derived by `TypeConverter.convertNumber` and therefore never absent. Measuring only the first link would have read as a reachability result and been wrong.

**The row that changed the family's shape was the SUPPRESSED fractional pair.** With both members `-1`, `maxLength` 17 is refused where a real fractional part admits it, so A12 takes its `== 0` branch against a member its collector never wrote. Both rows of that pair were needed: the over-long one is consistent with either reading, and only the boundary one discriminates. A checker that had read `-1` as "some fractional part" would have allowed one length too many on every such field.

### C record — the third declaration block: no family, no gap, and one mislabelled row that nearly inverted it (2026-09-14)

**A name is scoped to its PARENT, so two of the three "model-wide" laws are not laws of a stored model at all** ([KF363](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf363)). `Insured` under two different root groups is admitted, and so are two fields of different groups sharing a name. The two collisions A12 does refuse — the same parent, and two root groups — are build refusals carrying no class, and BOTH are already covered: `ELEMENT_NAME_UNIQUENESS` and `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`. The second was verified against the checker rather than inferred from the family's wording, which speaks of a group's CHILDREN while root groups are not any group's children.

**Eleven ledger rows are now re-dispositioned across three blocks, against two families.** That is the seam's measured yield, and it is a good rate rather than a poor one: a row closed on a shape fact never comes back, while a row left as "outside rulekit's authoring surface" sends the next reader at it again.

**The row that nearly made the grid say the opposite.** The "two subgroups of DIFFERENT parents" case was first written as an insertion into the same parent's element list — it measured NOT BUILDABLE, agreed with the ledger's framing, and would have shipped a model-wide law that does not exist. Nothing about the output looked wrong; the label and the patch simply disagreed. The general form of that check is cheap and belongs beside the control rule: **read what the patch does, not what the row is called.**

### C record — the second declaration block measured, and it yields NO family at all (2026-09-14)

**Four yes/no laws, four unreachable.** A `BooleanType` carrying an identical true/false pair, only one of them, or two blanks is ADMITTED in every combination ([KF362](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf362)): `TypeConverter.convertBoolean` defaults each member, so "missing" never reaches a check, and the pair laws do not fire on a stored model either. Eight ledger rows are now re-dispositioned across the two blocks — four here, four in the sort block — against two families.

**That ratio is the seam's real shape and is worth stating plainly, because it changes how to spend on it.** The `n/a-structural` bucket's rows come from A12's own metamodel document models, which validate a model-under-edit; a stored DM-JSON model reaches only some of them. So the enumeration's output is roughly one family per BLOCK and a majority of closed-forever rows — still worth working, and not at the rate the raw count of 69 suggests.

**The grid needed a POSITIVE CONTROL to mean anything, and it nearly shipped without one.** Every row was ADMITTED, which is exactly what a patch that failed to land would produce. The control — the same patch with an unknown `fieldType.type` — came back UNREADABLE, proving the bytes reach A12's deserializer. Two rounds were also lost to a hand-written model whose header omitted members that are themselves laws, so every row read NOT BUILDABLE for unrelated reasons; the fixtures build through `DocumentModelDraft` for that reason and a grid that patches one should start from a built model.

**One incidental defect, recorded rather than fixed here.** Modelkit's `BooleanSpec` and `ConfirmSpec` are empty records and the reader drops A12's custom true/false members, so a read→modify→write silently rewrites a declared encoding — the same class the Enumeration S-value work closed once already. It blocks nothing in this plan, since the codes are unreachable, so it is a `DEV-API-GAPS` row with the established fix pattern named rather than an interruption to the loop.

### C record — the thirteenth family, and the first block of the declaration seam, measured (2026-09-14)

**A group that cannot repeat cannot be sorted, and A12 says so with `MVK_INVALID_SORTINFO`.** The family is one predicate and rides the existing ordering file, because it is the same member read for a third claim — `GROUP_ORDERING_REFERENCES` resolves the entries, `GROUP_ORDERING_FIELD_TYPE` judges what they landed on, and this judges the CARRIER. Its own constant for the usual reason: a report merging them would let a group that cannot be sorted read as a group whose sort fields are wrong.

**The discriminating row is the EMPTY list, and it is the one a reader would skip.** An empty `sortFields` on the same non-repeatable carrier is ADMITTED, so the gate is on the entries and not on the member — a family keyed on the key's presence refuses every non-repeatable group that merely carries it.

**Three sibling mirrors went red, and they were right to.** Their fixtures put sort entries on non-repeatable groups, so every sort row had been carrying a second, unmeasured defect since those families landed. The carriers are now repeatable with the reason written beside them. That is the third time in six families that the signal came from outside the family's own tests, and the second time from a sibling.

**The block's other four codes are unreachable, which is the more durable half of the result.** They are all about a sort PRIORITY, and the DM-JSON entry has no such member — so the twenty minutes that produced one family also re-dispositioned four ledger rows from "outside rulekit's authoring surface" to "cannot be expressed in a stored model at all". A residue row closed on a shape fact stays closed; one closed on an evidence count comes back.

### C record — the residue enumeration re-derived over ALL THREE ledger dispositions, and what is actually left (measured 2026-09-14)

**The standing residue figure was computed over one disposition and read as the whole estate.** It enumerated `kernel-delegated` rows, found 24 live candidates, classified 16 of them as the per-operator typing gap, and concluded the area was drained. Two further buckets were never scanned, and each paid on the day it was: `erased` gave two families, `pre-checked` gave two more. The ledger's disposition vocabulary is the denominator, and the question to ask of each is the same one — **what does this disposition say about a STORED model?**

| disposition | rows | what it actually claims | reaches a stored model? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kernel-delegated` | 101 | the Kernel arbitrates; we do not reproduce | yes — the standing queue, now mostly the per-operator typing gap |
| `n/a-structural` | 96 | the typed BUILDER's shape makes it unrepresentable | **yes** — 69 unreported, and a stored DM-JSON model is not built by that builder |
| `erased` | 51 | the RENDERER never emits it | **yes** — `rule check` and `modify --condition` take raw strings |
| `pre-checked` | 46 | a builder or CLI pre-flight refuses it first | **yes** — a stored model never took the authoring path |

**So the largest remaining candidate is `n/a-structural` — 69 unreported codes in blocks that are constructs rather than lists**: the five SORT laws (a construct `GROUP_ORDERING_REFERENCES` and `GROUP_ORDERING_FIELD_TYPE` already half-own), four YES/NO type laws, six number-type laws beside `NUMBER_TYPE_DIGITS`, three name-uniqueness laws beside `ELEMENT_NAME_UNIQUENESS`, two index laws, two group-repetition laws, and a large table-backed-enum group that KERNEL-SEMANTICS §8 records as un-buildable and is genuinely out of reach.

**But the first block measured says this bucket is a queue of QUESTIONS, not of families — corrected in place before it could mislead.** These codes come from rules in A12's own METAMODEL document models, which validate a model-under-edit represented as an A12 document; whether one also constrains a stored DM-JSON model is a per-code reachability question with no shortcut. The sort block splits four-to-one: `MVK_INVALID_SORTINFO` is live and now reported, while the four PRIORITY laws cannot be expressed at all, A12's DM-JSON `Group.SortField` carrying only `field` and `order` with the position derived from the list index ([KF361](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf361)). So expect roughly one family per block rather than one per code, and expect the enumeration's value to be as much in the unreachability results as in the families — four ledger rows were re-dispositioned on a type-level basis by the same twenty minutes that produced the family.

**The method correction, which is the transferable part.** A residue is only as good as the disposition set it enumerates, and a disposition is a claim about ONE consumer — the builder, the renderer, the pre-flight. The portable checker is a FOURTH consumer that none of those three claims was written about, so every row deserves re-reading from its point of view rather than being inherited. That is the same mistake as ranking by file rather than by construct, one level up: trusting a derived score over the prose that owns the fact.

### C record — the twelfth family, and a law whose CLASS depends on the carrier four ways (2026-09-14)

**The `pre-checked` seam's largest coherent block was the enum VALUE domain, and it is four laws wearing one name.** A string constant outside an Enumeration's declared values draws `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON` from a comparison, `..._FOR_ENUMERATION` from a scalar value list, `..._FOR_ENUMERATION_CATEGORY` from a categorized one, and `..._FOR_ENUMERATION_OR_CATEGORY` from a multi-member quantifier checking the UNION of its members' domains ([KF360](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf360)). The category splits the value list and not the comparison, which neither kernel method predicts alone.

**So the family takes two carriers of four and says so in three sentences of KDoc.** The comparison and the quantifier need operand shapes the shared call reader does not reduce, and a categorized operand is not a bare entity spec — `asPath` disqualifies `->` by contract, which every operand-reading family depends on. Widening that reduction is a change to all of them and is not this family's to make; naming the scalars' class on the other carriers would be the wrong class on a correct rejection.

**One reader gap was closed rather than worked around.** `CallOperand` carried the path an operand names and nothing about a LITERAL, so no family could judge a constant at all. It now carries the string constant with A12's doubled `""` collapsed, which is where that unwrapping belongs: at the reader, once, rather than at each consumer comparing a value against a domain.

**Two source-order predictions were refuted in one day**, this one and the tenth family's. A number field in a value list was predicted to draw the loop's fall-through kind class and draws `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON` instead, a comparison gate answering ahead of the loop. The firing-site method keeps paying for grid WRITING; the day's evidence is that it buys nothing at all for grid TRUSTING.

### C record — the eleventh family, a SECOND unscanned seam, and the differential earning its keep (2026-09-14)

**`pre-checked` is the same denominator hole as `erased`, one layer over.** A pre-flight runs on the AUTHORING path — `EnumRef.category(name)`, `Rule.build()`, the CLI's `ModelPaths` — and the portable checker reads a STORED model that never took that path. 46 ledger rows carry the disposition; 23 name a code the checker does not report. Twelve of those are the per-operator operand-typing gap this plan excludes, and the rest are a real queue.

**The family taken from it is the `->` category reference, and the checker was blind to it BY CONSTRUCTION.** Its reference scan steps over the name behind the arrow deliberately — a category is an attribute, not an entity, so resolving it would report every categorized reference as undeclared. What the law needs is the PAIRING of reference and name, which no existing reader produced. Six rows, six distinct classes, three of them never measured in this estate.

**And the differential caught an over-rejection the family's own grid could not have.** A first version reported `MVK_NO_ENUMERATION` on `[format->containsYear]` in a reference-corpus model A12 builds: the field is typed by reference to a type definition, whose body preparation does not keep — so it may well BE an Enumeration declaring that category. Every row of the family's own grid used inline types, which is the set-wide constant no control inside the set can catch, and `CheckerSilenceOnCleanModelsTest` is what sat on the other side of that axis. The fix is silence on `TypeDefType`, riding a coverage gap `Check.kt` had already declared.

**That is the second time in three families that the signal came from outside the family's own tests** — the eighth was caught by a sibling family's rows, this one by the corpus sweep. Both are the same lesson in different clothes: a family's grid is built from what its author imagined, so the instruments that see past it are the ones built from somebody else's models.

### C record — the tenth family, and the run that refuted a chain read straight from source (2026-09-14)

**The construct was the `Having` clause's OWN operand, and it is one kernel method — the firing-site method paying for the third time.** `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkFilterCondition` runs four gates in order: field, wildcard, no semantic index, then the filter BODY. The body already had a family; the operand's three gates had none, and two of their classes had no measurement anywhere in this estate.

**Transcribing the chain would have shipped a wrong class, and one row caught it.** Source order puts `checkIsField` ahead of `checkHasWildCards`, so an operand that is BOTH a group and indexed was predicted to draw `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`. A12 answers `MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED`. The row cost one build and was run only because the family had to decide a precedence — which is the general case for the method: reading the firing site makes a grid cheap to WRITE, and does not make it optional to RUN. The order a method is written in is a hypothesis about the order it answers in.

**`MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED` does not mean what its name says.** An unstarred filtered field draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` — A12's iteration gate outruns the chain — so the class is reachable only through a semantic index on the operand, and then whether or not a `*` is written, because the index spelling consumes it. A family built from the name would have reported the wildcard class on exactly the shape A12 answers with a different one.

**The star turned out to be a non-variable on both defects, which is why each is measured twice.** A group operand answers the same starred or not; an indexed operand likewise. The previous family's rule — compare the operand's star count against the repeating levels on its path — therefore does NOT transfer here, and the pair of rows is what establishes that rather than an argument from the two families' similarity.

### C record — the ninth family, and the SEAM the residue enumeration never scanned (2026-09-14)

**The residue pass that declared this area drained started from every code the ledger calls `kernel-delegated`, and the eighth family had just come from an `erased` row.** That is a denominator hole, not a judgement call: 60 rows carry `erased`, and an erasure justified by the TYPED SURFACE — "the renderer never emits it", "the handle offers no such method" — is a fact about rulekit's builder and says nothing about a stored model, because `rule check` and `modify --condition` both take raw condition text and the portable checker reads stored declarations. Re-reading the 60 with that question produced this family within the hour.

**Most of the seam is already closed or out of scope, which is why naming it matters more than the one family.** Eight of the renderer-erased condition codes are ALREADY checker-reported (`MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_PARENT_GROUPS`, `MVK_BRACKET_MISSING`, both wildcard classes, `MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`); the rest are per-operator operand TYPING or ARITY, the standing gap this plan excludes. `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` was the one member that is neither — an operand KIND law, whose mirror this checker already carries.

**A tenth row was chased and closed on the way, and its ledger reason was wrong.** `MVK_ITERATION_RESTRICTION_IN_A12_MODEL` is recorded as erased because "rulekit renders only the unrestricted star". Its only A12-model raiser is `IterationChecker.checkIterationRestriction4A12Model`, which fires on `IExtmpRule.getRootgroupIterationRestriction()` / `getRowIterationRestriction()` — two getters declared solely on the H-model rule interface, with no DM-JSON spelling anywhere in the kernel. So the code is unreachable from a stored A12 model for a TYPE-level reason that no raw string defeats, and the recorded reason named a construct the gate never reads.

**The family itself is [KF327](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf327)'s trap arriving from the other side.** There, a family built from the operators' names would have refused five legal shapes; here it would have refused eight, because every field-LIST carrier expands a group operand instead of refusing it. Two carriers refuse, and both are the scalars. The discriminating pair is the two wildcard rows, which point opposite ways: starring a repeatable group moves the verdict INTO the kind class, and starring one that does not repeat moves it OUT — so the star is neither noise nor a uniform signal, and the family compares the operand's star count against the number of repeating levels on its path rather than testing for a star at all.

### C record — the eighth relational family: a code the ledger called ERASED, and a two-character over-rejection (2026-09-14)

**`MVK_POINT_IN_A12_MODEL` was carried as erased for the right reason and the wrong scope.** The ledger row said the typed renderer never emits the `.*` path suffix, which is true of the renderer and settles nothing about a model: `rule check` and `modify --condition` both take a raw condition string, so a stored declaration can carry the spelling and A12 duly rejects it. The code had no measurement anywhere in this estate before [KF356](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf356).

**The family's first version over-rejected, which is the one direction this project forbids, and no new test caught it.** `".*" in written` matches the same two characters inside a starred UP-STEP (`..*`), a different construct A12 answers with `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_REL`. Two rows of `RepetitionWildcardTest` — a family landed weeks earlier, about a different construct — went red and were the whole signal. The narrowing is the one the resolver's own `withoutRepetitionSuffix()` already draws: the dot must not itself follow a dot.

**It is the second family that deliberately reports where A12 stops.** On `Insured.*/InsuredName` A12 names the suffix class alone, because the suffix gate sits ahead of the wildcard's landing site within the segment. The checker names `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD` too, and the policy test is the one the combination family already drew: fixing the suffix leaves `Insured*`, still a star on a group that does not repeat, so the second defect is independently real rather than an artefact of the first.

### C record — the seventh relational family, and the set-wide CONSTANT the estate had carried for months (2026-09-14)

**The whole-rule chain read in the sixth record paid again, and this time the finding was in the SET rather than in any row.** Every measurement of parallel iteration in this estate — the accepted cross-group read, the pure-negative rejection, `IterationLawsTest`'s four rows, the new grid's five — pairs two index fields both declared STRING. So "two groups whose index field has the same NAME" and "…the same name and TYPE" were one thing across every row anyone had ever run here, and the record's own prose says "same-name/type" without any row having varied the type. Declaring the second key NUMBER draws `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX_TYPE`, a class with no evidence anywhere, ahead of the iteration gates.

**That single row is what makes the family safe, and without it the family would have been wrong.** A checker pairing groups on the index field's NAME alone reports `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_NOT_ALLOWED` where A12 reports the index-type class — a wrong class on a correct rejection, which is the defect this estate shipped once in September and built three differentials to catch. It was found by asking the set-constant question of the ACCOUNT rather than of a row, which is the one confound no control inside the set can catch.

**A second over-rejection was closed the same way.** A STARRED read of the joint group is ADMITTED — the star puts a wildcard at that level instead of the joint-iteration marker — so a family reading "the condition names two index-keyed groups" would refuse a legal model. Both bounds are in the family's KDoc as measured rows rather than as reasoning.

**The family deliberately asks for LESS than A12 does.** A12 compares the two index fields with `matchBaseType`; this asks for the same declared type string, and for the same field name, which is the intersection of what has been measured. Whether a differing NAME alone stops the joint iteration is unmeasured; both unknowns resolve towards silence.

### C record — the sixth relational family, and the WHOLE-RULE gate chain the estate had never read (2026-09-14)

**One kernel method carries six classes in source order, and reading it is the cheapest survey in this slice.** `ParserService.checkNormalRule` is A12's whole-RULE check chain, running after a condition parses: parallel iteration against `RepetitionNotUnique`, a parallel-iterated filter, a filter that parallel-iterates, a filter using a semantic index, two `RepetitionNotUnique` calls, two of its legacy twin, and a raw-type `Length`. Three of those codes had no measurement here. The family landed from the one that is decidable without modelling iteration — the call COUNT — and the rest of the chain is now a named, ordered candidate list rather than seven ledger rows nobody had connected.

**Eight predictions, eight right, including both precedence rows.** That is the second family in a row where reading A12's firing site first turned the grid into a transcription; the method is now in [TESTING-SPEC](TESTING-SPEC.md)'s landing checklist rather than only in these records.

**The mirror found the one row the grid could not, and it is a wrong-CLASS risk rather than an over-rejection.** The checker's call scanner descends into a `Having` filter body, so a condition with one top-level call and one inside a filter counted as two — and A12 answers that shape with `MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`, because the construct is refused in that position outright. Removing the filter's call leaves one legal call, so the count was an artefact rather than a defect. The fix subtracts calls found inside filter clauses, and the row went into the kernel grid so the claim rests on a measurement rather than on the catalog's prose.

**This family breaks the estate's usual silence rule on purpose, which is worth stating because it looks like the defect the others avoid.** Every relational family so far stays silent wherever another class pre-empts it. Here the pre-empting classes belong to CONSTRUCTS, and a condition with two calls and a bad `@From` scope genuinely has both defects — A12 just stops at the first. Suppressing the count would hide work the author still owes; the filter case is different precisely because there the second call is not a defect at all.

**The Java owner's corrective, written long before this grid, states the same law in its own words** — "counts nodes, not key fields and not And/Or nesting; one leaf may carry a composite key". The projection guard forced it into view, which is the second time that guard has doubled as an independent check on a measurement.

### C record — the fifth relational family, and the enumeration that found a whole unmeasured surface (2026-09-14)

**Selected by ranking the residue on EXISTING EVIDENCE rather than on the ledger's prose, which is what surfaced it.** Every `MVK_*` the ledger calls `kernel-delegated` minus every code the checker reports leaves 102; scoring each by how many times it appears in a laws test and in KERNEL-FINDINGS put a cluster of nine semantic-index codes at the very bottom, five of them with zero mentions anywhere in the estate. That is not a thin measurement — it is a construct whose entire diagnostic vocabulary had never been drawn, hiding behind ledger rows that each read as a settled law.

**Reading A12's firing site BEFORE predicting turned the grid from a search into a transcription, and that is the reusable part.** `CheckSemanticIndexImpl.doChecks` is one method carrying the whole chain in source order, so the grid's rows and their order were read off it rather than guessed; the previous families discovered the same ordering one failing prediction at a time. Two of the nine predictions were still wrong, and both were about a gate ABOVE the chain rather than inside it — which is exactly what a source read of one method cannot show.

**Two ledger rows were wrong in the way this plan has now recorded four times: written from the code's NAME.** `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_REF_OP` is never reported at all — A12 passes its bare text as the operation NAME into the shared value-validation check, which raises `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE` — and `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NO_FIELD` is reachable only when the key does not iterate, because the ordinary iteration gate answers first under a second name. Both rows named real laws under codes that cannot carry them.

**A `grep --include` with unquoted globs produced a confident ZERO, and it nearly retired a live code.** The first enumeration concluded `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_BELOW_WILDCARD` had no raiser in the kernel and was therefore dead prose; the shell had eaten the include patterns, and the code is live from a second site entirely. The record's own rule caught it — re-derive a suspicious zero a second way — and the cost of not doing so would have been a finding asserting a negative existential about somebody else's source.

**The family reports the one cell it can decide without knowing the rule's anchoring.** `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY` names `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NO_FIELD` when the key resolves to a group with nothing repeating on its path. Where something repeats, whether the read iterates turns on where the rule sits, which this checker does not model — so it stays silent and leaves those to A12, an under-report rather than a class it would probably get wrong.

### C record — standing candidate: the single-code rejection claim, which cannot see a second gate (2026-09-14)

**Three measurement defects in two days came from one place, and it has a denominator.** `KernelLaws.rejects*(…, code)` asserts the expected code is AMONG the diagnostics — `KernelLaws` says so itself, which is why the stricter `preemptedCode` spelling exists. A containment claim cannot see a co-firing second gate, cannot establish which gate reports FIRST, and cannot notice that the shape it measures satisfies a second condition nobody named. Re-derive rather than citing these: **203 `rejects*` calls, 223 `accepts*` calls, 28 strict/pre-empted spellings** (2026-09-14), so roughly 175 rejection claims stand on containment alone. `accepts*` carries no exposure — there is no code to hide.

**The three that have already fallen, each found by replacing one file's single assertions with a grid asserting the COMPLETE class list:**

| what was wrong | how the single assertion hid it |
|---|---|
| [KF340](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf340)'s mechanism — "resolution wins" | every row put the bad name first, so text order and resolution order were one thing |
| `SumOfProducts`' repeatable-level clause, short by a conjunct on FOUR surfaces | the one row exercising that arm gave both operands the same owning group |
| [KF349](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf349) — `MVK_NOT_IN_GROUP`'s reachability, which the ledger recorded as unexplained | the one cell sat on an index-keyed fixture, and nothing recorded that this was load-bearing |

**The queue is ranked by DISTINCT rejection codes in a grid-free file, not by assertion count** — `AggregateSlotLawsTest` (8 codes / 12 claims), `IterationLawsTest` (8 / 8), `NumberLawsTest` (6 / 14), `AggregateGroupOperandLawsTest` (6 / 8), `PathLawsTest` (5 / 6), `RepetitionNotUniqueKeyKindLawsTest` (4 / 4); fourteen files assert three or more. *(Corrected 2026-09-14, within the hour: this first ranked by assertion count and sent the reader to `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest` (42 claims) — which is among the most rigorously measured files in the estate, its claims probing ONE gate on single-defect shapes with discriminating cases already built. Precedence can only be unmeasured between codes that a file actually asserts, so the count of DISTINCT codes is the proxy and the count of claims is noise.)* Fifty-two files carry single assertions and no grid at all.

**This is NOT a sweep to run to completion, and saying so is the point.** Converting 175 claims would be a large mechanical job with most of its cells confirming what is already believed; the yield lives where a shape can break TWO rules at once, which is judgment. So it is a standing candidate worked one file at a time, highest-count first, and each pass is cheap: build the grid with the complete class list, write the expectations as PREDICTIONS, and run once — a wrong prediction IS the finding. Every one of the three above was a wrong prediction on the first run.

**First pass on the queue's top item, and it corrects the method: GREP THE ESTATE PER CODE BEFORE PREDICTING (2026-09-14).** `AggregateSlotLawsTest` was worked as two grids. The filter grid paid — both of that construct's other gates pre-empt `MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION` and neither co-fires, which four containment assertions could not have said, and the four rows collapsed into one grid with two rows added. The operand grid paid **nothing**: its two wrong predictions were both already measured in this repository, one as [KF263](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf263)'s within-model 2×2 (`MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE` reads the component SET, so two DATE fields differing only in format string are ADMITTED — the code is misnamed) and one as the three-way homogeneity partition the catalog already publishes (`MVK_STRING_ENUM_AND_NON_STRING_ENUM`). It was discarded rather than landed, because a lock restating a law another lock already holds is cost without reach.

**So the ranking measures where CONTAINMENT assertions live, not where knowledge is missing, and those are different things.** A file can assert eight codes by containment while every one of its precedence questions is answered elsewhere — the codes travel across families, so the file that asserts a code is rarely the file that owns its law. The fix costs one command per code and goes *before* the predictions: `git grep -n '<CODE>' -- docs/ rulekit/src/main/resources/catalog/operators.json`, then keep only the rows no existing entry answers. Applied here it would have cut a ten-row grid to two and saved a build. This is the record's own "look first in your own findings" rule, which the queue's ranking quietly encourages you to skip.

### C record — the third and fourth relational families, and the reader capability that had been calling itself a blocker (2026-09-14)

**The blocker was one line of contract, not a missing analysis.** Order 6's cell named "the condition reader cannot reduce `@From` and `Having` clauses to operands" as what the remaining relational families wait on, and the shape parser had been parsing both clauses correctly all along — `ConditionShape` has productions for each. What was missing was only that `callsIn` threw the structure away: `CallOperand.path` is null whenever a clause appears, so every consumer saw an operand it could not compare, and three separate places had written that silence down as a design decision. The fix is additive and leaves `path` exactly as narrow as it was — `base` is the spec before the first clause and `clauses` carries each one with its keyword as spelled, its own reduced path, and its authored text. Cutting only at depth zero is the whole subtlety, since a filter body may carry its own bracketed spec with its own clause.

**A second consumer followed immediately, and it is the one that paid for the mirror rows.** `FILTER_WILDCARD` reports a repetition wildcard inside a `Having` filter (`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`), a position the reference-level wildcard family deliberately does not judge because position is what picks the class there. Writing its mirror's DECLINED rows turned up an over-rejection the family had shipped past its own grid: a star whose LEAF resolves to nothing was being reported, and A12 draws `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` there. The prediction that it would not came from `RepetitionWildcard`'s measured "the wildcard class outruns the entity gate" — true at the NON-repeatable landing site and false here, so that ordering belongs to the landing site rather than to the wildcard check. **A silence row nobody writes is an over-rejection nobody sees**, which is why these mirrors state every declined shape rather than only the reported ones.

**The consumer landed in the same slice, deliberately.** A reader capability with no family reading it is the "parsed but never consumed" class this repository has already shipped once, so `REPETITION_SCOPE_GROUP` — `@From` must name a REPEATABLE group, `MVK_GROUP_NOT_REPEATABLE` — went in beside it. The `:consistency:jvmTest` sweep after the family compiled was green on all 366 other tests, which is the mirror-layer check for an over-rejection no family's own grid can see.

**Two kernel rows had to be measured before the family could report anything, and one of them refuted its prediction.** The repeatability class had only ever been measured where the scope was the ROOT, so reporting from `isRepeatable` alone would have transferred it to a carrier nobody had run — an ordinary non-repeatable group confirms it. And a scope naming a FIELD draws `MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`, not the repeatability class, because the KIND gate runs first: "not a repeatable group" is true of every field, so the plausible reading would have attached this family's fix to a different gate on every one of them. **The generalizable part is that both rows were about what the family may NOT say**, and neither would have been asked by someone confirming a law they already believed.

### C record — the second relational family, where the CHECKER found the missing rows twice (2026-09-14)

**Selected in one command, and the checklist made the landing cheap.** The residue script ranks candidates by how narrow their existing evidence is; three of the top six were already covered or need the per-operator catalog, and the root-only error-target pair had a six-cell grid plus machinery `ErrorFieldReference` already owns. Running the new seven-step checklist FIRST established that neither code carries a corrective and neither is named by the kits — so three of the seven steps dropped before a single build ran, where the previous family discovered the same facts one failing guard at a time.

**The measured law needs three conditions and I had grounds for one.** A rule reaching its error field only from ABOVE its own group, while reading a repeating field, draws one of two classes by the read's carrier; pinning turns out to be a conjunction — a reference that reaches the target AND sits at or below the rule's group — and neither half alone gives it. [KF348](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf348) holds the grid across three carriers.

**The two conditions I was missing were CONSTANTS of the grid, and in both cases the estate's existing mirrors found them, not my reading.** Every row carried a reaching reference, so "reached from above" and "reached at all" were one thing — `ErrorFieldReferenceTest` went red on a condition naming a sibling field. Every row put the error field in a repeatable group, so the law read as being about reach when it is also about there BEING a repetition — `FieldVariantFilterTest` went red on an ordinary two-field rule with nothing repeating anywhere. Both were over-rejections, the one direction that is never acceptable, and neither was visible from inside the grid.

**That is the strongest argument yet for the mirror layer, and it is worth stating plainly:** a checker family is measured against the kernel by its own grid and against the REST OF THE ESTATE by every other family's fixtures, and the second catches exactly the confound the first cannot — an unnamed constant — because those fixtures were built for other reasons and share none of it. The two silences are now measured rather than guarded: a flat model is kernel-ADMITTED, and a sibling-only condition draws the referenced-ness class alone.

**A by-product worth keeping.** `FieldVariantFilterTest`'s control rows asserted an empty expectation that had never been put to the kernel — the defect class recorded earlier in this log. Measuring the flat shape to ground this family's guard also confirmed those controls, so one row closed two questions.

### C record — the first RELATIONAL family, and the cell that separated two readings of one pre-emption (2026-09-14)

**It was selected by re-deriving the residue the plan told me to re-derive rather than cite, and the residue's own shape was the answer.** Every `MVK_*` the ledger calls `kernel-delegated` minus every code the checker reports leaves 105; 60 of those already carry kernel evidence, and almost all of that 60 is per-operator typing — comparability, operand kinds, date formats — which is the standing gap this plan does not reproduce. What stands out instead is the iteration cluster, which is Order 6. Two near-misses are worth recording because both read as candidates: `MVK_NAME_RULES_DUPLICATE` is already covered, the checker reporting it as `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` because A12 throws instead of raising the code, so it sits in the residue only as an artefact of keying on MVK codes; and `MVK_MULTIPLE_INDEX_FIELDS` had been re-classified the day before as parallel-iteration rather than a declaration law.

**The family is `RepetitionNotUnique`'s key placement, and the naive law over-reports on two of eight cells.** The collapse was already documented — a key needs a repeatable group strictly between the rule's group and itself, and three different shapes draw the one class. What no row measured is what happens when that is violated AND a key sits outside the rule group. Two readings fitted everything that existed, they disagree at exactly one cell, and the cell draws a THIRD class, `MVK_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP`, which had no evidence anywhere in the estate. [KF347](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf347) holds the grid; the checker stays silent on every pre-empted row, because both replacement classes belong to families that do not exist yet and guessing between them attaches one fix to a defect that has another.

**A surviving mutant, not the grid, found the row the grid could not.** The depth carrier added for "is a NESTED repeatable group enough" put the repeatable group directly above the key, so it could not separate "a repeatable group anywhere between" from "the key's own parent is repeatable" — and a mutant reading only the parent survived. The fix was a second measured row two levels down, not a checker change: the implementation was right and the evidence was not. This is the cheapest instance yet of the rule that a mutant with no control IS the finding.

**The fixture grew twice and the twelve pre-existing rows were re-run unchanged each time**, which is what makes them evidence the extension is inert rather than an assumption that adding a group changes nothing.

**Two mechanical consequences arrived on their own, and both are the estate working.** `CheckFamilyWiringTest` refused a new constant with only a call site until the fixture carried a declaration that triggers it, and `CheckerCorrectiveProjectionGuardTest` refused the landing until the corrective was re-projected. That projection is also an independent check on the measurement: the Java owner's corrective, written long before this grid, states the same boundary law in its own words.

### C record — the mirror-versus-laws gap, measured: the decidable half is not the half that was asked for (2026-09-13)

**The recorded gap asked for something no scan can decide, and measuring that was the whole result.** The gap below wants a mirror compared against the laws test it mirrors. Its direct form — every `rulekit.validate.laws.X` a mirror cites must resolve, and every code the mirror asserts must appear in that cited test — was run over all 62 checker mirrors before any guard was written. It flags **15 files**, and almost all of it is noise: `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` is the checker's OWN verdict and no laws test could carry it, `ACCEPTED` and `MODEL_ROOT` are verdict tokens and fixture names, and a mirror legitimately asserts a code measured by a laws test it does not cite. Eleven further mirrors cite no laws test at all because they are infrastructure — wiring, preparation, report shape — with no kernel law to mirror, so the guard would need an exemption list that is exactly the authored denominator this estate refuses. **A guard that fails honest content gets disabled**, so the direct form is recorded as infeasible rather than shipped noisy.

**Moving the same question from the TEST side to the MAIN-SOURCE side makes it decidable, and sharper.** What a mirror asserts is second-hand; what the checker REPORTS is the claim itself — a `Finding` carrying `MVK_X` says *A12 draws this*, which is a statement about the kernel. So the invariant is: every A12 code the checker reports is named by some laws test. Both denominators are derived, there is no exemption list, and it runs at **zero** false positives on the live estate — all 66 reported codes are measured (`CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest`, on `leakGuardTest`; both arms mutation-proven, the floor arm against a scan matching nothing).

**It is weaker than the gap asked for, and the difference is stated rather than glossed.** It would NOT have caught `FieldVariantFilterTest` contradicting [KF305](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf305): the code that mirror over-asserted was measured elsewhere, so a "has some measurement" test passes it. That residue is the scope-transfer class — a code measured on one carrier and reported on another — which no scan can decide and which the witness-grid discipline owns. The gap below is therefore narrowed, not closed.

**The same scan found a live defect in a doc that had been corrected the day before.** [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md)'s codegen-tier scope note read "five of the 26 are now measured, one consumer reports four"; the derived figures are **nine** and **seven**. Three of the four it missed had been measured before the 2026-09-12 amendment that restated the count. The mechanism is the one CLAUDE.md names as the strengthening edit: each pass INCREMENTED the count by what that pass had just measured, rather than re-deriving it, and a count assembled that way can only ever see the families that think of themselves as belonging to the tier — an element name's alphabet does not. The row is amended in place with the derivation named.

**And `scripts/mutant.sh` could not mutate a file that had just been created.** Its landing proof was `git diff --quiet`, which is silent for an UNTRACKED file, so planting the positive control into the new guard class reported "the file is unchanged after the edit" and restored. It now compares BYTES against the backup it already holds — no index, and the same copy `restore` puts back, so the proof cannot disagree with what will be undone.

### C record — the defect-pairing pass, priced and started: three members, three different shapes (2026-09-13)

**The standing candidate below is no longer a candidate — it is paying, and the price is one grid per member.** Pairing defects within a member has now been run on three of them and produced three genuinely different answers, none of which could be guessed from another: a condition reports the FIRST defect in its text ([KF342](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf342)), a field type reports ALL of them ([KF341](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf341)), and a message LAYERS them — a character gate that wins wherever the character sits, and past it every parameter defect in order ([KF343](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf343)). Three members, three shapes, so the fourth cannot be inferred either.

**Each pass cost one grid and found one live over-report.** The message pass found the checker naming a parameter class beside a control character, where A12 names the character class alone — the forbidden direction, and invisible to every test the three families had, because each family only ever saw a text defective in its own way.

**Amended 2026-09-13 — the fourth member, and the first pass to find a MISSING class rather than an extra one.** An enumeration's value list reports every defect, like a field type ([KF344](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf344)) — but the pairing rows turned up `MVK_CATEGORY_VALUE_DUPLICATE`, which A12 draws for a repeated INTERNAL value while a category exists and which the checker did not emit at all. Read off its name it would have been implemented backwards: a category whose own tokens repeat is ADMITTED. So the pass finds over-reports and under-reports alike, and a class named for the member it is reported against is not evidence about what triggers it.

**Two things the passes have taught that are cheaper than the grid itself.** Measure the pair in BOTH orders: the reversed row is what separates "this class wins" from "the earlier defect wins", and its absence is what made [KF340](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf340) wrong. And measure the GRANULARITY rather than carrying it over: the condition gate is per declaration and the message gate is per message, so a checker that assumed either would have been wrong about the other — one row each, and both were nearly assumed.

**Amended 2026-09-13 — the fifth member, and a second missing class.** A group's two ordering members both report ([KF345](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf345)), and the pairing rows turned up `MVK_SORT_FIELD_DUPLICATE`, which the checker did not emit. The sort list also draws its type class ONCE however many entries are opaque, which the checker does not match — kept deliberately, because it is the count divergence [KF340](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf340) already settled rather than a class one, and a finding per entry names the field to fix.

**The tier trap is real and the grid has to be built around it.** An unresolvable ordering name stops the model BUILDING, so a row putting one opposite a type defect varies buildability rather than the member; those rows live in a test of their own and say so. That is the same "a control that moves two variables is not a control" rule, met in a member where it is easy to miss because both defects are spelled the same way.

**Amended 2026-09-13 — the sixth member closes the list, and the pass ends where it started: on a gap someone had disclosed rather than closed.** A rule's slots report in full ([KF346](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf346)), and the pairing grid ran into `RULE_HAS_NO_ERRORTEXT` — a class the checker deliberately withheld because "which shapes split that way is not measured", written as an honest disclosure and left standing. Enumerating the shapes took six rows and turned the disclosure into a rule with exactly one exception; the class is emitted now, and leaving it out had been an under-report on every shape but one.

**Two traps this member sprang that the earlier five had not.** The rule derived on a ONE-locale model is false on a two-locale one — whitespace suppresses the class only when every declared locale is answered that way — so the first implementation was measured wrong within minutes of being written. And the first version emitted the class wherever no locale was covered, including models with no usable declared locale at all, where A12 reaches no verdict; that is the checker stating its own reading as A12's, caught only because four existing mirror rows went red.

**The pass is complete: six members, six grids.** Three report everything (field type, enum values, group ordering, rule slots), one reports the first defect in its text (a condition), one layers a gate over a full report (a message). No member's answer could be predicted from another's, and every one of the six turned up something live.

### C record — the withholding's mechanism was WRONG, and the set that hid it was my own (2026-09-13)

**The previous record read the order as "reference resolution runs first and wins". It does not — it wins when it comes FIRST in the text** ([KF342](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf342)). Every pair the earlier grid held put the bad name at the head of the condition, so both accounts predicted all of them identically; one row with the position reversed separates them. This is the estate's own named confound — one constant across every row, invisible from inside the set — and it reached a shipped gate and three documents before the next slice's denominator work turned it up.

**The correction cost was small; the miss was not.** Naming only three syntax codes left EIGHT more condition-text classes live, each an over-rejection on a condition that also carries a bad name — the forbidden direction, shipped. They were found by enumerating what reads the condition text rather than by reasoning about the mechanism, which is why the denominator question is worth asking even when the account feels settled.

**A second gate came out of it, and it is shaped differently from every other one here.** `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED` and `MVK_ERROR_INVALID_LANGUAGE_FEATURE` are withheld by ANY other defect in the same text, wherever it sits, so their trigger is not a property of the model but of what the other families already said. That gate therefore reads the FINDINGS, not the model — the first one here that does.

**The under-report is deliberate and disclosed.** The gate is position-blind because a finding carries no offset into the condition text; threading one through eleven families is a larger change than the over-rejection it buys back, and the cost is naming the bad name where A12 names the other defect — one true thing either way. Recorded here rather than as a gap row because it is a bounded, stated divergence, not open work.

### C record — where the withholding STOPS, and the method blind spot it exposed (2026-09-13)

**A field type reports every defect it carries** ([KF341](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf341)) — the opposite of the two members above, measured hours apart on the same kernel. The stop is a property of particular members, so it is never assumed from a neighbour; the row exists so a later reader cannot generalise the withholding into silence on input A12 answers in full. Nothing needed changing in the checker, which is the point of measuring it.

**The real yield is a blind spot in the METHOD, not a defect in the code.** That row also produced a class neither defect draws alone — `noValueValidation` beside a `pattern`. Every other grid in this estate varies ONE defect at a time, so a combination-only class is structurally unreachable: not tried and found empty, never built. The three withholdings found today were all found the same way, by breaking two things at once.

**What follows is a standing candidate, not a claim:** the single-defect grids are the estate's dominant shape, and each one is a place where a stop or a combination-only class could be hiding. Worth a pass that pairs defects within each member, priced before it is started.

### C record — the THIRD withholding, inside a single member, and what the class is really about (2026-09-13)

**The two withholdings above were between members; this one is inside one.** A condition's unresolvable name withholds every syntax class over the same text ([KF340](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf340)). So the pattern is not "A12 stops between members" but "A12 examines in a fixed order and reports the first thing it finds", at every granularity looked at so far. *(Corrected 2026-09-13 by the record above: the order inside a condition is the TEXT's, not resolution-before-syntax, and the gate this record describes named three of the eleven codes it needed.)*

**Family-per-defect-kind is the design that meets this head on.** Eleven families read one condition text. The checker had no test at all for a text carrying two defects, so nothing was red — and the input is not exotic, it is what a half-written condition looks like.

**Recorded as the codes to DROP, not the codes to keep.** A family added later that reports a new syntax class is then merely an under-report until its code is listed; a keep-list would silently start dropping it the day it landed. The error-target gate opposite is a keep-list for the mirror-image reason, and the asymmetry is deliberate.

**What is still unexamined is the rest of the order.** Three withholdings have been found by asking, each time, "what else does A12 decline to say once this fails?" — and each was found by breaking two things at once, which no single-defect grid can see. The estate's grids are overwhelmingly single-defect.

### C record — the SECOND withholding, and a mirror that had contradicted its own laws test (2026-09-13)

**The sweep the previous record asked for found a wider one.** A rule whose error TARGET does not resolve draws that pair and nothing else — the condition's class and both error-text classes are withheld ([KF339](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf339)). So the first withholding was not a fact about error texts; A12 examines a declaration in ORDER and stops, and the error-text case was one instance.

**It is applied where the report is assembled, not inside a family.** A family-local version would have to be repeated into every family added later, and the one that forgot would over-reject silently — which is exactly how this defect survived. The trade is a blunt instrument: every finding on such a declaration is withheld but the target's own, which is conservative where the measurement stops.

**A checker mirror had asserted the union of both codes and sat green, contradicting its own laws test.** `FieldVariantFilterTest` expected `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_MISSING` **and** `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` with the comment "which is what A12 does"; [KF305](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf305)'s grid had measured that very row as the target pair alone and said so in ITS comment. Nothing in the estate compares a mirror against the laws test it mirrors, so two contradictory claims about one input were both green for as long as the mirror existed.
