That gap is worth a guard and is not one this slice adds. *(Narrowed 2026-09-13 — see the mirror-versus-laws record at the head of this log: the direct form proved undecidable without an authored exemption list, and the decidable half that shipped, `CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest`, would not have caught THIS mirror, because the code it over-asserted was measured elsewhere. What remains open is the scope-transfer residue.)*

**The scope question stayed open on purpose.** Whether the withholding reaches non-member defects was probed with an illegal rule name, which makes the model unbuildable — two variables — so it decides nothing and is recorded as a probe rather than a row.

### C record — the cross-member withholding, and the over-rejection three families had been shipping (2026-09-13)

**Found by reading the vocabulary rather than by a failing test.** `FehlertextErrorType.INVALID_CONDITION` says a parameter can only be checked where the condition is valid — so the question was whether the checker reports a parameter class on a model whose condition is broken. It did, and A12 withholds that class entirely ([KF338](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf338)).

**A family-per-member checker gets this wrong by construction.** Every family reads its own member and reports what it finds; nothing in that design expresses "A12 stops asking once a sibling member fails". The fix is a withholding rather than a new family, and it is the first cross-member dependency the estate carries.

**The sweep found the family shipped an hour earlier carrying the same defect.** The character law needs nothing from the condition, so there was no reason to expect it to be gated — and it is. Declaring the parameter fix done without running that row would have left a live over-rejection in a family whose own commit message claimed the over-rejection direction was what it was built to avoid. One shared gate now serves both, and any future family reading `errorMessage` inherits it.

**The shape is not exotic, which is what makes it worth the slice.** A half-typed condition is the ordinary state of a model being edited, and that is exactly when a checker runs. An over-rejection that only appears on malformed input is still an over-rejection on the input a checker most often sees.

**The parse half is deliberately under-covered.** This layer holds no grammar, so "does not parse" is stood in for by an unbalanced delimiter — a necessary condition. Balanced-but-refused text keeps its parameters judged, which can only miss a finding. `incompleteInput` was the first instrument reached for and is the wrong one: it fires only where delimiters ARE balanced, by its own design.

### C record — the forty-first family: the message text's control characters, the cheapest law on the surface (2026-09-13)

**The second pick off the unprefixed re-derivation, and it cost one run.** `INVALID_LINEBREAK` and `INVALID_CHARACTERS` are a character predicate over one string — no resolution, no model walk — and nine rows measured the whole law including both ends of the boundary. It is the cheapest family the checker has, and it was invisible for the same reason the rest of `FehlertextErrorType` was.

**The implementation is the ORDER, not the predicate.** A text carrying both a line break and a tab draws the line-break class ALONE. A reader reporting each kind it finds would over-report on exactly the text an author produces by pasting — the most likely real input on this surface, which is what makes the row worth its run rather than a completeness exercise.

**A repertoire row measured nearby answers a different question, and would have read as coverage.** `RuleMessageAdmissionGateLawsTest` already measured "which characters may a message carry" and found a CODELESS refusal at the BMP boundary. Every row there is printable; nothing in it reaches a control character, and its refusal carries no class at all. A grep for the topic would have returned it and it would have looked like the answer — the narrow-prior-art shape, met here in the estate's own record rather than in a peer's.

### C record — the fortieth slice: the message parameter's ADDRESS, and what the MVK-keyed enumeration could not see (2026-09-13)

**Selected by re-deriving the candidates against the UNPREFIXED vocabulary**, which the record below said had to happen. Reading `FehlertextErrorType` directly turned up six classes and a search of the whole estate — docs, tests, checker — found five of them mentioned NOWHERE: `INVALID_LINEBREAK`, `INVALID_CHARACTERS`, `ERROR_TEXT_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`, `INVALID_PATH_SPEC`, `INVALID_CONDITION`. The enumeration blind spot was not a near miss; it hid a whole surface's diagnostic vocabulary.

**It widened the thirty-ninth family rather than minting a fortieth**, because it is the same mechanism one step earlier: resolve the parameter's address, then judge membership. The address had been silently discarded, so three outcomes A12 answers went unreported — and the pick was made on the suspicion that the silence was instead an over-REPORT, since a checker resolving a bare name through a model-wide fallback would pick a candidate and judge membership against it. It was not: the resolver already returns `Reference.Ambiguous` and the family fell silent. The suspicion was still what found the three under-reports, which is the argument for chasing a possible over-rejection even when it turns out not to exist.

**The grid needed a THIRD kind of duplicate to say anything.** A name duplicated in a group the rule declares in is admitted, and so is the same name read from the other carrier — the local hit explains both. Only a name the declaring group does NOT own reaches the model-wide tier, and only a duplicated one of those is refused. A fixture with the first kind alone measures a local-first rule and a two-tier rule identically.

**The checker's fixture was missing the flag the law depends on.** `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` gates the second tier; the kernel fixture is built by `DocumentModelDraft` and carries it, the checker's hand-written JSON did not, so the mirror reported the unresolvable class where A12 reported the ambiguous one. A hand-built mirror of a drafted fixture silently differs in every default the draft supplies.

**The over-rejection the slice created, and the grid that caught it.** Reporting unresolvable addresses made `$BaseYear$` — a plain name that resolves to nothing — report `INVALID_ENTITY`, where A12 ADMITS it as a message-grammar terminal. The family's "forms this family does not read" grid failed on it immediately. That grid exists to keep disclosed silence honest; here it worked as a regression guard against a widening, which is the stronger job. The fix is the one exclusion this surface DOES justify — and note it is the exact shape removed two commits earlier as speculative: excluding CONDITION keywords was unmeasured and hid findings, excluding the MESSAGE grammar's terminals is measured and prevents an over-rejection. The lesson is not "exclusions are bad" but that an exclusion is worth exactly its measurement.

### C record — the thirty-ninth family: what an error message may name (2026-09-13)

**The first family reporting a class with no `MVK_` prefix**, and the one the record above predicted: `INVALID_FIELD` was invisible to the candidate enumeration because the ledger is keyed by `MVK_*` codes only. The kernel half was already measured by `RuleMessageParameterLawsTest`; what was missing was the boundary an implementation needs, which is now [KF335](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf335).

**The slice was scoped BEFORE it was opened, and the scope is most of the work.** A12's message grammar is a third DSL — a category suffix, a semantic-index key, a row specifier, a base-year terminal with a signed offset, its own quoting rule — and each form this reader did not recognise would have been reported as the membership failure, which is an over-rejection. So it reads the plain `$Name$` / `$Name.value$` form and yields nothing for everything else, with the silence locked by its own grid rather than left as an assumption.

**Three shapes that look exactly like the failure are a different class each.** A name resolving to nothing is `INVALID_ENTITY`; an unterminated parameter is `WRONG_NUMBER_OF_DOLLARS`; and a GROUP draws the membership class rather than `INVALID_GROUP`, so keying the class on the resolved element's kind is wrong in the other direction too. None of the three is guessable.

**A computation's message is gated as tightly as a rule's, by its own members** — its computed target plus what its preconditions and operations read. That was the reverse of the first two readings taken, both off a fixture that never landed: the first injected the computation with a regex that matched nothing, so four ADMITTED rows were measured against an unmodified model; the second gave the computation an operation reading its own target, so its control drew the self-read class. A positive control inside the grid — one row A12 must refuse — is what caught both, and it is why the control sits in the grid rather than beside it.

**One question turned out to be unaskable, which is a result and not a gap.** Separating "the condition reads it" from "it is the rule's error target" cannot be done: A12 refuses a rule whose error field its condition does not name, so the target is necessarily inside the membership set and there is no second term for an implementation to find.

### The candidate ENUMERATION had a blind spot, and it is the selection method rather than any one family (2026-09-13)

**Families 36–38 were all chosen from one diff: the ledger's `kernel-delegated` rows minus the codes the checker's main source emits.** That diff is a good instrument and it has a denominator bug — every ledger row is keyed by an `MVK_*` code, so a class A12 reports under any OTHER spelling can never appear in the residue, however reachable or valuable it is. It is not a small omission: the kernel declares **65 unprefixed classes** across its nine error-type enums, **49 of them the error-text parameter surface** that every rule's stored `errorMessage` meets ([KF323](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf323), derived and corrected in place the same day).

**That surface is measured on the kernel side and had no checker family — SHIPPED since, and the two paragraphs below are kept as the derivation that found it rather than as open work** *(closed 2026-09-14; the family is `MESSAGE_PARAMETER_REFERENCE` in [`Check.kt`](../consistency/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/consistency/Check.kt), landed with [KF335](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf335)/[KF336](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf336), and it took exactly the safe slice the next paragraph names — the plain form, every other shape falling through to silence)*. `RuleMessageParameterLawsTest`, `RuleMessageParameterLanguageLawsTest` and `RuleMessageAdmissionGateLawsTest` already hold grids over it — the membership gate that answers `INVALID_FIELD`, the quoting rule, the suffix forms, the semantic-index key. So it sits in the same bucket family 35 came from, which was the cheapest family so far: the kernel law already locked, only the portable half missing.

**It is deliberately NOT started here, and the reason is the convergence gate rather than its value.** A message parameter has many forms — a category suffix, a base-year terminal with a signed offset, a semantic-index key, an escaped `$$`, an exempt-terminal quoting rule — and each form this checker did not recognise would be reported as a bad field reference, which is an over-rejection, the one direction this project forbids. The safe slice is the plain `$Name$` / `$Name.value$` form with every other shape falling through to silence, and that slice needs its own acceptance matrix before it is opened rather than at the end of a long session.

**Re-derive the residue against BOTH vocabularies before picking the next family**, and read the tail-collision note in KF323 first: five unprefixed classes share their tail with an `MVK_` code, so a diff that strips prefixes merges laws that are not the same.

### C record — the thirty-eighth family: the circle a model's computations may not form (2026-09-13)

**This is the first family whose law is a GRAPH over the whole model rather than a property of one declaration**, and it was worth reaching for precisely because it looked relational and is not: the topological sort A12 runs reads only which field each computation writes and which fields its own members read, both of which a stored model spells out. Nothing about it needs a document, which is what moved it out of Order 6 and into reach ([KF334](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf334)).

**Two rows decided the implementation, and neither could have been reasoned to.** A read in an alternative's `precondition` builds an edge exactly like an operation read — the two members look nothing alike, and a field read is even spelled differently in each (`FieldFilled(Volume)` against `[Volume]`), so reading only `operation` is the natural implementation and would admit a model A12 refuses. And the two ends of a circle may sit in different groups, closed through an aggregate in one direction and an absolute read in the other, which is what licenses keying the graph on resolved paths alone.

**The pre-emption is the part a checker cannot infer.** A computation reading its own target is a cycle of length one in any graph an implementation would build, and A12 answers it with `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`. The self-loop is therefore dropped inside the edge builder rather than filtered at the report, so nothing downstream can reintroduce it. This checker does not carry the self-read law — its two pre-emptions need derived-scale inference, a standing gap — so silence there is an under-report, which is safe; answering with the cycle class would not have been.

**The measurement cost was two wrong probes before a right one, and both were spelling.** A field read inside an operation is `[Weight]`, not `Weight`: the first probe returned `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` on all four rows, which reads exactly like "the law does not fire here" and would have retired the candidate. Rendering one computation through the typed surface and printing what it produced settled it in one run — the estate's own DSL is the cheapest oracle for a spelling question, and guessing at one is how a grid measures the parser instead of the law.

### C record — the thirty-seventh family: the predicates a computation's precondition may not use (2026-09-13)

**The denominator was read from the Kernel's source, not from the ledger row that described it.** That row said "custom conditions, `RepetitionNotUnique`, or the legacy count-given op" and happened to be right; `NormalisierteBerechnungsFormHelper`'s own `if / else if / else if` chain is what settles it, and reading it also produced the fact the row could not have carried — that the third feature has no English spelling at all ([KF333](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf333)). Naming a subset as if it were the whole is the failure this estate keeps measuring, so the source read is the cheap half of the work, not the thorough-looking half.

**Two of three are reported and the third is a stated boundary.** The count-given op is a DE-only terminal with no catalog id, so an `en_US` model cannot reach that arm and this family has no measurement for it. Silence there is an under-report, which is always safe; a refusal with no measured basis is the one direction this project forbids, so the omission is recorded in the family's own KDoc rather than left to look like an oversight.

**The RULE control is what makes this a computation law.** Both predicates are ordinary in an `errorCondition` — measured — so a checker refusing them wherever it met them would refuse a legal model. This is the third consecutive family whose real content is a slot separation rather than a new shape, which is worth noticing: the stored members look alike by design, and every one of these families is one careless generalization from an over-rejection.

**The OPERATION row is the one that was nearly skipped, and it is the sharpest.** The precondition and the operation are two strings on the same alternative, read through the same code, and the filter keeping this family off the operation had no control until that row was measured. A12 answers the same predicate in an operation with `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` — its *grammar* vocabulary — so judging both slots alike would have produced the wrong class, not merely a finding too many. The checker under-reports that shape (a `Name(path)` operation is well-formed to the grammar family, and what A12 refuses is the operator's KIND — the standing arity/operand-kind gap), which is disclosed in the mirror test rather than hidden behind an empty expectation.

**The candidate recorded as NEXT was then investigated and declined, which is the cheaper half of the enumeration.** `MVK_REF_ERROR_ENTITY_IF_FILLED_NOT_REFERENCED` and its `_IF_NOT_FILLED_` twin were carried as "an `IfFilled` marker in the error text must name an entity the condition references". The kernel source says otherwise on both counts: the law is about two declared LISTS of relative paths, not message text, and a stored rule carrying either member is **UNREADABLE** — A12's deserializer refuses the property, so no consistency verdict is reached at all ([KF61](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf61), amended in place). Both ledger rows are corrected. That is the third ledger row this week whose stated LOCUS was wrong, after the three index-family rows — a pattern worth naming: a row written from a code's NAME rather than from its firing site reads as a law and points at nothing.

**Two pieces of speculative code came out in the same pass.** `namesKeyword` re-tested each spelling against `CONDITION_KEYWORDS` after the caller had already resolved it from `CONDITION_OPERATOR_KEYWORDS`, whose values are a subset of that table — a branch no model can take, and a mutant relaxing it would have survived for want of a control. The family's `isEmpty()` early return was the same thing one layer up: both ids exist in both languages, so it was dead. Replaced with `getValue`, which fails loudly if the generated table ever stops carrying them.

### C record — the thirty-sixth family: the index field a computation may not decide, and how the next family was CHOSEN (2026-09-13)

**The family itself is small; the way it was selected is the part worth recording.** Browsing the ledger for a likely-looking code is how the previous two were picked, and it wasted a whole laws test earlier the same day on a law already locked. This one came from an enumeration instead: every `MVK_*` the ledger calls `kernel-delegated` (121), minus every code the checker's main source emits (71), leaving a printed residue to read (104). *(Figures re-derived 2026-09-14; they previously read 107 and 65, and the gap is the PREDICATE rather than growth — name it when you re-derive, or you will get a third pair. `kernel-delegated` counts a ledger ROW whose bucket cell CONTAINS that word, so a row bucketed `pre-checked + kernel-delegated` is in; `emits` counts a `"MVK_..."` string LITERAL anywhere in `consistency/src/commonMain`, so a code named only in KDoc is out while one listed in a withholding set is in.)* Most of it is relational (iteration, semantic index, parallel iteration — Order 6) or per-operator typing, which is the standing gap; the local declaration laws stand out immediately. Re-derive that diff rather than trusting this paragraph; it is two commands.

**`MVK_ERROR_INDEX_AS_CALCULATED_FIELD` had never been put to the Kernel from either estate.** The ledger carried the law as prose, no lock named the code, and the measurement confirmed it reachable — a real verdict a consumer receives, not a build refusal like the group-valued target beside it ([KF330](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf330)).

**The SORT field is the whole reason this is a separate family and not a line in the last one.** `indexFieldName` and `sortFields[].name` are the same kind of member on the same group, resolved by the same code — this checker reaches both through one shared helper — so an implementation that refused a computed index field would refuse a computed sort field in the same breath. A12 admits the second. Without that row the family is one careless edit from an over-rejection, and nothing would have caught it.

**Two scope questions were measured rather than inherited.** Whether the law follows the computation's own group or its resolved TARGET: it follows the target, so keying on the resolved field alone is correct. And whether the rule slot answers the same way: it does not, which is the same slot-separation the thirty-fourth family measured, holding on a second law.

**The control needed a control, which is the recurring shape.** The first outside-the-group row wrote into the field its own operation sums, so it drew the self-read class instead of the locus under test — a control moving two variables, caught only because its verdict named a code that had nothing to do with indexes.

### C record — the thirty-fifth family: what `Length` over a raw-type field may look like, and the parenthesis that decides it (2026-09-13)

**The kernel law was already measured and locked; only the checker half was missing.** [KF35](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf35) and `NvvRawTypeLawsTest` have carried the raw-type reads since 2026-07-15, so this family needed no new kernel work for its core — which is what made it the cheapest family available and worth picking over a code with no measurement behind it.

**One row had to be measured anyway, and it inverted the obvious implementation.** The lock fixes the ACCEPTED set — `Length(f) > c` and the mirror, as the whole rule condition. A family keying on "the whole condition is that, else report" asserts the stronger claim that every OTHER layout is refused, which an accepted set can never establish. The complement grid measured it: **redundant parentheses are TRANSPARENT**, so `(Length(f) > 5)` is ADMITTED and the token-exact match a reader reaches for first would refuse a legal model. Both comparison reversals and `Or` are refused as expected; `Not(…)` turns out to be a GRAMMAR refusal one tier earlier and belongs to no raw-type gate. The ordinary-string control carries every one of those layouts and stays admitted, which is the axis separating the layout from the field.

**Two gates, reported in A12's order.** The shape is decided first and pre-empts the constant domain, where a decimal point or a signed-32-bit overflow draws `MVK_INTERNAL_ERROR`. Reporting the constant on a text that also fails the shape would name a code A12 never reached — measured, and the reason the family does not simply validate the constant wherever it finds one.

**A stale ledger row fell out of it.** `MVK_INTERNAL_ERROR` was recorded as *"not author-triggerable as far as measured, and not a structural law"*, while a lock in this repository had been measuring exactly that trigger since before the row was written. Corrected in place. That is the second time this week a ledger disposition has disagreed with a green lock beside it, both found by needing the fact rather than by auditing the ledger.

**What it deliberately does not report.** A12 closes the other value windows on a raw type with `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE` — a comparison, a value list, a computation's bare copy. Deciding those needs which operand POSITIONS of which operator are value positions, which is the per-operator catalog this plan does not reproduce. The class stays with the Kernel and is recorded rather than guessed at: silence under-reports, which is safe, and a guess would not be.

### C record — the thirty-fourth family: what a computation may compute INTO, and the twin slot that answers differently (2026-09-13)

**The family is a pair of measurements, not one.** A rule's `errorEntityRelPath` and a computation's `computedFieldRelPath` hold the same kind of value, are read by the same reader and resolve through the same code, so an implementation judges them alike unless a row says otherwise — and exactly one row does: a computation may not compute into a group, while a rule may report on one, fixed or repeatable alike ([KF329](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf329)). Either half alone licenses the wrong rule for the other slot, which is why the rule column is in the grid rather than cited from the family next door.

**The code it reports is NOT A12's key for the law, and the measurement is what decides that.** `MVK_GROUP_AS_CALCULATED_FIELD` exists, but the Kernel's builder refuses a group-valued computed field before `checkConsistency` runs, so no consumer ever sees that key from this shape. The report says `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` — the code the checker owns for precisely this tier, already carrying the computed field that names nothing. A family reaching for the `MVK_*` key because the law has one would have claimed a verdict A12 does not issue.

**It is another `erased` row that is live in stored DM-JSON**, the bucket that keeps producing families: the kits cannot author the shape because their computed-field slot is a typed field handle, and the checker reads a string where a group path is one edit away. The wildcard family landed from the same bucket the same day.

**The group KIND does not enter it.** Fixed and repeatable are refused alike, so the family needs no repetition reasoning — measured rather than assumed, because every other group law landed this week has turned on exactly that axis.

### The checker's standing over-rejection control, and what it found on its first run (2026-09-13)

**Thirty-three families had no control that could see a family firing on an ORDINARY model.** Each is locked against hand-written fixtures exercising the shape it judges, and a fixture written beside an implementation shares whatever that implementation assumes — which is exactly how the wildcard family's invented `repeatable` member passed every test it had. `adapter.laws.CheckerSilenceOnCleanModelsTest` supplies the missing direction: every committed model `BoundModel.ofJson` builds must draw no `A12_CONSTRAINT` ERROR from the checker. 40 of the 43 committed DM-JSON files qualify; the three that do not are include-bearing and need a resolver this sweep does not supply, so they drop out rather than needing an exclusion list.

**The Kernel picks the population, which is the property that makes the sweep maintainable.** Nothing here decides whether a model is valid — a deliberately broken fixture simply fails to build and is not measured — so adding a fixture never requires touching this test. A floor of 15 answers "did the sweep reach real models", deliberately far below the live 40 so it does not move as the estate grows.

**It found a real over-rejection on its first run, in the oldest family in the module.** `CustomCondition <Name>` names host code the consuming system registered ([KERNEL-SEMANTICS §14](KERNEL-SEMANTICS.md)), and the reference scanner resolved that name as an entity — so every model using the escape hatch drew `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`, and three committed ones do. The shape parser already knew the form (`applikationsBedingung`: a callable-condition keyword followed by a bare NAME rather than a call); the two readers had simply read the grammar differently, which is the class a single-reader estate cannot produce and a two-reader one produces silently. The fix makes the reference scan apply the parser's own test.

**A corpus arm was added the same day, and it is the half that matters.** The committed models were authored here, against this checker's own reading of A12 — a shared origin no number of them removes. The reference corpus (`a12-kernel-mm` — all 59 stored models, Kernel-expanded then Kernel-built, the same population the parser sweep holds) was written by A12's own team, and it immediately produced a row our grids structurally could not: `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED` on two rules whose condition pairs the `RuleGroup` self-reference with an ordinary operand. Every self-reference row we held carried the spelling ALONE, so "the self-reference reaches the rule's whole group" and "a condition naming no entity has nothing to compare" predicted the same verdict on all of them — the constant-across-the-set confound, and the corpus is what sat on the other side of the axis ([KF324](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf324), corrected in place).

**One of the eleven findings on that first corpus run was a regression this session had introduced minutes earlier**, which is the sharpest evidence for the control. The `CustomCondition` fix above had been written to reuse the SHAPE parser's test — any callable-condition keyword not followed by `(` takes the bare name behind it. That test is sound in the parser and unsound in the reference scan, because the parser applies it only where a condition is expected positionally while the scan is a flat token walk: the UNCATALOGED infix `In` swallowed its right operand, and nine rules across five models reported a phantom absolute reference. The committed arm was green throughout. Reusing a proved mechanism across a boundary is a claim, not an inheritance — and the tell was exactly the documented one, that the reuse needed no new branch.

**What the sweep did NOT reach, and the second arm that now does (closed 2026-09-13).** Every corpus model is handed to the checker already EXPANDED by the Kernel, so the checker's own include resolution — its `ConsistencyWorkspace` mount path, the one a TypeScript or browser consumer has no Kernel to stand in for — never ran against a real model; only the own-domain `WorkspaceMountTest` covered it, and the committed include-bearing models dropped out of the committed arm as well, because `BoundModel.ofJson` is called there without a resolver. `noFamilyReportsWhenTheCheckerMountsTheIncludesItself` closes it: the UNEXPANDED entry plus the sources its `header.modelReferences` name go through `ConsistencyWorkspace`, with the Kernel still picking the population from the expanded form.

**Its vacuity trap is the sharpest one this control has met, and the obvious guard does not work.** An unexpanded mount returns a report with no findings, which is indistinguishable from silence — and the STATUS cannot separate them, because a clean report is `Inconclusive` too (coverage stays incomplete while required families are unimplemented). A first version asserted on the status and failed on both models for that reason, which is the useful direction to fail in. `checksCompleted` is the discriminator: empty on that early return alone. A mutant that hands the workspace an empty provider map is caught by this arm.

**The population is two models and cannot be widened from the reference corpus — measured, not assumed (2026-09-13).** All 59 `a12-kernel-mm` document models are self-contained: not one declares a `header.modelReferences` entry, so the corpus that gives the other arms their independence contributes nothing at all to this one. The two committed include-bearing models are the whole available population, and they cover both purposes (`include` and `typeDefinitions`) with one real group mount between them. Widening it means authoring own-domain multi-model fixtures, not finding more real ones.

**The lane is scoped to that one class, not to `:adapter:test`.** It reads three committed model trees as FILES, so they are its declared inputs, and the broad lane excludes it — the pattern TESTING-SPEC requires for any test that reads repo files at runtime. `test` still depends on it, so a full run and CI drive it unchanged.

### C record — the thirty-third family: a repetition wildcard on something with no repetitions, and a DM-JSON member that does not exist (2026-09-13)

**The first family that judges a REFERENCE rather than an operand, and the measurement is what licenses that.** On a REPEATABLE group the same star draws three different answers by position — `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` in a scalar slot, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` in a quantifier's operand list, admitted in a count or an aggregate — so a family that ignores position is exactly the shape that should be wrong. On a non-repeatable one every position collapses to `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD` ([KF328](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf328)): eight positions across two carriers, each beside its repeatable control. Per-position independence is what a reference-level reading assumes, so it was measured rather than inherited from the two positions that motivated the family.

**It outruns the entity gate, which is the opposite order from every other pair measured in this cluster.** A starred fixed group followed by a leaf that resolves to nothing draws this class and says nothing about the leaf — so the family does not wait for the whole reference to resolve; it resolves each starred SEGMENT's own prefix and judges that. Every other law landed here pre-empts on an unresolvable operand instead.

**The defect this family produced is the worst kind available in this work: an invented DM-JSON member, agreed on by the implementation and by its own evidence.** The repetition test read `"repeatable": true`. A12 has no such member — it is `repeatability`, an integer whose default is 1, and `ModelDocument` tests `> 1`. Every test passed, because the hand-written fixtures beside the implementation carried the same invention, so the two agreed with each other and not with A12. On a real model the family would have reported `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD` for every CORRECTLY starred group — the over-rejection direction this project forbids, reached while implementing a family whose entire purpose is to catch a wrong star. Nothing in the family's own tests could have caught it; it went red only when the family was wired in and a fixture written for something else disagreed.

**So the guard that follows DERIVES the accepted spelling instead of listing it.** `FixtureRepetitionMemberGuardTest` drafts a repeatable group through the real emitter, reads back which member carries the count, and then requires every repetition-shaped member name in the checker's sources to be exactly that one — `maxRepetitions` and any other plausible twin fail without anyone enumerating them, and a rename in the kits moves the guard with it. The asymmetry it closes is structural rather than careless: the law fixtures are drafted through `DocumentModelDraft` and the unit fixtures are hand-written JSON, and only an oracle keeps those two spellings together.

**Extended 2026-09-13 — the two shapes it left alone were then MEASURED, and the family names all three classes.** The first pass declined them because A12's ledger gives each its own code and naming the wrong class on a correct rejection is still a wrong finding. One grid settled both: the rule-context entity draws `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_FOR_RULEGROUP` and a leading up-step draws `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_REL` ([KF328](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf328), extended in place). Two of its rows are pre-emptions wearing a measurement's clothes — from a root-level rule the DEPTH class answers first, and a mid-path `..` is refused by the parser, starred or not — and each carries the unstarred control that says so, which is what keeps them out of the wildcard column.

**Each of the two new classes needed a question the reference list cannot answer, and for the same reason.** The rule-context spelling is a KEYWORD, so a starred one never appears as a reference at all and the family asks the condition TEXT; a reference that is nothing but up-steps names no entity, so it is not reported as a reference either — that one stays a disclosed under-report rather than a second text-level question. This is the third family in two days whose law turns on a spelling `referencesIn` deliberately drops, which is a property of that reader worth naming rather than rediscovering.

**And the grid exposed a parser over-rejection in passing.** `GroupFilled(..*)` is refused by A12 SEMANTICALLY, so it parses — while the checker's parser took `..` and `*` as a product, which fails outright with nothing after the star and, worse, *succeeds* with a path after it. The two readers therefore disagreed about the same text in both directions. The path production now takes a wildcard after an up-step; the 657-condition corpus sweep is unchanged, as a strictly looser parser must be.

**One shape is still left alone**: a star on a named turning point (`..Ancestor*`), whose class is unmeasured. Silence is the answer where the class is in doubt, because a wrong code on a correct rejection is indistinguishable to a reader from a wrong rejection.

### C record — the thirty-second family: a FIELD where a group predicate refuses one, and an ERASURE's reason found false (2026-09-13)

**The family's content is which carriers do NOT have the gate.** `MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED` reads like a law about group predicates, and five of the eight carriers admit a field operand outright: only `NumberOfFilledGroups` and the two scalar presence predicates refuse one ([KF327](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf327)). A family assembled from the operators' names would have refused five legal shapes — the same failure the enum-value-token family met one week earlier, and the reason a carrier set is measured rather than derived.

**It also corrected a Java-side erasure's stated reason, which is the `erased` bucket's characteristic failure.** The ledger recorded the typed `GroupRef`/`FieldRef` split as resting on "group predicates take groups only". That sentence is false, so the erasure had no basis; it now carries the measurement instead, and stands, because the split bites on the AUTHORING path alone — no read path reconstructs typed operands from a stored condition, so a model carrying the shape stays readable. That is the sharper half of the client-side-refusal rule, checked rather than assumed.

**Three laws now share one call and one pass, each with its own carrier set and none a subset of another.** Duplicate and overlap run over all six group-list carriers, the root law over those six split three-three, and the kind law over a set of three that includes one group-list carrier and two scalars the others never see. They are ordered because A12 orders them, and the order was measured end to end rather than composed from the individual findings.

### C record — the thirty-first family: a ROOT group where the carrier does not admit one (2026-09-13)

**It rides the thirtieth family's capability and lands the day after it for that reason.** The law is per call and per operand, so it was unreachable before the argument list was; once [`callsIn`] existed the whole of KF185's root half became implementable, and the measurement that had been deferred as "needs operator identity" took one grid.

**The carriers split six ways into two threes, and the split does not follow their names.** `AllGroupsFilled`, `NotAllGroupsFilled` and `GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled` refuse a root operand outright, sole or not; `AtLeastOneGroupFilled`, `NoGroupFilled` and `NumberOfFilledGroups` admit a sole root and refuse one beside anything else. Two of those six had never been put through the gate, and `NoGroupFilled` — whose name pairs it with the refusing three — sits with the admitting three.

**The wildcard asymmetry is the trap, and it runs against the family landed the day before.** A starred operand is taken OUT of the duplicate comparison and left fully inside the root gate; an implementation that reused the skip would admit `AllGroupsFilled(/Batches*, Billing)`, which A12 refuses. The two laws share an operand list, a file and a call, and disagree about the same token.

**The locus question was the one that decided whether this family was implementable at all, and the answer is that it does not arise.** An unstarred repeatable co-operand makes A12 report its wildcard class from the outside anchoring and the root class from the inside — so the root violation holds in both and only the reporting ORDER moves. Had the verdict itself moved, the family would have needed iteration analysis the checker does not have, and would have been recorded as a gap instead.

**Ordering is the whole of the interaction between the two laws**, so they are evaluated in one pass: both duplicate classes pre-empt the root classes, and a root beside its own descendant is an overlap. Splitting them across two passes would have needed one to know the other's verdict.

### C record — the thirtieth family: a group list that names one group twice, and the first family that needed a PARSE (2026-09-13)

**This family could not be built on the reference scan every earlier one used.** The same group named once in each of two `AllGroupsFilled` calls is ADMITTED and named twice in one is refused, so a condition's flat set of names cannot decide the law — the argument list has to be recovered per call. The syntax layer already had a parser for the grammar family, so the capability is a collector on it (`callShapes`) plus one interpreter that turns each argument's token run into a path (`callsIn`), rather than a second grammar. A text that does not parse yields no calls at all: a truncated argument list is a SHORTER list, which is exactly the shape that makes an operand law look satisfied.

**Operator identity is read from the catalog, not carried here.** The keyword generator now also emits `CONDITION_OPERATOR_KEYWORDS`, so the family names its six carriers by catalog id once and reads a German model through the same table. Keyed by id rather than by keyword because only that direction is a function — `Valid` and `ValidType` share a spelling in both languages.

**Two rows run against each other and neither alone licenses the right implementation.** A relative path beside the ABSOLUTE spelling of the same group is an exact duplicate, so comparing authored text misses it; `Garage` and `GarageBay` are admitted side by side, so comparing text prefixes refuses a legal model. Identity is the resolved element and containment is by path step.

**The starred skip is the over-rejection guard and it was a genuine surprise.** `NumberOfFilledGroups(Claims*, Claims*)` names one repeatable group twice and A12 ADMITS it — the same skip the field-list carriers make, now measured on the group list. An implementation comparing every operand refuses that model. The mixed spelling never reaches the gate either: A12 answers it with `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, and skipping the starred operand leaves a single operand here, so the silence follows from the one rule rather than from a second.

**The one deliberate divergence is in the safe direction and is stated.** A12 stops at the first violation in a condition; this family reports every violating call. Each such call is a violation A12 reaches as soon as the earlier one is fixed, so the extra finding is a truth about the model rather than a refusal A12 would not make.

**Scope is the group list and nothing else.** A12 draws the same two codes on field lists and value lists; that is a different carrier with its own measurement, and the ledger row now says which half is audited.

### C record — the twenty-ninth family: a rule whose condition never names the field it reports on (2026-09-13)

**The gate was locked on one row and that row was the wrong half for a checker.** A condition naming a sibling draws `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`, which establishes that the gate exists; what an implementation needs is the opposite — which spellings SATISFY it — because every spelling it fails to recognise becomes a refusal of a model A12 admits. This is the first family in the set whose entire risk sits on recall rather than on precision.

**The containment relation had to be measured in both directions, and they disagree.** Naming the GROUP a target field sits in reaches the target, so comparing the target's own path refuses legal models; naming a FIELD inside a target group does not reach it, so treating containment as symmetric misses real violations. One row each, and either alone licenses the wrong implementation.

**One shape is deliberately under-reported and one is pre-empted.** A reference inside a `Having` filter does not satisfy A12's gate, but the names a filter contributes are indistinguishable from an operand's once condition text is scanned — so they are treated as reaching and the family stays silent. An unresolvable operand pre-empts the gate in A12 and does so here too, which keeps one mistake from drawing two fixes.

**And the recall risk is bounded by a test the reference extractor is not part of**: nothing is reported unless the target's own name is absent from the condition text other than as a path prefix. A spelling the extractor cannot parse therefore cannot produce a refusal — it produces silence, which is the direction this project allows.

### C record — the twenty-eighth family: a group named in a condition that carries no field (2026-09-13)

**The family judges a REFERENCE, and the reason is the whole point of the law.** A group with nothing beneath it is legal to declare and the model stays valid — it is the ordinary state of a model mid-edit, between `group add` and `field add` — so a checker reporting the declaration would refuse the obvious authoring order. What A12 refuses is every condition that NAMES such a group, and that is what this family reports, on the rule or computation carrying the text.

**Emptiness is over the SUBTREE, and that row is the family's over-rejection guard.** A group with no direct field but one populated descendant is admitted in every position an empty one is refused, at any depth. An implementation testing direct children passes every violation row and refuses two legal models, which is why the shell rows sit beside each violation in both estates' grids.

**The position question was the one a checker cannot answer, so it was measured instead of assumed.** Reading condition TEXT yields the entities a condition names, never which operand slot each occupies — so "a condition naming an empty group is refused" had to hold beyond the group operators the existing grid covered. It does: a field-list operator and an index position draw the same class, and in the index case it PRE-EMPTS the wildcard class that refuses the populated control there. Without that measurement the family would have been reporting a class A12 might not have issued in two of the positions it sees.

**The class is the carrier's again**, as it is for the name laws landed the same day: an empty ROOT group answers with `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_IS_EMPTY` and any group below it with `MVK_GROUP_IS_EMPTY`. That is the third law in one session where a root group parts from the groups under it, which is now the first thing to check when a group law is transferred.

### C record — the twenty-seventh family: an enumeration's value tokens, where the finding is how LITTLE A12 checks (2026-09-13)

**Six of this family's rows are ADMITTED, and that is its content.** An empty value token, a token with a space and a 300-character token are all models A12 takes, while four codes — `MVK_VALUE_INVALID`, `MVK_VALUE_TOO_LONG`, `MVK_VALUE_TOO_SHORT`, `MVK_VALUES_DUPLICATE` — read as though they governed exactly those shapes ([KF321](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf321)). A family assembled from the code names would have refused four legal models; the only token law is that they must be distinct, and A12 reports that under the INTERNAL name.

**The label law needed two rows that contradict the two obvious readings, and neither follows from the other.** Labels written only for an UNDECLARED locale are admitted, although no declared locale is answered — which kills "is this locale answered". Labels present for the declared locale with EVERY text blank are refused, although again no locale is answered — which kills "is any label present". What the rows support is *once any value carries an entry for a declared locale, every value owes a non-blank text for every declared locale*, and that is published as the observed law with no mechanism attached to the split.

**A cleared client-side refusal, stated because clearing is only cheap once it has been measured.** `DocumentModelDraft` refuses a partially-labelled enumeration at author time and cited a code from a comment; the grid is now its basis and the refusal stands unchanged. That is the same value the pattern audit had either way — the audit's worth does not depend on it finding something.

**It also forced a scope correction in a sibling test rather than in source.** The supported-characters family's multiplicity fixture declares one offending value twice, which this family now also refuses; counting every code there would have made that test's subject depend on which unrelated family landed next, so it filters to its own.

### C record — the twenty-sixth family: a number type's digit pair, whose law is not the comparison its name implies (2026-09-13)

**The family exists because the obvious implementation is wrong in both directions at once.** A12's fractional-digit rule reads an ABSENT maximum as 0 — so `minFractionalDigits: 2` with no maximum is refused — and takes the pair out of the check entirely when the maximum is NEGATIVE, so the same minimum beside an explicit `-1` is admitted ([KF320](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf320)). A checker comparing the two members as declared admits a model A12 refuses and refuses one A12 takes, and only the two rows that split absent from negative can show it.

**The value range beside it is the plain comparison, and is stated separately for that reason.** Nothing about the digit pair predicts it; the two share a carrier and not a law, which is the same shape the enum-category and supported-character families already met.

**The scale cap rides here as a BUILD-tier verdict rather than a finding.** A `maxFractionalDigits` above 14 leaves A12 able to read the model and unable to build it, and A12 has no code for that at all — so silence would present an unusable model as clean. The checker reports `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, which is the tier it already owns.

**The same grid corrected two client-side refusals in `NumberSpec`, one in each direction.** It refused a negative digit count that A12 admits — on the read path too, so a legal stored model could not be inspected — and it compared the pair only when both members were set, so the builder accepted a shape the kernel then refused, because the emitter always writes the maximum. Both are fixed in the same change as the family that measured them; the scale cap the same record enforces is confirmed rather than corrected, with the transitivity argument's second link now measured instead of reasoned.

### C record — the twenty-fifth family: a string type's own parameters, and the SENTINEL that had been read as a number (2026-09-13)

**Five codes taken as one topic, because four of them hang off one member.** `noValueValidation` excludes a pattern and a minimum length, forbids a non-empty hint list, and requires the line-break flag; the length ordering sits beside them on the same two members ([KF319](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf319)). Measuring any of them alone produces a law that is false about the others, which is the batch-by-topic rule the rounding pair already paid for.

**The controls are where this family's risk sits, not the violations.** `maxLength` beside `noValueValidation` reads like the obvious third exclusion and is ADMITTED; an EMPTY hint list is admitted where a filled one is refused; a maximum of one is admitted without line breaks and line breaks are admitted with a maximum of two. Every one of those is a model A12 takes, so a family assembled from the code NAMES rather than from the grid would have refused four of them. The `template` the earlier ledger gloss named is not even a member of this type.

**The finding under the finding: `-1` is A12's own "no bound", and this repository's Java kit read it as a negative one.** `IFieldType.UNDEFINED` is `-1`, so a model may spell an unset length by omitting the member or by writing the sentinel. `FieldTypeSpec` compared the bounds as plain numbers, so `minLength: 2, maxLength: -1` — a minimum with no maximum — was refused as an inverted range, and since the reader builds one of those records for every declared field, a model A12 accepts could not be INSPECTED through the kit. Third occurrence of [KF216](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf216)'s class and, again, the read path was the sharper half.

**It also corrected a row the over-rejection audit had CLEARED.** [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md)'s 2026-07-25 audit lists "the kernel enforces both inverted-bound refusals modelkit makes" among its cleared findings; that holds only where both bounds are real numbers, and nothing in the audit said so. A clearance is a claim with a denominator like any other, and this one's was the rows it happened to try.

**The sibling carrier was measured, and it diverges.** `CustomFieldType` carries the same two member names and the same client-side refusals. The sentinel behaves identically there — so the fix covers both sites — but an inverted range of two REAL bounds leaves A12 unable to build the model at all, with no code, where the string type reports `MVK_MIN_LENGTH_BIGGER_MAX_LENGTH`. Carrying the string type's row across would have stated a code A12 never emits on that carrier.

### C record — the twenty-fourth family: the pattern's error MESSAGE, and the comment that had asserted it (2026-09-13)

**The family was chosen because the family before it had already written the law down without measuring it.** [KF165](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf165)'s grid supplies an `errorMessage` beside every pattern, under a fixture comment saying A12 requires one — a claim no row of that grid could test, because not one of them varied the message. That is the strengthening-edit register: a sentence that decides what NOT to check, carried by rows measured for something else. Varying it turned out to expose two things the comment did not say.

**The pairing is one law with two directions, and the code names do not say which is which.** `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_AND_PATTERN_INVALID` rides the pattern and `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_AND_NO_PATTERN` rides the message; they are near-anagrams, and only the grid states the direction. A type-level message with no pattern is refused just as firmly as a pattern with no message ([KF318](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf318)).

**A message "exists" where it REACHES a declared locale, not where the member is present — and that corrected the first prediction.** An empty list, a blank text and a text for an undeclared locale each leave `errorMessage` there and give A12 nothing, so each refuses a pattern beside it; the undeclared-locale row is the one that separated *counted* from *collected*, and it was predicted ADMITTED. Running the same three weakenings on the OTHER arm then showed all three ADMITTED without a pattern, which is what says one predicate serves both directions — the implementation needs no branch, and a checker built from the pattern arm alone would have guessed.

**Every row of that first grid shared one constant: a single-locale header.** On such a model "every declared locale answered" and "some declared locale answered" are indistinguishable, so the bilingual axis was run rather than reasoned about — and a THIRD code appeared, `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_FOR_LANGUAGE_MISSING`, the same one a rule's own message draws. It fires independently of the pattern: a half-translated message with no pattern draws it and the message code together. A consumer keying on the two pattern codes would read a bilingual gap as clean.

**One reader now serves both carriers, and the measurement is why rather than the member name matching.** A12's boundary between binding and refusing does not follow the shape a reader would guess — a SCALAR at `errorMessage` binds and collects nothing, while a non-object ENTRY refuses the source outright — and the string-type carrier was measured to agree with the rule carrier there. Only then was the reader extracted to `ErrorMessages.kt`; the locale-collection rules (declared-only, last-wins, blank-is-absent) came with it, so the two carriers cannot drift apart on a law that is easy to restate slightly wrong.

### C record — the twenty-third family: a declared `pattern`, and the estate's own preflight it falsified (2026-09-13)

**The family was chosen because its gate is TEXTUAL, so a portable checker can reproduce it exactly.** A12 puts a declared `StringType.pattern` through two stages: Java regex compilability, and a finite blacklist applied to the pattern's source characters ([KF165](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf165)). The second is pure text and reproduces without an engine; the first belongs to one engine and is left to A12 with a gap that says so, raised only when the model actually declares a pattern.

**The grid was measured at the DECLARED locus because the estate's account came from the other one.** [`PatternLawsTest`](../src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/PatternLawsTest.java) covers a pattern literal inside a condition, and everything written about the gate was written from there. The field locus turns out to raise a **different code** — `MVK_PATTERN_INVALID`, the condition's two words swapped — so a consumer keying on `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN` sees nothing at a declared pattern. Nothing about a shared check suggests two codes; only running it does.

**Two defects in our own `KernelPatternPolicy`, in opposite directions, both invisible without this grid.** It rejected `\P{L}`, which A12 admits — an over-rejection that fired on the READ path too, so a legal stored model could not be loaded through `FieldTypeSpec`. And it admitted `a\*+`, which A12 refuses, because it consumed escapes while scanning and so read the pattern rather than the text. The first came from a 2026-07-20 measurement whose MECHANISM was wrong: `\P{L}+` really is refused, by the `}` before `+`, and the escape plays no part — the discriminator holds the escape fixed and varies the quantifier, and it had never been run. Both are now held to the kernel row-for-row in both directions, and both replant as mutants that die.

**The scan itself became the second `:predicates` leaf, and the estate's own guard is what required it.** Writing the family produced a second copy of the blacklist, and `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` refused the landing until the pair carried a disposition — which is the guard doing exactly its job, since the previous pair of copies had drifted in both directions unnoticed. `kernelPatternBlacklist` is now one live statement both estates call, `:core` gained its first project edge to reach it, and the leaf's own rows run on JS where the kernel grid cannot.

**A wrong mechanism travels further than a wrong measurement.** That single sentence had reached five surfaces — two findings docs, an API finding, a review record, the shipped diagnostic enricher, the operator catalog's two gotchas and three CLI schema descriptions — because each copy cited the one that came before it. Correcting the family meant correcting all of them, which is the cost the claim-kind rule exists to avoid.

### C record — the twenty-second family: the model's character restriction, whose SCOPE was the whole question (2026-09-13)

**The reachability was already measured and the two questions a consumer needs were not.** `checkConsistency` re-runs the validation code generator, so a model restricting `supportedCharacters` to `A-Z` refuses an enum value `beta` — that row sat in [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md)'s scope-boundary note. It says nothing about WHICH declared strings the gate reads or what a definition entry MEANS, and both are load-bearing for a family that reports.

**The scope answer is narrow, and the wrong answer was the plausible one.** The identical refused spelling is ADMITTED in an element name, an error message and an enum category token under the identical restriction ([KF317](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf317)). A family that read the restriction as "the model's text" — which is what the member's own name suggests — would have refused three models A12 takes.

**Two rows that no reading could have supplied.** The multiplicity is per DISTINCT character and the distinctness is scoped to the VALUE: `aa` draws one diagnostic, while `ab` and `ba` in two values draw four. And the restriction belongs to the EXPANDED model rather than the declaring file — a provider carrying no restriction of its own has its enum values judged by the host's once mounted, measured on a two-model fixture rather than assumed from the codegen mechanism.

**The definition language is read only as far as it is measured.** Singletons and inclusive ranges are judged; a COMBINED grapheme entry — more than one code point — is legal and its membership test is a walk over code-point SEQUENCES, so the family leaves the WHOLE model alone rather than reading such a restriction per character; the alternative narrows a restriction the checker cannot read into one it can, which is the over-rejecting direction. *(Corrected 2026-09-13: this said "atomic", which is the kernel's name for the opposite case. `CharsetUtil.parse` puts an ATOMIC grapheme into the ranges array as a range of length 1 — so a precomposed `é` is read here — and only `isCombinedGrapheme` populates the sequence structure. The first test row for it was spelled with a literal accented character and so measured this repository's own file normalization rather than the branch it named; the rows now build their code points explicitly and pin both sides of the boundary.)*

### C record — the twenty-first family: an Enumeration's categories, whose oracle had been sitting green for two weeks (2026-09-13)

**It was found by deriving the shortfall rather than by picking a family.** Subtracting the codes the checker emits from the codes the `validate/laws` tier measures leaves a list; most of it is operator semantics the checker deliberately does not reproduce, but the enum-category pairing sat there with a complete, committed, green oracle ([KF216](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf216), measured 2026-08-29) and no consumer. That subtraction is worth repeating: it is a derived denominator for *what to build next*, in the same register the family denominators use.

**The gap sentence written an hour earlier was already wrong, which is the lesson.** It said an `EnumerationType`'s `values` had "no measured content law at all" — a negative existential asserted without the enumeration the rule demands, and refuted by a file in this repository. Corrected in place, and the family built rather than the sentence patched.

**What genuinely needed measuring was the WIRE SHAPE, and it answered more than was asked.** The law was established through the typed kit, so it said nothing about which stored members carry it. Each of the four names written nearly right makes the model UNREADABLE — A12's reader refuses a member it does not know and names the six it accepts — where the prediction was an over-permissive reader silently dropping it. So the pairing can only ever be asked of a body whose member names A12 has already accepted, and a checker reading the wrong one is looking at a model A12 would never have loaded.

**The multiplicity is the part a family could quietly get wrong.** A12 reports once per unpaired element, so two values under an empty token list draw two findings; the kit's own builder collapses both codes into one message, which under-reports and is recorded as such in the divergence guard. A checker inheriting that shape would have reported a different fact while looking correct.

### The DATERANGE pair — the first CONTENT law past the member A12 builds from, and the first table whose staleness would over-reject (2026-09-13)

**The gap that named it had gone stale in half and was a to-do in the other half — the fifth time this session.** It cited two rows: a `DateFragmentType` asking for a 1900 check on a year-free format, which the nineteenth family had already started reporting, and a `DateRangeType` whose format is not admitted with its separator, whose oracle was sitting in a derived artifact nobody had consumed. Re-aimed rather than deleted: what remains genuinely unmeasured is the CONTENT of an `EnumerationType`'s `values` and a `CustomFieldType`'s `name`, and a declared `format` outside the admitted vocabulary, which A12 refuses and this checker cannot report because the refused set is a probe's universe rather than a law.

**The measurement the artifact could not supply was the TIER.** [TEMPORAL-FORMAT-VOCABULARY](TEMPORAL-FORMAT-VOCABULARY.md) already derived the eight admitted `(format, separator)` pairs, but it asks the kernel a boolean, and a consumer that reports a refused pair has to know whether A12 examined the declaration at all. It is the build tier, with no consistency code ([KF316](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf316)) — so the check belongs to the field-type family, which already owns that tier, rather than to the date-config family, whose findings are A12's own verdicts. The same grid settled what stored JSON can ask and the typed builder cannot: an omitted `rangeSeparator` is the kernel's `/`, and an explicit `""` is a different declaration with admitted pairs of its own.

**This is the first checker table whose going stale would OVER-reject, so containment was the wrong guard.** Every other registered vocabulary claimant is protected by "does it name every admitted member", which catches a short list. Here a short or wrong entry makes the checker refuse a pair A12 accepts, and containment cannot see an entry that is present and wrong. The guard therefore re-derives the whole table from the kernel and compares it entry for entry, in both directions — including the seven formats mapped to NO admitted separator, which are measurements (refused at all seven probed separators) rather than absences.

**The checker's own format partition was an unregistered claimant, and registering it was the cheaper half of the same defect.** It carries the thirteen admitted formats split by whether they bear a year, lives in a module that cannot see the kernel, and nothing tied it to the derivation — the shipped shape of the `dd.MM.` incident. A planted mutant dropping one member is now caught.

### C record — the twentieth family: a declared NAME's alphabet, which turned out to be two laws (2026-09-13)

**The slice began as a transfer and the measurement refused it.** A group-name alphabet was already measured — two gates, the second of which the kernel's own message contradicts — and the obvious family applies it to every element. Run row for row on a FIELD, five of thirteen rows differ ([KF315](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf315)), and one differs in the forbidden direction: a field named `_Slots` is ADMITTED where a group is refused. A single-alphabet family would have rejected a model A12 takes, and nothing downstream could have caught it.

**Neither carrier is the stricter one, which is what makes the divergence hard to notice.** The group refuses the leading underscore a field takes and admits the non-ASCII letter a field refuses; the 60-character limit is the group's alone. The `_A12_DV` codes name the mechanism — a field's name answers to a second validator the group's does not reach — and that is visible only once both grids exist.

**The character denominator is derived rather than sampled.** Every printable ASCII character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]` was put to the kernel on both carriers, 33 rows each, with the expectation written per OUTCOME. That is what turned up the third answer: `/` draws `MVK_PATHS_INCONSISTENT` rather than a bad-name code, because a name carrying the separator makes the element's own path disagree with the tree it sits in. A hand-picked punctuation list would have had a hyphen, a space and a colon in it, and would have missed that entirely.

**What the family deliberately leaves to A12**, all under-reporting: a non-ASCII character on either carrier (legal on a group; exactly one measured on a field, so refusing the class would rest on one row), the group's length limit — which A12 refuses with no code at all, a shape this checker has no slot for — and the second code a digit-initial field name draws beside the reported one.

### C record — the nineteenth family: a date field's CONFIG against its FORMAT, and the vocabulary that made it safe (2026-09-13)

**The first family whose whole risk is over-rejection on a model A12 BUILDS.** Every earlier family either reported a model A12 could not load or reproduced a parse verdict. This one reports two of A12's own consistency codes on models the kernel reads, builds and then rejects — so a wrong row here is a legal model refused, with nothing downstream to catch it.

**The restriction that makes it safe is the format vocabulary, and it is asked as a membership test rather than as a predicate.** Both gates fire only over the thirteen formats A12 is measured to admit; an unrecognised format leaves the family silent, because that set is a probe's result over a candidate universe and is explicitly not closed ([KF313](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf313) found a thirteenth the same day). Deriving "carries a year" or "is a complete date" from the string instead would make the checker agree with its own reading of the format rather than with A12 — which is the reason the laws tests list both sets as measured, and their own doc says a client-side refusal may key on nothing wider.

**The carrier denominator is three and the gate applies to two.** `youngerThan1900Check` is declared by `DateType`, `DateFragmentType` and `DateRangeType` — derived from the kernel's `fieldtypes/` package, not surveyed — and the range carrier is measured EXEMPT: every admitted pair takes the check, the year-free ones included. A family that read the member wherever it found it would refuse a model A12 admits, and this is the second member this session whose law differs by carrier, after `variantFilter`.

**The member NAMES were measured, not read off the Java fields**, and the grid carries a nearly-right spelling as its control ([KF314](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf314)). That row also extended a known law to a new carrier: a field TYPE's body refuses a member A12 does not declare, exactly as the field's own body does, so a misspelling cannot masquerade as the real member.

**Two guards did work no reviewer would have:** the divergence guard refused the landing until both codes carried a recorded disposition against their rulekit statements — they are `PROSE_ONLY` there, the enricher's correctives, and rulekit's own declining of a client-side refusal is about its READ path, which the checker does not have. And a surviving mutant showed the vocabulary restriction is load-bearing on the precision gate alone, the pre-1900 one being bounded already by its own membership test; the missing control is now a row.

### A field type's body — the member A12 builds from, derived from the BUILDER rather than the fields (2026-09-13)

**The gap named a shape and an example, which made it a to-do rather than a limit — the fourth this session.** "A body carrying some members but not the one its type constructs from (an `EnumerationType` with `alphabeticalSorting` and no values)" is a sentence that describes a test nobody had run. It is now run, and the emptiness test it justified is gone.

**The denominator is derivable, which is the whole reason a refusal may name a member at all.** These classes take their arguments through a Lombok `@Builder` on a private constructor, and an argument is optional exactly where the hand-written `…Builder` class pre-initialises its field — explicitly, with a comment saying why. So the required member is the `@NonNull` constructor parameter the builder leaves alone, and there is exactly one per type ([KF312](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf312)). A survey of members someone happened to try would have been the wrong instrument for a check that refuses.

**`EnumerationType` is where reading the FIELDS instead of the builder gets it wrong.** `values` carries an initialiser, so it looks defaulted; the builder writes the field through the constructor and defaults every other `@NonNull` parameter and not that one. The measurement follows the builder. Worth recording because the two readings are both "source-verified" and only one of them is about the construction path the deserializer actually takes.

**Two rows show the member is NECESSARY and not sufficient, and that is what fixes the check's shape.** A `DateRangeType` naming `dd.MM.yyyy` is unbuildable with its required member present, because A12 allowlists the format-and-separator PAIR and the default separator is `/`; a `DateFragmentType` asking for a 1900 check on `dd.MM.` draws `MVK_ADDITIONAL_CHECK_INVALID`, the check needing a year the format has not. Each has a discriminator that ADMITS with the identical member set, so neither row is read as the member failing. The checker therefore reports ABSENCE and stays silent on presence, and the gap is re-aimed at exactly that remainder rather than deleted.

**Two predictions were wrong and both were about a SECOND gate past the one under test.** That is the recurring shape when a required-member reading is turned into a sufficiency claim: the reading is right about what it names and silent about what else stands between a bound object and a built model.

### Two syntax failures in one text — the gap whose own deferral had gone stale (2026-09-13)

**The gap said the fix needed "the grammar", and the grammar had arrived two slices earlier.** It described the shapes where an unpaired quote is reported as a lexical failure while A12 answers `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, and deferred them until a parser existed. `ConditionShape.kt` had been that parser since the shape slice; nothing re-read the deferral. This is the third gap this session whose text named measured shapes and was therefore a to-do rather than a limit — worth recording as a pattern, because a deferral's premise is exactly the sentence nobody re-derives.

**What the old note had was two shapes; what it needed was a rule.** "A string literal runs on and swallows a delimiter" and "a parse error sits earlier in the text" are two descriptions of one thing, and neither generalizes. The measurement is positional: A12 reports whichever failure sits EARLIEST, whatever phase owns it ([KF311](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf311)). Nine rows, as ordered PAIRS of the same two defects, because order is the only variable that moves without changing what the defects are.

**Two rival accounts had to be defeated, and each needed its own row.** "The parser outranks the lexer" fits every row where the parse error comes first, so the grid puts a stray `]` BEHIND a bad quote — and the lexer still wins. "The last error wins" fits every row whose failure is fatal to the token stream, so the grid needs the one shape with two live candidates: ANTLR skips the character it could not take and lexes on, and `FieldFilled('Tier) And` therefore raises the lexer's failure and then runs out of input. A12 names the lexer.

**The arbitration had been living in one family, which is why a second defect was invisible.** The grammar family yielded to the lexer; the completeness family never asked. So `FieldFilled('Tier) And` drew two findings with two codes — one of them a code A12 does not give for that text — and no test of either family could see it, both being right about their own half. The fix is one arbiter, `shapeFailureAhead`, consulted by all three, and a checker-side grid that asserts the WHOLE report's codes per row rather than one family's.

**The direction of risk is worth stating, because it is unusually benign.** The arbiter can only move a text between two families that both report it, so a wrong answer costs a wrong CODE and never a missed or invented finding. That is what made the increment safe to take on a mechanism claim; the mechanism itself (ANTLR's lexer is demand-driven) is recorded as a hypothesis consistent with every row, and the rows stand without it.

### D record — the stored-member denominator, enumerated (2026-09-12)

**D's acceptance asks for "complete declaration parsing … rules, operations, common preconditions, every alternative, targets, and relevant messages", and that needs a denominator rather than a judgement.** It is derived from the members A12 itself declares — `md.model.a12internal.Rule`, `Computation` and `Computation.ComputationAlternative` — cross-checked against the member names the checker's source actually reads. Both halves are mechanical, so this table can be recomputed rather than trusted.

| stored member | carrier | read by the checker? |
|---|---|---|
| `errorEntityRelPath` · `errorCondition` · `errorCode` · `errorMessage` | Rule | yes — four families |
| `severity` | Rule | **yes, since this record** — a strict enum; see below |
| `computedFieldRelPath` · `computationAlternatives` · `errorMessage` | Computation | yes |
| `commonPrecondition` | Computation | read, and measured NOT to substitute for a per-alternative precondition ([KF292](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf292)); nothing else about it is checked |
| `precondition` · `operation` | ComputationAlternative | yes — presence only; text is the syntax family's |
| `roundingMode` | Computation | **yes, since this record** — a strict enum, and half of one gate; see below |
| `toleranceRangeOp` | ComputationAlternative | **yes, since this record** — the other half of that gate |
| `variantFilter` | Rule | **yes, since this record** — read for WHICH declarations A12 never checks; see below |
| `guiPatterns` · `variantErrorMessages` | Rule | **yes, since this record** — bound only, never judged; see below |
| `errorCodesToSuppress` | Computation | **yes, since this record** — a one-argument pragma, not a set; see below |
| `variantFilter` | Computation | **yes, since this record** — the same law, measured on this carrier too |

**Seven members were unread when this denominator was drawn, and the denominator is now DRAINED** — `errorCodesToSuppress`, `roundingMode`, `toleranceRangeOp` and both carriers' `variantFilter` closed here, `severity` in the preceding record, and `guiPatterns` with `variantErrorMessages` on 2026-09-13. `variantFilter` was the load-bearing member of that group and it closed on BOTH carriers at once ([KF299](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf299)), which is why the remainder shrank by more than one row; the last two were measured together, as one topic, for the reason the rounding pair established. Every one got the same treatment — measure what A12 demands of the stored shape, then implement or disposition on a cited basis — and none was assumed harmless because the typed builder cannot author it, which is the erased-bucket error A1 exists to prevent. Two of them corrected an MVK-ledger row a builder-shaped reading had called settled, which is that rule earning its keep.

**First closed member: a rule's `severity`, and it was already measured but deliberately unimplemented.** The prose in `DeclarationSlots.kt` said the slot was "deliberately NOT checked" because an absent severity is accepted and every other spelling is a reader refusal rather than a verdict — both true, and the conclusion did not follow. The checker OWNS the reader-refusal tier (`CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` covers it, and `errorCode: null` was reported that way in the same function), so the effect was that a model carrying `severity: "Error"` — unusable, never loadable — drew a completely clean report. The slot now reports that tier and still never reports `MVK_SEVERITY_MISSING`, which fires for no measured shape at all.
